i am very pleased with the text of the building code negotiated by the rapporteurs with the helpful commissioners tajani and barnier for today's second reading. this should simplify the market for construction products. it will unite enthusiasm, simplify documentation and eliminate various unnecessary bureaucratic practices. i particularly welcome the fact that the view has finally prevailed that building construction is the provision of building services rather than the marketing of products and that building activities also include non-series-produced components incorporated into buildings, if any. companies in the czech republic will also really appreciate this small reform, because the old directive increased their costs without affecting the quality. my imco committee came together and unanimously adopted the text, including the simplification for micro-enterprises. and i believe that tomorrow the whole parliament will do so. this will be further evidence of the improvement of the legal environment in the european union in a direction which supports the single market.
it has been 19 months since canada suddenly and unilaterally introduced a visa requirement for citizens of the czech republic, which as a member of the union is not allowed to introduce a reciprocal visa for all canadians. president barroso was quick to say that this was an eu problem, not a bilateral one, but unfortunately commissioner malmström is not addressing this as a priority, but is passively monitoring the bilateral working discussions. the council replied to my question that it was not discussing the union's reciprocity, since it had not yet received any proposals from the commissioner. czech citizens are thus de facto second-class european citizens in terms of their rights, and they are surprised that canadians continue to travel to the czech republic without visas. i would point out that the commission's fickleness also increases the risk that the czech parliament will postpone ratification of the treaties between the union and canada. it has no choice but to defend the rights of its citizens when the eu fails to put pressure on canada. that is why i join you in asking the commissioner to deal with canada immediately and also to stand up for the romanians and the bulgarians. or are the common visa policy and the principle of solidarity no longer european values? is the commissioner aware of what's at stake today?
the sustained increase in the number and scale of humanitarian disasters in third countries, which is also a consequence of climate change, requires quantitative as well as qualitative improvements in humanitarian aid. i therefore fully support the measures mentioned in the report, including the transformation of member states' civil protection to also serve as actionable coordinated assistance of the european union. however, i also consider it urgent to improve the safety of humanitarian workers. i think the commission should also allocate capacity to raise awareness, especially in haiti, so that affected people understand the causes and consequences of their disaster and help protect the safety of humanitarian workers, and not the other way around. otherwise, it is necessary and not undesirable to use the protection of military forces.
it is entirely appropriate to underline the importance of food security for the eu population, which is a fundamental human right. the european union must therefore strive to ensure that healthy and nutritious food is economically and physically available to all eu citizens, enabling them to lead active and healthy lives. strict attention should be paid to the highest standards of food and agricultural production, food safety and quality, and environmental sustainability. however, in this context, attention should also be drawn to the strict requirement of the same quality and safety standards for food imported from third countries in order to protect the competitiveness of european farmers. i would also like to support measures to motivate farmers to become more energy efficient, as energy costs are a key factor in the rate of profitability of agricultural activity. as the most important of all the reports, i see the strong call on the commission to ensure, in the public interest, that the public has access to information on the results of food security controls in cooperation with the member states, in particular by rapidly sharing this information between member states. we must not repeat the same failure as the sale of meat containing traces of dioxin, as is the case in germany now, when other member states were insufficiently and too late informed of the situation.
in writing. we consider food security to be a basic human right. already, 900 million people in the world suffer from chronic hunger because of poverty. by 2050, the world's population is expected to exceed 9 billion people, and global food production will need to increase by as much as 70 percent. agriculture is faced with increasing production on the one hand, and high energy prices, dwindling uncontaminated land and the effects of climate change on the other. we have to deal with the instability and price fluctuations caused by speculation in commodity markets and the depletion of food supplies. the future security of basic food supplies requires a strong common agricultural policy. the cap should improve food security by increasing productivity and supporting environmentally sustainable food production as well as traditional farming, smallholder farming, organic farming and local and regional food distribution. these farming models contribute to food security by using proven methods and practices from across the eu. agriculture is the eu's most strategic policy, and its success will help ensure the survival of europe's population in times of food crisis. in order to enable the common agricultural policy to combat the effects of climate change, to keep food prices at an acceptable level and to increase the supply of safe staple foods, it is essential that its budget be kept at a minimum current level.
in writing. if agriculture is understood as a strategic sector, then the eu's self-sufficiency at least at the level of basic food and feed production is a necessary condition for ensuring food security. the eu's dependence on agricultural imports causes food prices to rise and harms direct producers in the supply chain. according to the food and agriculture organisation of the united nations (fao), the demand for food and feed could increase by 70% by 2050. all available forms of agriculture will therefore have to be used and financial investment in science and research in this area will have to be substantially encouraged. the effective use of traditional agricultural varieties characteristic of certain regions also requires greater support and public interest. for example, the environmental impact assessment of food production and transport distances is easy for consumers to understand. the current waste of up to 50% along the production chain or the disproportionate growth of freight transport are not factors compatible with ensuring food security. it is impossible to pretend to be concerned about food security or environmental protection while tolerating dependence on imports of products grown on another continent and imported thousands of kilometres away. such practices create a huge ecological footprint and at the same time force developing countries to import almost all the food they need, making them dependent, destroying their economies, impoverishing their populations, and destroying their environments.
for the group gue/ngl the greatest figure in serbian history, saint sava, said that serbs are both people of the east and people of the west. and the problem is that people in the west don't trust them because they think they're people from the east, and people from the east because they think they're people from the west. in fact, serbia is the bridge between east and west europe, it is a key country for the unity of europe and after all, the novel of the great serbian writer dobrica ćosiće "time of death" (time of death) shows that where serbia is actually concerned, the key game is the unity or fragmentation of europe. i think that times should change in that sense, to realize the vital importance of serbia for the unity of europe. it's a key economy in the balkans, and without the balkans, europe simply won't be united. i would like to emphasise that we have common tasks that can be solved fairly quickly, such as the refugee problems in serbia - serbia has about 750 000 refugees on its territory - environmental problems, some of which are inherited from the 1990s, such as the opening of the danube shipping route, for example, we have an intergroup in the european parliament dedicated to the danube, and we could also focus on helping serbia with a bold judicial reform and the fight against organised crime.
i welcome and support all steps towards serbia's rapid integration into the eu, as this is what the majority of serbs want. however, i would also point out that a large part of the shortcomings that are blamed on serbia in the ep's draft resolution can be attributed to the previous long-term destructive involvement of some eu member states in the balkans and the direct aggression of nato against serbia. serbia should have an easier path to the eu, as membership is not a reward for the country but a chance for the eu to contribute to solving many of the problems that it has directly or indirectly caused. i would also like to stress the need for a very cautious and balanced approach to the situation of national minorities in serbia. the ep resolution acknowledges the 'further aspirations of the albanian minority' in serbia and calls for respect for its 'right to provincial autonomy'. if minority problems are solved by creating collective legislation granting them special rights, this solution will ultimately be disastrous for the state concerned, and not only for serbia. the only way to ensure the rights of minorities is to vigorously promote and protect individual human rights.
in writing. in terms of gdp generation, construction represents 10% of the economic output of the european union. the construction market still presents a number of obstacles which prevent the full development of the single market. the aim of the proposed regulation is to amend the existing directive in order to simplify the conditions for placing construction products on the market. it is mainly a market surveillance area and a common framework for the marketing of construction products. the aim of the amendment of the directive is to help the construction industry to remove trade barriers in times of economic downturn, thereby preserving jobs and maintaining a number of construction companies that would otherwise not be able to withstand the economic downturn. in its first reading position, the council omitted parliament's amendments aimed at making it compulsory to indicate dangerous substances contained in construction products (e.g. asbestos). i consider this to be very dangerous and therefore also support the report presented, as it re-introduces the obligation for construction companies to indicate the hazardous substances contained in construction products. the health of the citizens must be protected. last but not least, i appreciate the efforts made by member states to ensure the independence of the representatives on the standing committee on construction, which assesses and verifies the durability of construction products. the report also includes amendments to improve safety and health protection not only for construction workers but also for the users of the buildings themselves. it is not only for the above reasons that i have decided to support the report.
in writing. the draft recommendation on the establishment of harmonised conditions for the placing of construction products on the market calls for the highest possible level of protection for workers and persons using construction sites. i agree with the rapporteur that it is essential to be able to trace possible dangerous substances in construction products and i support the amendments on the need to indicate dangerous substances on construction products. i do not understand the council's position that this was omitted entirely from the original proposal. in order to improve the information on new and existing construction products, so-called contact points should be established, which must be impartial, i.e. personally and financially independent of the bodies involved in the ce marking process. the council has proposed too many exceptions to the requirement to bear the ce marking; these exceptions must be revised and reduced as much as possible, otherwise the whole system of ce marking would lose its relevance. i support the proposed regulation as amended and will vote in favour of its adoption.
in writing. - i support this new approach, where the aim of the legislation is not to define the safety of products, but to create a transparent environment in which reliable information on the properties of products is provided. i also appreciate the fact that the european parliament has decided to give preference to the electronic form of documents, namely the declaration of performance attached to products placed on the market. however, i have some doubts about the legislative systematicity of amendment no 45 concerning article 17. while i support the proposed principle of fair and balanced representation of different types of stakeholders in the process of creating harmonised standards, i believe that these issues should be addressed in a comprehensive way in the revision of the european standardisation system, rather than in an unsystematic way in individual legal acts. this course of action, in my view, will lead to a lack of clarity in the legal order.
in writing. the report presented is a very timely response to the many natural disasters we have experienced in recent years. experience of being able to provide effective assistance after previous disasters, e.g. in australia, haiti, russia, shows that it is essential for the ec to have fully clear procedures and means for providing effective humanitarian assistance. so far, these disasters have been avoided as far as the european continent is concerned, but this does not mean that an effective set of structures and means should not be in place. experience gained in recent years has shown that it is not possible to provide transport to the affected areas without the technical and organisational support of the military. ngos don't have those options. it is evident that very often the inhabitants of the affected areas are cut off from the possibility of transport by land and the capacity of civil aviation is limited. if the use of military force is justified and acceptable in any case, it is precisely in the case of humanitarian or natural disasters.
i would like to begin by thanking both rapporteurs, mrs grossetête and mr bowis. it is exactly 2 years, 6 months and 16 days since the european commission presented its long-awaited proposal for a directive on patients' rights in cross-border care, but its history goes back much further than the original proposal for a directive on services. today, it is confirmed that it was a big mistake to exclude healthcare services from the services directive. it must be remembered that the directive in question deals only with patients' rights and that the question of the free movement of health services in the european union remains unresolved. while health systems in the member states are based on the same general values, the actual organisation of healthcare provision differs widely from one member state to another. the text we are voting on tomorrow, which i support, is a great compromise between the rights of citizens, patients and the concerns of the governments of the member states. despite many concessions and opposition, the proposal is a small but important step forward. the proposed directive clarifies, albeit not entirely, patients' rights, ensures their access to information, fully confirms the member states' governments' sole responsibility for the organisation and financing of healthcare, for the quality, safety and availability of care in their territory, in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity, and gives them the possibility to lay down rules under which citizens may have to seek consent before receiving care abroad. i hope, ladies and gentlemen, that we will continue to work on the directive in this parliament for years to come and improve patients' rights in the european union.
those of you who have been in the european parliament for a long time will remember how this standard came into being. it's a funny story. at the beginning was mrs gebhardt's report, which was to allow the free movement of services across the european union. we've taken a section of it and decided that health care deserves a special standard. when it was finally presented by the commission, the free movement of health services gradually fell out of it, leaving us with the part that describes the right of patients to move freely within the european union in order to seek and receive health care. i want to emphasise that sometimes our europeanism is more about symbols and flags and very little about the fulfilment of the actual freedoms on which the union should be based. the standard was accompanied by numerous concerns of individual member states about the over-drafting and liquidation of national budgets. in closing, let me say that this is not exactly what we wanted, but it is the right step and certainly a step in the right direction. i want to say hello to the news crew and thank them for their work.
i am very relieved that parliament has finally reached a political agreement on the right of patients to cross-border healthcare services under conditions that have been upheld for years only by the european court of justice on appeal. i am pleased that today's compromise formulates the terms of the payment as some of us had already advocated in the services directive seven years ago. i would like to thank mrs grossetête and mr bowis, whom i congratulate, from the bottom of my heart for having achieved this acceptable agreement and for having drawn up the important safeguards for the enforceability of this right. i believe that the right to mobility will make rare disease treatment accessible to all europeans and ultimately lead to shorter waiting times for operations across the union. it also fulfilled one of my lifelong personal goals in the field of health. let me remind you that there is still a lot of work to be done, such as completing online health services, healthcare workers' mobility and other objectives.
the compromise draft directive, after two years of negotiations, aims in particular to ensure legal certainty for patients in the context of the provision of cross-border healthcare. that's certainly a positive result. however, the complexity of the negotiations has highlighted not only the differences in health systems across member states, but also the different economic conditions under which healthcare is provided to european citizens. the fact that this is a very sensitive problem is also evidenced by the recent massive statements of dissatisfied doctors in czech hospitals. in this context, certain risks or uncertainties arise with regard to the impact of the directive, for example in terms of medical tourism for quality but cheaper healthcare. only time will tell whether this is a marginal problem and whether the positive effects of the directive will prevail.
i'm also going to talk about alzheimer's disease. we know that this is a report which has no legislative impact of its own and is not too controversial. i hope he doesn't split this plenum. but we know that this is a very serious disability with very serious economic implications and therefore a very serious social problem. it is closely linked to the ageing of the european population, and its increase is beginning to resemble a neurological epidemic in europe. what can we do about this? we can emphasise prevention that saves money and improves health, we can improve the dietary habits of the european population, we can consolidate our scientific base in europe and coordinate the work of individual member states, we can support civil society and family associations and we can help to create specialised centres in europe.
in writing. dementia and alzheimer's disease in particular are not only a major medical problem but also an extremely sensitive ethical and social problem, the importance of which will continue to increase in the context of the ageing of the european population. the document very rightly focuses on the various aspects of this disease, in particular the promotion of early diagnosis, research and improvement of patient care itself. however, i consider the support for families of people with dementia to be equally important, and the emphasis on non-discriminatory access to quality health and social services to be essential. in this context, i see a crucial role for local and regional authorities and non-profit organisations, which can respond quickly to the growing need for these services. the european alzheimer's and other dementias initiative is also very important for starting a serious and open public discussion about this disease in the broadest sense. that is why i welcome and support this initiative.
since i myself was unable to attend the certainly interesting debate on this subject on monday because of my children, i would like at least now to express my full support, which i have expressed by voting, for this resolution. i strongly hope that the follow-up to this resolution will lead to a reduction in the bureaucracy involved in international adoption of children, but also to better monitoring of their subsequent fate and to help in the fight against child trafficking.
in writing. - i voted for the grossetête report because, after many years, it removes the uncertainty about reimbursement and the resulting access to crossborder healthcare. while the benefits of cross-border healthcare have so far been mainly enjoyed by better informed or more mobile patients, the possibility is now open to everyone. i agree to make the reimbursement of costs conditional on prior authorisation in justified and clearly defined cases where cross-border healthcare involves a patient's stay in hospital for at least one night, requires highly specialised and expensive medical equipment or poses a particular risk to the patient or the public. in these cases, i consider prior authorisation to be one of the tools to prevent excessive medical tourism. the growing health tourism is not a good sign for many european health systems. however, if a patient is exposed to unreasonably long waiting times in his home country, it is not surprising that he would want to travel to a place where he could receive care more quickly and, moreover, perhaps in a higher quality. however, in countries where foreigners will travel more for medical procedures and operations, there is a danger that domestic patients will take their turn after foreign demand is satisfied.
- there is a general lack of information and a priori a negative attitude towards access to healthcare in crossborder settings. within the eu freedoms, it should in principle be possible for every citizen to benefit from healthcare services in another member state if he or she receives better or faster treatment there, or if he or she pays for it himself or herself. the provision of healthcare in another member state is therefore a priority issue. this debate has been going on for years, but we have made no significant progress. in another member state, you will only receive treatment in an emergency situation. it is therefore not possible to plan healthcare or medical treatment in another member state. the health insurance of the country in question is always required, but only eu citizens with permanent residence in that country can obtain it. this is nonsense, because no citizen can have health insurance in two or more eu countries, since he has only one main permanent address, in the country where he lives. we are legally preventing eu citizens from investing more in their health and treatment if they want to. the recommendation is at least a modest step forward, and i will therefore vote in favour of its adoption.
in writing. - the document translated follows on from the debate held by the ep in the previous parliamentary term. the commission's proposal itself contained certain shortcomings and risks that could have a negative impact on the consumers of care, i.e. citizens. i welcome the compromise reached in amendment no 107, in particular because it increases the guarantees for patients in the provision of cross-border care and highlights the positive trend towards increasing patient awareness. finally, i welcome the fact that the amendment emphasises the responsibility of the member states to provide safe, high-quality, effective and accessible care on their territory. i also consider it important to define the conditions under which a member state may refuse to grant prior authorisation. the amendment also contains positive steps in interoperability and promotes cooperation in both prevention and diagnostics.

the low rate of project implementation, the absence of action plans and evaluation reports, the absence of high-level meetings, the risk of duplication with the eastern partnership and many other problems suggest that the decision to give the lead to the member states in implementing the black sea strategy was inappropriate. it appears that the european commission should play a more prominent role as the secretariat and facilitator of this black sea strategy. i also believe that this strategy, like other macro-regional strategies, should remain budgetary, institutional and legislative neutral, i.e. no additional resources should be spent, no new institutions should be set up and no new legislation should be prepared. there is no need for new operational programmes and specific budget lines, but there is a need to focus on the implementation of specific projects in which the states concerned will have a genuine interest.
in writing. the european union strategy for the black sea region is reminiscent of yetti. everyone's talking about her, but no one's seen her. this conviction is reinforced by several contradictions which i see at the very beginning of the resolution. first, it is the demarcation of the black sea area. it is somewhat curious that azerbaijan, as a country on the caspian sea, is included, but macedonia is not, although its two closest neighbours, bulgaria and greece, are included in the black sea region by the ec's definition. i never knew greece stretched to the black sea. the 13 areas of cooperation, which envisage greater eu involvement in the region, are indeed a very broad agenda. the commission has not yet seemed to appreciate the importance of this area. finally, if in the spring of this year eur 1.5 million from the pilot programme for environment and regional development was diverted to a banana project in the acp countries, i have to ask on what basis the commission reached this decision. it's curious that the author "ignored" south stream, but he mentions the controversial and unfilled nabucco project. i would like to stress that the possibility of engaging in partnerships with the various regions of the european union will also be very important for the further development of the region beyond the area mentioned. the conclusions of the last visa waiver negotiations with ukraine are missing.
in writing. - we should not simply close the door and make it difficult to have a better dialogue. the imposition of severe sanctions could create a barrier and further isolate the country. in their final outcome, they would most likely have an impact beyond the intended target. eu-belarus communication should be intensively pursued, using all diplomatic means, and regularly reviewed. as regards effectiveness, it may be necessary to set up a different type of dialogue, conducted in a different form and at different levels, including a balanced rather than a unilateral assessment. however, the basis should be a careful evaluation of the experience of eu-belarus exchanges to date and, on that basis, their up-to-date incorporation into a strategic plan that would clearly reflect the eu's foreign policy towards belarus. in the long term, there could be an improvement in relations and the launching of new cooperation projects that would contribute to the further development of the country and preserve its sovereignty. the eastern partnership programme can play a significant role in this.
in writing. - i voted for the ungureanu report because i believe that the eu urgently needs a coherent and visible strategy for the black sea region. this should be closely linked to the european neighbourhood policy, to which the czech eu presidency made a significant contribution in the first half of 2009. the security of energy supply to the eu and the related support for energy projects of eu interest aiming at diversifying gas supplies to the eu should be a key priority of the eu black sea strategy. the establishment of a euronest parliamentary assembly is also important and urgent for the security of energy supply to the eu. the projects to be supported by the black sea strategy include the nabucco project, the south stream project, the project to transport liquefied natural gas (lng) to europe and the development of lng terminals in black sea ports, and the konstanca-terst pan-european pipeline project. the commission should not hesitate to conclude agreements with potential nabucco supplier countries by the end of 2011. the energy infrastructure package, which the commission intends to present in the near future, should place a strong emphasis on the aforementioned energy projects in the black sea region. lastly, the considerable potential of renewable energy sources in the black sea region, which can make a significant contribution to securing the energy future of the eu and the world, should be highlighted.

energy security is an area where the european union should be involved and where it should proceed in the most uniform and coordinated way possible, unlike many other european initiatives, and that is why we, as the group of european conservatives and reformists, support the creation of a common energy market, as this strengthens the position of the member states vis-à-vis the main suppliers and also prevents the abuse of the dominant position of some suppliers of strategic raw materials for foreign political games. but we also say that even in energy integration, national specificities, i.e. national energy mix, including the use of nuclear energy, must be respected. on the economy, let me say this: my group is made up mainly of parties from those countries which are not members of the euro area, which do not use the euro. but that doesn't mean that we want a weak and fragile and unstable eurozone or that we want the euro to collapse. on the other hand, i am clear that the recovery of the european union's economy and financial stability must not lead to further transnational trends, must not lead to fiscal federalisation, must not become a pretext for further devolution of powers from the national to the european level. we simply refuse! a few notes on what's happening in north africa, tunisia and egypt. this is very similar to what happened in 1989 in central and eastern europe. the democratization trends in these states are certainly welcome and should be supported. we want the european union's immediate neighbourhood to be stable and democratic. but at the same time, let us be careful that these legitimate aspirations and appeals of the people of these countries do not slip into the other direction and lead to undemocratic, authoritarian, religious dictatorships. the european union should act to prevent this development.
i expect the energy summit to be a comprehensive, but also sensible and responsible approach to the interests of citizens. according to the latest eurostat surveys, energy price stability is one of the main priorities for a large proportion of the union population. it is no coincidence that the czech republic is the leading country in this statistic. in our country, irresponsible political and economic lobbying has pushed for inadequate financial support for photovoltaic power plants. the rampant entry of entrepreneurs, including foreigners, and their quest for fabulous profits is turning the public against renewable energy as such, as people face unnecessarily high electricity price increases. granted, it is not easy to provide sufficient energy at affordable prices, but we can at least get close to that goal. the way forward, i believe, is to seek a balanced energy mix from renewables to nuclear energy, while breaking down the extremes on both sides. only then will we have the confidence of the citizens of the union, who are the reason we are here.
mr president, i do not share the opinion that an ongoing uprising in egypt is dominantly driven by forces longing for democracy of a european style. i see the current riots and chaos as a revolution for change that is motivated by striving for a better life. but the crucial question for us europeans is the threat that the dramatic situation could be abused by radical islamists. if there were such a dramatic political change, europe, as well as america, would lose a moderate arabic ally and the egypt peace treaty would be endangered. egypt can quickly and easily turn into a regime that is hostile to europe, israel and america and become close to hamas. the position of the eu should stay firmly on the side of those who are striving for freedom and who relentlessly support free elections.
in writing. in 1995, the european union set itself the ambitious goal of creating an area of peace, stability and prosperity in the mediterranean. the mediterranean countries received funds on condition that they undertook the necessary economic and political reforms. but none of that happened. in most cases, the european union's efforts to help its neighbours to the south have been driven by fear of islamic radicalism coming to power and threatening the security stability of the region. history shows that supporting authoritarian regimes does not pay off. it has not paid off for the united states in south and central america, and it is not paying off for the european union. some critics rightly speak of our hypocrisy. we talk about human rights, the need for social dialogue, economic development, and we have for decades turned a blind eye to the fact that in tunisia and other countries in north africa, cruel and undemocratic regimes rule. it remains to be seen that this is a major failure of those who have promoted such a policy. the embarrassing call by eu officials for mubarak to organize democratic elections is just the sad outcome of this failed policy.
the recommendation for the second reading establishing the development cooperation instrument notes a high degree of agreement on the measures and accepts in principle all the amendments from the first reading. i personally consider it a priority to address the systemic issues of multiannual development aid financing and agree with the rapporteur that the method of financing needs to be finalised in 2012 and 2013. the recommendation notes that the regulation radically reduces the reserves under the ceiling of heading 4 of the mff for the period 2007-13 and leaves insufficient room for manoeuvre to address and respond to possible future crises. the recommendation also addresses the legislative issues of the council's procedural procedure, which parliament criticises as it should have the same degree of control as the council in the area of strategic decisions on where and how development aid is spent. i therefore support the inclusion in the development cooperation instrument regulation, in accordance with the substance and the spirit of the treaty of lisbon, of a procedure for delegated acts which also covers the programming of the other external financial instruments currently under review (the instrument for stability, the instrument for human rights and democracy and the instrument for cooperation with industrialized countries).
in writing. one of the main objectives of the union's development policy is the reduction and permanent eradication of poverty, to which the proposed banana accompanying measures (bam) of eur 190 million for the period 2010-2013 approved by the commission on 17 march 2010 would seem to contribute. it is also true that in the past the relevant schemes in this area were drawn up from a commercial rather than a developmental perspective, and it is this perspective that should be reflected in the new proposal. the idea of supporting bam to improve the living standards of the population living in acp banana-supplying countries affected by the development of customs duties on banana imports into the eu also seems to be a good one. however, the commission proposal shows certain shortcomings, such as the absence of an impact analysis of the proposed measures, the question of the timing of the drawdown, the questionable large-scale use of union reserves and the persistent problem of under-financing of heading 4, which the commission rightly pointed out in its evaluation report on the functioning of the interinstitutional agreement. overall, i consider that the report presented by mr charles goerence presents a precise analysis of the issue from both a trade and development perspective, reflects the comments approved by the committee on budgets on 13 september 2010, and also contains relevant recommendations on bam, and therefore i recommend that it be approved as proposed.
there is no doubt that this directive is moving in the right direction, as it seeks to change the quantitative collection objectives, to set rules for the cross-border transport of electrical and electronic equipment and to harmonise administrative steps towards obliged entities in order to reduce red tape. it is precisely because of the excessive bureaucracy that i reject the definition of a producer at european union level, also in view of the possible enforceability of the obligations laid down by the member states. in this case, we should follow the motto that if something works, there's no reason to change it in any way. as regards the scope of the directive, i personally prefer a closed scope, as i am concerned that opening the scope would include practically non-recyclable equipment or would include equipment with a long service life and thus jeopardise the achievement of the agreed collection targets. in this case, i believe that when we set ourselves goals, we should not only be ambitious, but above all realistic and not burden more towns and cities.
in writing. the collection of electrical and electronic waste is a very topical issue nowadays. it is therefore more than necessary that the relevant area be covered by the updated directive. i believe that some of the amendments which will be voted on in the first reading very unfortunately combine the commission's proposals which facilitate the collection, sorting and subsequent recycling of waste with others. these, in turn, bring the whole issue into if not chaos, then at least less light. unfortunately, at the stage of the final vote, there is not much we can do about it. it is interesting to note that the majority of the political groups agree on a number of fairly good amendments. if this agreement is supported in the final vote, the resulting form of the directive could support a significant increase in the collection of weee and its better recyclability. one of the key points is article 14 (1), which if amendment 91 or the somewhat less clear wording of amendment 103 is adopted, can promote the recycling and take-back of electrical and electronic equipment. it is important that the outcome of all this work is a directive that clearly defines the obligations of the specific actors and at the same time allows for the most efficient recycling process. in this respect, the odd classification of lighting equipment into several categories or too many exceptions for whole categories of large installations is undesirable.
in writing. waste electrical and electronic equipment is the fastest growing source of waste. they present a realistically higher risk to the environment and public health than spent nuclear fuel. a revision of the directive was necessary. a large amount of e-waste is either not treated in accordance with its wording and ends up in landfills, or is illegally exported outside the eu. it is alarming that we have no idea what happens to more than half of europe's electrical and electronic waste. this situation is unsustainable because if collection and recycling targets are not set and respected, the current production of 4 kg of waste per person per year could rise to 24 kg by 2020. recycling e-waste can put more gold back into the production process than is mined in the eu, and the same is true for other rare earths. i therefore strongly welcome the creation of new stable jobs and, above all, the reduction of the eu's dependence on imports of rare earths. i have a very negative view of the solar panel exemption. in the context of the rohs exemption for cadmium telluride panels, this represents a further increase in the risk of highly toxic cadmium pollution, which is being combated in other eu legislative measures. behind this inconsistency, which is not worthy of the eu or the european parliament and consequently does not bring any good to european industry either, are lobbying interests.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the banana trade agreement, which definitively ends the 16 year trade dispute over banana imports between the eu and the us. the seriousness of the problem is documented by the judgments of the european court of justice, which has ruled against the discriminatory regime for importing bananas into the eu. i appreciate that the import of bananas will finally be equalised and that the unjustified preferential treatment of former european colonies, which distorts the world banana trade, will end. but even after the leveling of trade conditions, europe continues to favour farmers in africa, the caribbean and the pacific who are still favoured. as a compensatory measure, it has provided them with eur 200 million in aid to help them prepare for a tougher competition. but i fear that sooner or later we will have to accept the fact that african bananas, because of their lower quality and higher production costs, will simply not survive competition from latin american bananas. i also hope that the successful conclusion of the negotiations on bananas will lead to a number of tariff reductions for other tropical fruits, which should bring down their prices and thus benefit all eu citizens.
in writing. in view of the proposals made, the gue/ngl's views on the submitted material concerning the adjustment of the rules for possible access of this agricultural commodity to eu markets, the international trade (import/export) rules concerned, i state my position, which is in line with the czech republic's position on the matter. the czech republic supports the change of import regimes with a reduction of the customs burden for imports from third countries for this agricultural commodity, welcomes the geneva agreement reached governing trade in this commodity. agricultural production itself and international trade in this commodity, especially access to the eu market, is a key sector for many of the countries concerned. this should always be taken into account when shaping the eu's common agricultural policy. in accordance with the negotiated conditions, a certain degree of stability could therefore be established in the sector, as is apparent from the texts. however, the basis should remain the impact assessment on the countries concerned, combined with an estimate of possible developments over a longer time horizon. despite the priority of adjusting international trade rules, the relationship between this important agricultural commodity and the final consumers cannot be overlooked. it is important to maintain the appropriate nutritional value of this food and its initial production stage using ecological elements. the corresponding quality should not be ultimately neglected but rather given priority over quantity.
in writing. - i voted for the report on waste electrical and electronic equipment, which deals with a very topical issue. on the one hand, improper handling of these facilities would inevitably have environmental and health consequences. on the other hand, we are faced with the problem of the loss of raw materials, which is one of the key issues in the globalised economy. according to available data, 85% of e-waste is currently recycled, but only 33% of this is officially registered and managed in accordance with the provisions of the eu directive. i cannot fully agree with the envi opinion that the costs of collection, treatment, recovery and environmentally sound disposal of waste from electronic equipment should be covered under the 'polluter pays' principle. on the contrary, i am of the opinion that producers, retailers and consumers should not pay for the collection and transport of waste from the door to the collection point, but that this responsibility should, as it is now, primarily lie with the municipalities. at the same time, i see no problem in allowing manufacturers to show the cost of collection and recycling of the product when selling a new product to the consumer. i think it is good that consumers are informed about these costs and are thus led to behave responsibly in an environmentally friendly way.
in writing. i supported the resolution on the fight against tuberculosis for a number of reasons. first of all, i would like to stress that the humanitarian aspect of this disease must be considered, because it is a disease that can be prevented by vaccination alone, and therefore the deaths of more than 2 million people a year are not only extremely high, but above all unnecessary. i fully agree that tb is a striking example of inequality between nations, since it has been almost eradicated in industrialized countries. the tvbi foundation serves people with tuberculosis around the world, especially in the least developed countries. personally, i see this approach to fighting tb as an expression of solidarity among european citizens and an effort to reduce the number of deaths that are completely unnecessary, and that are the result of a lack of funding for vaccination in less developed countries.
mr. chairman, i'm glad you mentioned the thousands of changes that have been made by individual states. it is more than a year since the services directive was due to be transposed into the legal systems of all the member states of the union. unfortunately, it's obvious that the thousand changes aren't enough. some states, in their efforts to apply protectionist measures in their domestic economies, have not considered the implementation of this directive as their priority. paradoxically, the consistent implementation of the services directive and the removal of barriers to cross-border provision of services could act as a catalyst for economic growth throughout the union, allowing a faster recovery from the economic crisis. in the context of the implementation of the directive, i am very concerned that the single points of contact, which were supposed to make life easier for entrepreneurs by providing them with information on doing business both in the member state concerned and in other member states, are currently only operating in fourteen member states. commissioner, knowing you, i trust that you will continue with your colleagues in your efforts to implement the single market act and that you will consistently require member states to transpose the services directive into law, including its actual implementation. the successful implementation of this directive is a prerequisite for the creation of the single internal market. you'll have the support of our imco committee.
mr president, the single market for services in europe is still not living up to its potential. the services directive, which was intended to remove barriers to the provision of services across europe, was to bring significant improvements. unfortunately, that's not the case yet. that is why i welcome the report by mrs gebhardt. i share the view that member states need to be effectively encouraged to speed up the transposition of the directive into their national law and to complete it in full. but i think it's equally important that small and medium-sized businesses and entrepreneurs know that there are places that can help them with their service businesses. i therefore consider the visibility of the single points of contact to be a key aspect. for this reason, i have focused my amendments on the need for an intensive information campaign to support this project. it should be addressed to all stakeholders, including public authorities. the european union may have the best intentions, it may have the best legislation and supporting measures, and it may even have all the countries implementing them as best they can, but if businesses and traders don't know about it, the result will always be embarrassing. the single market act also seeks to make europe fit not only for citizens and consumers but also for businesses and traders. so let's listen to them, but above all, let's give them enough information to be able to access it.
mr president, there is no doubt that the services directive was to be instrumental in opening the way to the development of services in the member states, and in particular to the cross-border use of a much wider range of possibilities for both businesses and consumers. the problem is that protectionism still persists in many member states. today, when we are debating the implementation of this directive after several years, i wish to express my regret that it took us five years to finally bring the original bolkenstein directive into force here in parliament, after it had been revised and supplemented in a number of respects. and now we have no clear evidence that it has done what it was supposed to do, because the member states still lack an understanding of the philosophy and meaning of the internal market. i am very sorry that five countries do not have functioning single points of contact, but more importantly, there is no good interoperability between the other points of contact in the member states. as my speakers have already said, the problem is that small businesses in particular are not yet sufficiently informed about what the contact points can offer them, and in some countries these contact points do not even exist. what i find useful is that the member states actually evaluate themselves amongst themselves. hopefully, it'll have some kind of a ripple effect in the future. i want to thank the rapporteur and also the shadow rapporteur handzlik for basically truthfully describing the situation as it is here. i would like to draw attention to article 36, where this report recalls that member states are obliged to carry out risk assessments and to ensure that undertakings do not encounter unnecessary obstacles when carrying out the necessary procedures by electronic means.
mr. chairman, in his annual state of the union address, president obama called for a stronger america in innovation. he called the innovation "the sputnik of our generation". i am pleased that with the innovation union programme, the european union is sending a clear signal of the importance of innovation in maintaining europe's economic position in global competition. i see the creation of a european union unitary patent as one of the key instruments for the success of the 'innovation union' project and for keeping ahead of the innovation tigers such as china and brazil. the unitary patent will help to increase the competitiveness of european businesses considerably. i am therefore very sorry that the emergence of such a key tool for the development of innovation in the european union is being held back by some countries by secondary issues such as the protection of language. i therefore support the proposal for enhanced cooperation even at the cost of the non-participation of some member states. please don't let us miss the "sputnik of our generation".
mr president, i have long supported the single european patent because it will make investments in innovation more attractive, enable businesses to better protect their know-how, accelerate the transfer of knowledge from development to practice and hopefully better protect us against the entry of counterfeiters into the internal market. and that's just the challenge. it is to the detriment of our competitiveness that after ten years the council has only reached enhanced cooperation. i welcome the fact that the czech republic has reconsidered its position and intends to join the negotiations. i also realise how sensitive the case for enhanced cooperation is when it comes to language arrangements, especially for some large countries. but i believe that all developers in the european union will benefit. it is important that patents should be as cheap as in the usa, and i believe that the council will make further simplifications. the new judicial system for european patents is important, and should clarify a number of issues surrounding software and biotechnology patents. i support the proposal for enhanced cooperation.

in writing. eu support instruments for smes must be particularly flexible and as bureaucratic as possible. banks are less and less willing to finance riskier business ventures, including start-ups or innovative products. access to finance is one of the biggest obstacles facing smes today. it is therefore necessary to strengthen the different ways of supporting microfinance for enterprises and to ensure better coordination and interlinkage between the different programmes already in place. however, their availability is also hampered by the complexity, time, financial and disproportionate administrative burden of providing relatively low amounts of funding. i also consider it important to improve general awareness of existing eu support options and instruments for smes.
the next item is the debate on– the oral question to the commission on implementation of the eu strategy for the danube region by silvia-adriana ţicău, constanze angela krehl, hannes swoboda, adrian severin, ivailo kalfin, karin kadenbach, olga sehnalová, rovana plumb, csaba sándor tabajdi, evgeni kirilov, katarína neveďalová, vasilica viorica dăncilă, daciana octavia sârbu, ioan mircea paşcu, george sabin cutaş, britta thomsen, corina creţu, claudiu ciprian tănăsescu, ioan enciu, cătălin sorin ivan, mario pirillo, kinga göncz, marc tarabella, françoise castex, victor boştinaru, inés ayala sender, bogusław liberadzki, jo leinen, michael cashman, brian simpson, saïd el khadraoui, thijs berman, eider gardiazábal rubial, ismail ertug, edit herczog, on behalf of the s&d group; michael theurer, vladko todorov panayotov, renate weber, sophia in 't veld, jan mulder, gesine meissner, jorgo chatzimarkakis, catherine bearder, viktor uspaskich, wolf klinz, nadja hirsch, cristian silviu buşoi, giommaria uggias, ramona nicole mănescu, adina-ioana vălean, hannu takkula, jürgen creutzmann, alexander alvaro, holger krahmer, on behalf of the alde group; reinhard bütikofer, michael cramer, eva lichtenberger, barbara lochbihler, heide rühle, elisabeth schroedter, isabelle durant, on behalf of the verts/ale group; peter van dalen, oldřich vlasák, on behalf of the ecr group (o-0029/2011 – b7-0013/2011)– the oral question to the commission on eu strategy for the danube region by tamás deutsch, lambert van nistelrooij, danuta maria hübner, marian-jean marinescu, iosif matula, andrey kovatchev, jános áder, zoltán bagó, kinga gál, béla glattfelder, andrás gyürk, ágnes hankiss, lívia járóka, ádám kósa, csaba őry, ildikó gáll-pelcz, györgy schöpflin, lászló surján, józsef szájer, edit bauer, alajos mészáros, csaba sógor, lászló tőkés, iuliu winkler, elisabeth jeggle, on behalf of the ppe group (o-0014/2011 – b7-0011/2011).
the author. - mr president, macro-regional strategies are first and foremost a political instrument which represents one of the possible forms of territorial cooperation. they should be a tool for the effective use of existing initiatives, programmes, financial instruments, a platform for cooperation and institutions. macro-regional strategies are also intended to provide a framework for the better functioning of the union and its internal market and a platform for business-to-business links. they should serve to streamline and better structure the approach to the challenges faced by larger european cross-border geographical entities. however, both the challenges and the tools must be clearly defined. from the point of view of our group, the group of european conservatives and reformists, it is crucial that the concept of macro-regional strategies at the level of the european union be addressed in a structured, systematic way in the context of other policies. it is therefore desirable to adopt a strategic approach to this issue, particularly as regards the number of strategies, concentration, nature of projects and governance. we support the concept of macro-regional strategies, including the danube strategy, provided that it continues to stand by the principles approved by the council (3 ne): budgetary and financial neutrality, legislative neutrality and institutional neutrality. the role and future of macro-regional strategies is linked to the debate on the future use of european funds, but we believe that the discussion on the future architecture of cohesion policy and the anticipation of potential financial allocations for individual countries in the next financial perspective is redundant in today's debate. macro-regional strategies should not affect the future architecture of cohesion policy, other than transnational cooperation, or the financial allocations to individual objectives.
mr president, the danube strategy is the second macro-regional strategy to promote cooperation in territories with common characteristics and shared challenges. we will certainly agree that the main objective should be to ensure sustainable development, jobs and prosperity in the danube. this should be done by making maximum use of existing instruments and policies. i therefore fully support the so-called principle of 'no', i.e. 'no unnecessary bureaucracy, no new institutions and no new or additional financial resources'. however, alongside these objectives, a specific question arises for the commission. how will they ensure that the strategy is properly implemented in practice under these conditions? in particular, the regional and local level, in cooperation with the non-profit sector, must play an important role in the context of the principle of subsidiarity. those levels should cooperate effectively and their activities should be monitored and evaluated by the commission. implementation should follow the commission's action plan. but is the commission also preparing a shorter and clearer version of this document, which would bring the main practical benefits of the strategy directly to citizens and local and regional public administrations? and the last question: what is the possible follow-up of the danube strategy to the new financial framework 2014-2020? is there an overlap?

the next item is the report by martin callanan, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council setting emission performance standards for new light commercial vehicles as part of the community’s integrated approach to reduce co2 emissions from light-duty vehicles (com(2009)0593 – c7-0271/2009 – 2009/0173(cod)) (a7-0287/2010).
the rapporteur of the committee on transport and tourism. - mr president, it is true that transport is one of the sectors where it is desirable to reduce emissions. but our pursuit of emission reductions should not jeopardise the competitiveness of european car manufacturers. this requirement is particularly urgent in view of the ongoing financial crisis, which is still having a serious impact on the european automotive industry. in this respect, i consider the achieved value of 147 g co2/km for the average emissions of new light commercial vehicles to be quite ambitious and i am very curious about the feasibility study that the commission is to carry out on the basis of the updated results. at the same time, i am watching with great caution the efforts of the commission and some other actors, who find the proposed target to be rather unambitious, to reopen the debate on this subject and to try to amend this regulation to set stricter targets. i believe that economic actors need to be assured that legislation will not be constantly changing. now it's time to give car manufacturers the space to work and develop new engines to deal with regulation and new targets. at the same time, i believe that recital 24 should also be noted, which envisages extending speed limitation devices to light commercial vehicles. i'm glad we're going to be addressing this issue separately and that this debate will be more substantive and less ideological.

mr president, thank you for moderating part of this debate in czech. but most of all, i'd like to thank the reporters and the editor in chief, martin callanan, for their hard work. this regulation we are discussing is a follow-up to the climate change policy as we know it from the european parliament, and there is no room for too much polemic with this policy and the whole concept. it must be remembered that these are mostly unilateral actions of the european parliament, which do not have a corresponding reflection outside the european union in the rest of the world. there is a need to consider what constitutes a sensitive incentive and what constitutes an undue burden on european businesses, and i believe that this report has succeeded in approaching sensitive incentives. in this case, i strongly disagree with what my keynote speaker, carl schlyter, said. we are not helping europe's competitiveness by tying a steel ball to the feet of european industry. so far, all the evidence is that industry in europe is indeed shrinking and moving across borders. the measures in the light commercial vehicle sector are much more complex than those in the passenger car sector and place a greater burden on small businesses, as already mentioned here. i would like to conclude by congratulating all the rapporteurs on their work and i consider the compromise reached to be excellent.
