the next item is the report by antonio cancian, on behalf of parliament’s delegation to the conciliation committee, on the joint text approved by the conciliation committee for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council concerning the rights of passengers in bus and coach transport and amending regulation (ec) no 2006/2004 (00063/2010 – c7-0015/2011 – 2008/0237(cod)) (a7-0020/2011).
for the group gue/ngl mr president, the rights of passengers in all modes of transport have become an increasingly important item of the european parliament's deliberations in recent years. i must admit that in many cases i see problems that can arise with the enforcement of passenger rights, and the coverage of which by insurance will certainly be a very interesting segment for many financial institutions. on the other hand, logically, the cost of transport will also increase on the basis of these new costs. it is not new that the council has tried to change the basic elements of the draft regulation in a fundamental way in the case of passenger rights in bus and coach transport. we know this position of the council from previous conciliation procedures. in this context, i would like to thank the vice-chair of the delegation, ms rodi kratsa-tsagaropoulou, the chair of the committee on transport and tourism, mr brian simpson, and the rapporteur, mr antonio canciano, for their well-done work. they did reach a very reasonable compromise. i consider the following to be positive elements in particular: 1. the definition of long-distance transport as transport over 250 km of the entire line, with the regulation also applying to persons who travel only part of the distance; 2. improving the position, albeit not ideal, of disabled persons and persons with reduced mobility in this type of transport; 3. compensation and assistance in the event of an accident. i would like to note here that damage to ancillary equipment such as wheelchairs is assumed to be compensated in full, which brings with it some uncertainty and will certainly require this wording to be specified for insurance purposes, e.g. by an upper limit; 4. passengers' rights in the event of cancellation or delay are fairly clearly defined. i also regard the improvement in passenger information as positive; 5. like most of my colleagues, i welcome the fact that the transitional period for the implementation of this regulation has been reduced to a maximum of four years. on the contrary, i am not at all happy about the possibility of extending this period for another four years. i believe that there will be no major problem in establishing the exercise of passenger rights on lines parallel to the long-distance section in the near future. the question of the thresholds is, of course, the absence of an inflation clause which would adjust the thresholds in line with the evolution of the euro exchange rate so that the standard thresholds remain the same in real terms. i think the deal is a good compromise. the ultimate aim is to further extend passenger rights in all modes of transport and this further step in bus and coach transport is certainly a successful outcome of the conciliation procedure. the gue/ngl group supports this result.
in the past, the european parliament, together with the council, has adopted legislation on air and rail passenger rights. it is therefore logical that we are now adopting an analogous standard for bus transport, despite the fact that passengers in europe increasingly prefer individual transport and that the level of bus and coach transport itself is rapidly improving. but our drive and enthusiasm for regulation should have clear limits. it is logical to define basic standards for cross-border travel in the european union, to protect vulnerable groups of travellers and to ensure their rights. on the other hand, it is inappropriate interference in the internal market to try to extend the rights to local associations, to try to get the highest possible compensation and to regulate everything down to the last detail. it is clear that any unnecessary obligations impose additional costs on the carriers, which are ultimately reflected in the price of the ticket. as a former mayor, for example, i was concerned about the proposal to extend the directive to urban and suburban transport. i am glad that common sense has prevailed in this case and that urban and suburban transport has been spared excessive regulation.
madam president, the european parliament is voting on an important compromise which will significantly strengthen the rights of passengers in bus and coach transport. i consider it significant that, in addition to compensating passengers for loss of or damage to their luggage, compensation for bus delays and other measures, the enhanced rights of disabled passengers and persons with reduced mobility have also been included in this compromise. in particular, i support the right of disabled people to information and assistance services, at least as they were eventually agreed in the conciliation procedure. public transport is an essential and important part of the daily lives of people with reduced mobility. it was not easy to reach a compromise, and i would like to join in congratulating the rapporteur and all the members of the negotiating team on their hard work. the adoption of this regulation will bring about a common framework of protection for all modes of transport at european level, which is certainly not an ideal but nevertheless a very significant step forward in terms of passenger rights.
in writing. the report on passenger rights in bus and coach transport aims to establish passenger protection rights applicable throughout the union, for all carriers and for different modes of transport. the council did not agree with the ep's amendments, so it was necessary to open the conciliation procedure. the main points of the agreement reached are largely the same as those originally proposed by parliament. in particular, it is important that the regulation will apply to all scheduled, national and crossborder services, with member states being able to apply exemptions for scheduled services and certain specific services for a maximum of 8 years. passengers will be entitled to compensation in the event of an accident up to a maximum of eur 220 000 per person and eur 1 200 per baggage. in addition, the carrier must provide basic assistance (food, clothing, medical care) and accommodation for up to two nights in the event of an accident. in the event of a delay of more than 120 minutes or a cancellation of a connection, the carrier must offer the passenger the choice between continuing the journey, re-routing at no additional cost to the passenger or reimbursement of the fare. if he is unable to do so, he must reimburse the fare and pay an additional 50% of the fare. the agreement also protects the rights of disabled persons and persons with reduced mobility. for these reasons, i fully support the adoption of the report.
in writing. i consider the adopted text of the regulation to be a balanced compromise ensuring passenger rights without imposing an excessive administrative burden on carriers, most of which are small and medium-sized enterprises. i consider it a success that the ep has managed to impose, against the will of the council, the application of the regulation to all scheduled, national and crossborder transport where the distance is 250 km or more, whereas the council has pushed for a minimum distance of 500 km. i welcome the fact that in the event of cancellation, delay of more than 120 minutes or overbooking, passengers will be entitled to a refund of 50% of the ticket price in addition to the right to continue or be rerouted to the final destination or reimbursement of the ticket price. as the bus passenger rights regulation follows the train passenger rights regulation, i am not pleased that, unlike bus passengers, train passengers in many eu countries have to wait to exercise their rights, especially as regards the right to a refund of between a quarter and half of the fare for delays of more than one hour. the czech ministry of transport also used the option of postponing the validity of the relevant european legislation by five years. it cited the relatively large construction activity on the domestic railway network, which is causing the delay of trains. this ultimately means the introduction of a double standard system that puts one group of passengers at a disadvantage compared to another.
in writing. − while it is understandable that the proposal is largely based on legislation on co2 emissions from passenger cars, it is also important to take into account that these sectors do not operate in the same way. light commercial vehicles have a longer development and production cycle and are used predominantly for commercial purposes and, unlike passenger cars, have fewer options for modifying their shape and weight, which would reduce emissions. the main way to achieve this in light commercial vehicles is by modifying the engine and vehicle mechanics, which is a much longer and more costly process than simply changing the body of the vehicle. the fact that diesel is used much more frequently as a fuel for light commercial vehicles also plays an important role. i have reservations about the proposal that the penalties for manufacturers not reducing co2 emissions from light commercial vehicles should be higher than the penalties for not reducing emissions in the passenger car sector. i believe that the penalties should be equal in both sectors. i understand the reasons for introducing speed limitation devices for light commercial vehicles, but i am afraid that this could become a precedent for introducing speed limitation devices for other types of vehicles. careful consideration should be given to whether the regulation is excessively restrictive beyond the principle of proportionality.
in writing. - the fragmented system of patent protection is, in my view, an obstacle to the internal market, which is particularly harmful to innovative smes. that is why i support the creation of a simplified, inexpensive, single patent for the whole of the eu. i regret that we were unable to reach a common solution because of irreconcilable differences of opinion on the revision of the patent translations. i regard the insistence on the need to translate patents into more of the eu's official languages as a manifestation of national selfishness, since the resulting costs, administrative burdens and time would largely erase the benefits of the unitary patent. i very much welcome the decision of my own country, the czech republic, to join the enhanced cooperation we have supported today, joining the ranks of the vast majority of member states that have already joined. although the unitary patent will not cover the whole of the eu, i have no doubt that it will be a useful tool for the development and competitiveness of smes.
in writing. − patent protection is one of the key tools for competitiveness and economic growth. it supports scientific research activities and thereby increases employment in the high value-added sector. i supported the parliament's recommendation on the draft council decision authorising enhanced cooperation in the area of unitary patent protection, as it is based on the council conclusions of 4 december 2009 which define the future shape of the unitary patent system. this european patent system is to be based on two pillars, namely a single system for the settlement of patent disputes (european patent court and eu patent) and the creation of an eu patent (an instrument for the validity of a patent throughout the eu). enhanced patent cooperation will lead to easier validation of european patents in the territories of the participating member states, which will reduce costs and simplify the patent application process, while contributing to scientific and technical progress and enhancing the functioning of the internal market. the czech republic joined enhanced cooperation at the beginning of february this year and i therefore believe that my country's involvement in the system of unitary patent protection will also lead to the promotion of scientific capacities and better scientific results not only in the czech republic.
in writing. there is no doubt that the area of intellectual property rights, and in particular industrial property rights, deserves particular attention and care. but the eu institutions and bodies do not always succeed. complex system solutions are often overridden by particular or even lobbying interests. the problem of the eu formerly community patent is an example of this. the possibility now arises of at least a partial solution in the form of enhanced cooperation between some member states in the area of the creation of unitary patent protection. the czech republic is interested in participating in this enhanced cooperation and thus wishes to participate in the further negotiation of the specific proposals for the unified patent regulation and its language regime. failure to participate in enhanced cooperation would mean that it would be impossible to influence the shape of the future eu patent system. the economic contribution or benefits to the undertakings coming up with new technical solutions is also an important aspect, given the size of the common market of enhanced cooperation participants. i would also like to recall that the czech republic reserves the right to withdraw from enhanced cooperation if such cooperation takes a direction which is contrary to the positions of the czech republic, in particular on the language regime and the area of patent litigation.
in writing. − i am convinced that enhanced cooperation on patent protection will help to remove fragmentation in this area, provide better frameworks for innovative businesses across the union, lead to a more globally competitive eu and a better functioning eu internal market. the expected reduction in the costs associated with the patent protection process is also undeniable. that's why i endorsed the report.
mr president, whatever our relationship with the single currency, i believe it is in the interest of everyone living in the european union that the euro and the euro area develop stably, whether we use the euro or not, because our economies are very closely interlinked. however, when president barroso described the european stability mechanism, he used words such as better surveillance, broader economic coordination, coordination of budgetary policies, harmonisation of social policies. this, my friends, is opening the way to fiscal union, and economic history does not know that fiscal union could ever survive without the creation of political union. so this little change that we're talking about here today is a huge change for individual member states in the european union. the european union is changing politically for the future. mr weber said that 'european citizens want a stronger europe'. we've known that all along. let's ask them, because for decades now, european citizens have only accepted, not decided, what happens in the european institutions.
madam president, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on her excellent handling of the many hundreds of amendments that have been tabled. although the report is not legislative, it is of great importance for the future of social systems in the member states of the european union. the main conclusions we support are:- decision-making on pension systems is a political decision-making in the full and exclusive responsibility of individual member states; - the increase in the retirement age is necessary; - the only source of growth in the european union in the coming years will be labour productivity growth; - the key role is to promote the employment of older workers, in particular by making labour relations more flexible and by correctly adjusting pension systems; - coordination of pension systems and their reform at the level of the european union is not appropriate; - we do not support the unification of the retirement age within the eu or its link to the average lifespan; - we do not support any attempt to harmonise definitions, such as the adequacy of pensions, at community level.
in writing. colleagues, virtually all countries are faced with the question of how to finance pension systems based on rolling funding in the future, due to the ageing of the population. i appreciate the excellent report by mrs oomen-ruijten, which opens the door to solutions and honours subsidiarity. let me mention a recipe that has been promoted for many years by kdu-čsl in the czech republic. children who are gainfully employed should be able to transfer part of their compulsory contributions to the current scheme to a pension account for their parents and to partially compensate them for the costs of childcare through this contribution to a higher pension. this individualised intergenerational solidarity is both more fair to today's pensioners who have properly raised the next generation and is also certain to help rehabilitate the social status of multi-family families. having kids should pay off, not just pay off. this is the master key that permanently addresses both the consequences and, more importantly, the cause of our pension problem.
in writing. ten years of work on the green paper, wrongly titled 'towards adequate, sustainable and reliable pension systems in europe', has produced a completely anti-social collection of nonsense. i don't know how it is in other countries, but in the czech republic, for example, the birth rate projections that experts drew up ten years ago differ so radically from the current reality that i consider the green paper's claims for the years 2040 to 2060 to be completely nonsensical. if experts cannot predict the birth rate for five to seven years, what value can their forecasts have over a period of 30 years? the whole green paper is based on the false assumption that if i mandate by law that a taxpayer invest a portion of the funds allocated to the pension system in a non-guaranteed fund, then he will receive a higher pension than just the first pillar. the constitutional court responsible will necessarily declare such a law unconstitutional. even in countries where the second pillar was introduced in the past, it was found that this path does not lead. for example, in chile in the 1970s, after pinochet's coup, private funds not guaranteed by the state became a duty for citizens outside the military and the police. why would i do that? why is the current conservative hungarian government also eliminating the inefficient second pillar of pension security? these are the main reasons why the gue/ngl group cannot vote for the report by mrs oomen-ruijten. we can't support the upcoming heist of the century!
madam chairman, i would also like to thank the rapporteur and the shadow rapporteurs for a job well done. we all know the pharmaceutical industry is a huge business. they say it's the third largest in the world after drugs and guns. so it's a pie that someone will always try to get illegally. we'll never be able to stop fake drugs from coming. our task is to minimise the risk of them reaching the european market and reaching our citizens. we know that if someone sells a fake watch, it's a violation of intellectual property, but if they sell counterfeit medicines, it's a direct threat to health. that's what led us all to support this report. we know that it doesn't solve everything, we know that there's a problem with, for example, the fact that the packaging is often manufactured separately from the medicine, and there are counterfeit medicines in real packaging going around the world. but it's a step in the right direction and it's about maximizing protection.
madam chairman, for two years we have been discussing the alarming rise of counterfeit medicines, including life-saving ones, in our market. the result is a good directive, which tightens controls on distributors and dealers, introduces mandatory security features and checks on their integrity. manufacturers will also assess the risks of excipients, and this has been done for years in the czech republic, for example. so it's great that it's going to be for the whole of the european union. it is also necessary that the system is not limited to prescription medicines. if we are not to lose our fight against counterfeiting, member states will have to achieve a higher degree of integration in the health sector, agree to ban websites offering counterfeits and introduce inspections of producers in third countries, as they already do for imported food. there are 500 fake drug factories in china alone. we simply have to add more gas if we want to strengthen the security of the european market.
in writing. the report is generally favourable for croatia. it shows that the country is close to the end of accession negotiations. however, as in previous years, it also points to persistent, often serious, shortcomings. in the light of the experience of the croatian-slovenian territorial dispute, it is concerned that a more significant positive shift is not taking place in a number of territorial disputes between croatia and its other neighbours (in particular bosnia and herzegovina, but also serbia and montenegro). serious questions have long been raised by the very complacent attitude towards the punishment of war crimes committed against serbs. there are still "several hundred cases" to be investigated and prosecuted, whose ignorance by the croatian judiciary keeps the serbs suspicious. croatia has also still not submitted the so-called artillery diaries to the icty. so it is time to think about how far the eu wants to go on this issue. even the return of serb refugees in 2010 was limited. the report does not say how many of the approximately 130,000 serbs who returned to croatia after the war are from serbia and how many are from the republika srpska in bosnia and herzegovina. however, the number of serb refugees in the republic of serbia (bosnia and herzegovina) has remained roughly the same for several years (between 25 000 and 35 000 persons). the croatian authorities are preventing their return (mainly because they do not address issues such as the return of property, apartments, rental rights, pensions, etc.).
in writing. - the report proposed is a good continuation of the work carried out in the previous parliamentary term. the commission proposal, despite all the recommendations, avoids defining the terms in detail. such an approach could have the consequence that the protection of patients as consumers of medicinal products would not be complete and enforceable. i therefore welcome amendment no 120, which substantially clarifies the original commission proposal. the definition of 'falsified medicinal product' is very positive. the proposal on 'supply chains' is also excellent, as it increases the right of patients to protection if approved. i consider it absolutely essential to enforce recital 5c of amendment no 120, which provides for the possibility of enforcement and control of compliance with eu rules. the amendment brings eu rules into line with the conclusions of the court of justice. in this context, the proposal also supports member states' own action to increase consumer protection.
in writing. - as external financing for many smes depends on loans and credits, it is necessary to establish loan and credit guarantee programmes and to increase the role of the european investment bank in this respect. the commission should also seek to remove the obstacles to the development of risk capital and bond markets in europe and extend risk-sharing instruments to equity investments. this requires an increased awareness of the risks of many national tax and incentive systems that incentivise debt financing and discourage debt financing through equity capital increases. i consider the complexity of eu financial regulations and guidelines for european financial instruments to be a serious problem, especially when european funds and programmes are used to support individual businesses by providing relatively small amounts of funding. the time and financial requirements to meet the requirements of these regulations are totally disproportionate to the benefit to the final recipient of the funds. the fragmentation of sme support programmes is also unsatisfactory. the commission should ensure that the cip, the 7th framework programme and jeremie are more closely linked and that a single contact point is established for them, where smes can obtain clear and comprehensive information on the possibilities of using these instruments.
madam president, if we are to have a serious debate on the rise in food prices, we should not avoid the question, which may be unpleasant for some, of the extent to which the european union itself is contributing to this negative development. all commodity analysts agree that two factors are driving the rise in food prices. the first is the massive printing of new money that has been happening in the united states and the european union in recent months. and it is the influx of this new money into the markets that is driving up the prices of food and other commodities. so the fault is not with speculators, as the report we are discussing today claims, but with the faulty policies of the us, european and other central banks. the second factor pushing up food prices is the policy of both the european union and the united states, which is forcing consumers to use biofuels more and more. the commitments made by the eu and the us in this area have been the source of a sharp increase in the volume of non-food commodities grown on agricultural land. as a result, less and less agricultural land is used for food production, and food prices logically rise as a result. if we want to help reduce world food prices, we must abandon our nonsensical policy of promoting biofuels.
in writing. according to the world bank, food prices have risen by more than 15% since october 2010 alone, and for example, the price of cereals has increased by 39% year-on-year according to the fao. in addition to threatening the lower income groups, food price increases signal much deeper and more serious problems that pose a major threat for the future. global food production is threatened by a number of factors, such as pests, disease, and natural disasters. these factors are currently being exacerbated by extremes in weather likely to be caused by climate change. good examples are the drought and fires in russia in 2010, or the floods in pakistan and recently in australia. so it can be expected that the food price problem may worsen in the future. the debate on the origins of climate change is futile in this case, because the risks are real and questioning them is an unacceptable gamble. however, food prices are also influenced by economic factors, in particular the abuse of the market system by state manipulation of the us dollar and irresponsible speculation in commodity trades. europe must realise that the decisions it makes now will affect the future of the whole food system. in the short term, we can reduce food waste, reduce our dependence on imports, reduce or stabilise consumption and set up regulatory mechanisms in the market to prevent speculation.
mr commissioner, i wholeheartedly support all the steps taken by the high representative to help egypt achieve democracy and prosperity without further bloodshed. but that doesn't mean turning a blind eye to the growing violence that radical muslims are now committing against the copts. i'm in contact with europeans and czechs who are helping the copts, but they are literally risking their lives as well. the european union must call on the egyptian military to set an example by punishing the persecutors of christians. at the same time, the european union should actively help the endangered copts to temporarily reach a safe country. i would ask you to propose to the council an interim protection institute under directive 2001/55/ec to enable the copts to save their lives until egypt is a safer home for them. can you take a stand on that, commissioner?
in writing. the report presented focuses on several aspects of the european investment bank's activities. part of its activities focuses on delivering eu development policy in countries that have difficulty obtaining finance on acceptable terms on capital markets. through this policy, the eu, through the eib, seeks to bridge the gap between the north and the south, as less developed countries have less access to finance for the development of their economies. i believe it is entirely right that the eu's instruments for aid to third countries should be maintained and appropriately extended as an expression of the eu's solidarity with third countries and to fulfil the eu's share of responsibility for the functioning of the global economy. i consider the extension of the eib's mandate to include operational guidelines, to be drawn up by the commission together with the eib after consulting the european external action service, for each region under the mandate for lending to third countries, to be the right step to achieve the eu's external policy objectives. i also see the eib's mandate to lend to iceland, belarus, libya, iraq and cambodia as being activated. while iceland is an economic aid donor, the other countries mentioned above need to be very careful to ensure that aid is directed exclusively towards the promotion of democracy and human rights.

in writing. since 1963, the european investment bank (eib) has been conducting financing operations outside the eu in addition to its core mission of financing investment in the eu in support of the eu's external policy objectives, in particular in the field of development cooperation. the proposed eu guarantee for the period 2011-2013 aims to enable the eib to finance investments outside the eu without adversely affecting its credit standing. the proposal also contains several new elements, such as the activation of an additional mandate of eur 2 billion to combat climate change, fully in line with the europe 2020 strategy, the introduction of new horizontal targets and the extension of the range of countries for eib lending. the proposal also includes requirements for greater transparency, evaluation of concrete results and the role of the european court of auditors in carrying out audits of the transactions concerned. the proposal also correctly contains an arrangement for the eib to act in this area in accordance with the principles of aid effectiveness under the european consensus on development, as well as article 208 tfeu, the 2005 paris declaration and the 2008 accra action programme. overall, i consider that the report presented by my colleague ivail kalfin provides a detailed analysis of the issue, refers to the relevant eu legislation and also contains relevant recommendations in the area of monitoring the effectiveness of the proposed guarantee, and therefore i recommend that it be approved as proposed.
in writing. in addition to my position and in view of the preparation process for the eu-republic of korea free trade agreement, which is accompanied by a number of concerns and expectations not only of the industries concerned, i would like to add the following. the long-standing trade rules between the eu and the republic of korea are hereby significantly modified. assessing their impact will only be realistic over a longer time horizon. only results and calculations after a longer period of time show the state of the considered economic prosperity of all the parties to this agreement, the declared bilateral improvement of the trade balance or the contribution to the improvement of the social situation. the total quantity and variety of products traded between them, which are inextricably linked to their certified quality and proven place of origin, need to be monitored and evaluated regularly, in a coordinated and careful manner. the possible direct application of the aforementioned safeguards arising from the bilateral safeguard clause of the eu-republic of korea free trade agreement would be a highly sensitive issue and the result could be contrary to the euphoria preceding the adoption of this agreement.
in writing. the danube river connects ten european countries. the danube also represents a major socio-economic potential, with over 200 million people living in its vicinity. the danube is an important interface for eu cohesion policy programmes. the danube strategy focuses on three priority areas: connectivity and communication (including transport, energy, information society); environmental protection and prevention of natural hazards; strengthening the potential for socio-economic development. i believe that the implementation of the danube strategy should be based on a 'bottom up approach' in terms of facilitating the movement of goods and improving infrastructure, and thus the general conditions for doing business throughout the region. the strategy should not be the reason for the creation of new institutions. its main purpose should be to strengthen coordination between the various regional actors. for this reason, no specific financial allocation should be made to the strategy. the principle of budgetary, legislative and administrative neutrality, which was approved by the council, must therefore be maintained. in this sense, macro-regional strategies should be primarily a tool for more effective cooperation and the use of existing resources. at the same time, criticism of the commission's intention to encourage shipping on the danube mainly by improving its navigability must be addressed. mechanical interventions in the riverbed, according to critics, will endanger the vegetation on its banks and the overall deterioration of the surrounding environment.
in writing. - i have read the motion for a resolution on the rule of law in russia. i have three little notes. in particular, i think it is right that the european parliament should use every opportunity to raise the issue of human rights and the quality of the rule of law. we should do this more often, and we should do it to all countries. the second point concerns the form. our comments must be objective. that means not just paternalistically instructing others, but offering a helping hand. honestly, i don't understand how all the critics of the trial of the yukos oligarchs can be so sure that it is a trial under political pressure? the political pressure is certainly to challenge the verdict, which does not take into account the evidence presented of tax evasion and other violations of the law. why do critics automatically assume that the evidence is fabricated? i could give you more examples of this. russia has two basic problems related to the rule of law; the first is the fact that it is undergoing a transformation of law and legal culture. it takes time, not just the goodwill of the kremlin. the second is the unstable security situation and the distrust of the good intentions of some of the advisers. how will the european union help russia in the fight against terrorism and in the extradition of terror suspects who have found safe haven in the union states? how will it deal with violations of the political rights of so-called non-citizens in the countries of the union? it's not enough to advise, it's necessary to help.
for the ecr group. mr. chairman, the board in this room says that today's material is called innovative finance. i think it is fair and correct to say that we are talking about the introduction of new european taxes and we already know in advance who will pay these new european taxes. they're paid by the banks and they're paid by the financial institutions. i think it's wrong to blame the banks for starting the financial crisis or for causing the economic crisis, because we know for a long time that the main causes of the crisis are economic imbalances in the global economy, long-term low interest rates, excessive regulation of the financial sector and, unfortunately, political interference in areas where political agencies have no place. it is very controversial to read in the material how much, for example, the european union or european institutions will gain from the introduction of a financial transaction tax, because as with any tax and with the introduction of any new taxes, it is very likely that the financial sector will optimise its revenues or even - unfortunately for the european economy - move its tax residences outside the european union. and if banks and financial institutions stay here and if they provide financial services, as of course they will, then of course they pass these costs on to their clients and to consumers. i think that european countries - and this is well illustrated - are going through a very complicated period of fiscal consolidation and the european institutions should not be left out. unfortunately, not a word about the european institutions cutting spending, but rather about the introduction of new taxes. if the european institutions were to get rid of the parts of their european agencies that are often unnecessary, redundant and expensive, there would certainly be funds left to support some of the european projects. let me say one more thing about eurobonds. if the eurobonds are approved, then there is no need to save, no need to save, no need to reform, because someone will always pay. the moral hazard is deepening. european conservatives will vote against this bill because we are against the introduction of new taxes or raising taxes.
mr president, the baltic macro strategy is clearly inspiring other regions. after the danube macro strategy, we have a strategy for the atlantic region on the table. but in this case, the situation is somewhat specific. the european union has been paying attention to the atlantic region for some time now, in the context of the development of crossborder and transnational cooperation. the atlantic region was a region supported under interreg iii b and is also an integral part of the current objective 3 territorial cooperation programme. the priority of these supported programmes was to extend regional development strategies to the transnational level, to promote transport systems and better access to the information society, to promote environmental and natural resources protection and to promote the economic integration of peripheral regions in particular. it would therefore be highly desirable to first evaluate existing programmes, define their contribution and then, on the basis of the findings, develop a new strategy for this area. but we should not forget to include transatlantic links in this strategy. like it or not, we are part of a euro-atlantic civilization and share similar values. as overseas, we seek a land of freedom from barriers to the free movement of goods, capital, people and services. although the objectives of the united states and the european union are in principle complementary, they are often only minimally coordinated. it is therefore desirable to create the conditions for transatlantic cooperation in european programmes and policies. more ambitious cooperation and better coordination within the european union atlantic strategy could try to cover a more ambitious agenda in this regard, as voluntary practical cooperation on common themes could greatly assist in strengthening transatlantic ties.
mr president, allow me to draw the attention of the european parliament to the judgment of the european court of justice last week, c-236/09, which will make it impossible to distinguish between insured persons by sex as from 21 december next year. the union directive or its interpretation in this case clearly exceeded the threshold, is contrary to the laws of nature and will have a substantially negative impact on the insurance sector. it is as illogical as forbidding insurance companies to take into account, when calculating the cost of nonlife insurance, whether a house is on a hill or in a swampy area or whether a motor vehicle driver is a frequent wrecker. and it's not just demographers who know that in advanced societies, for almost the entire age range, the mortality rate of the male population is higher than the mortality rate of the female population, and usually very significantly higher. women tend to live 10 percent longer than men. in the czech republic, it is now 73 years for men and 80 years for women. to deny this truth and to impose a directive from brussels on insurance companies not to distinguish between men and women is contrary to all the mathematics of insurance, but above all it is a fundamental discrimination against groups of insured persons. as a result, women will have to pay higher premiums and men will receive lower pensions.
mr president, we have been looking for a long time here in parliament for instruments which would make it possible to prevent more and more dangerous products from entering the market. i agree with the author of the report that a review of the legislation is needed and that it should lead to better coherence of all measures, including the coordination of criminal sanctions at member state level. i also support the proposal to make it compulsory to carry out and publish risk analyses before new production is launched. since it is precisely from third countries that the most dangerous products come, i consider it crucial that this obligation be included in international trade agreements before we introduce it for european producers. i also support the other proposals and appreciate the high quality of mrs schaldemos' report.

mr president, we have been very active in promoting innovative medicine in recent years, and i therefore welcome the report which draws attention to the disadvantaged groups in access to treatment and, in particular, prevention, which i consider to be crucial. however, in a number of respects, the report infringes the subsidiarity principle of the member states. i fundamentally reject the wording of, say, paragraph 25, because abortion should not be a method of birth control. points 29, 26 and 53 are also in dispute. i also want to point out that life expectancy, which shows really large differences within the 27s, is only partly driven by the level of health care and access to it. this is more influenced by the overall standard of living, lifestyle, or level of development or well-being of countries, which are not the same and which should help to balance even development programmes financed by eu funds.
in writing. any food dependence puts the eu in a very vulnerable position. in the case of protein crops, their cultivation brings a number of economic, ecological and agronomic benefits. reducing meat consumption in favour of plant-based protein in the diet has positive effects on the environment and human health. excessive imports of protein crops and their derivatives destabilise european agriculture, especially small and medium-sized farms, and contribute to price instability. the low level of domestic production of protein crops, in turn, limits research, seed production and knowledge of sustainable technologies, in particular as regards the inclusion of husks in sowing practices and the adequate use of grassland. there are a few key measures that need to be taken. the commission must report on the possibilities for increasing protein crop production in the eu, support research, breeding and seeding of protein crops and, in the context of the rural development programme, promote protein crops as a quality source of feed. a monitoring mechanism for the origin of imported protein crops focusing on the sustainability of the farming practices used by the countries of origin should be established. the commission is also invited to present targeted measures to promote the cultivation of fallow land, which could make a significant contribution to reducing the protein deficit in the eu. in the context of climate change, soil fertility, water protection and biodiversity, the production and targeted support of protein crops should be increased in the european union.
madam president, it is clear that the entire european union and its member states will continue to face the fading effects of the economic and financial crisis in 2012. the european parliament must therefore adapt its budgetary strategy to this situation. we must strive for maximum budget discipline and austerity. i am glad to have found this fact in the report and that the report emphasizes this fact. but alignment cannot mean spending growth in line with inflation. our aim must be to keep parliament's expenditure at the same level as this year. the maximum effort must be made to save and make better use of existing parliamentary capacities. this must cover all areas of parliament's work, from the rationalisation of building management to the better use of information technology and personnel policy. the opportunity to save money exists here and we must find the courage to realize it.
for the ecr group. madam president, i am the shadow rapporteur for the report on the situation of women in poverty in europe, so i will focus on this report. this report covers a whole range of issues that illustrate very well the position of women in the european union. i am pleased that there are many points in this report which point to the need to make use of flexible forms of work, including fixed-term and part-time work, because i believe that women who are on their own, in particular, but also families with young children in general, need to take advantage of this wide range of offers on the labour market in order to be able to combine their working and family life. at the same time, i of course welcome and fully support the fact that the continuing large gender pay gap is again highlighted. i just want to say, very quickly, that the report contains some things that we can hardly support. this is the case, for example, with the call for a european charter of women's rights or the reference to the report on maternity leave, which, as we know, has not been accepted by the member states. in this spirit, we have tabled certain amendments. i would like to thank the rapporteur, because the report is really good and comprehensive.
in writing. the issue of gender equality, especially at the time of the centenary of international women's day, deserves due attention. i personally consider a number of points in the report to be extremely important. one of the things i'd like to highlight is the gender pension gap. this inequality is due to the fact that working women interrupt their professional activity more often than men to take care of children and sick or elderly family members, and because of their family obligations, they are more willing than men to accept part-time work or work in precarious conditions. this is precisely what leads to inequality in pension levels. i consider it equally important to call for special attention to be paid to vulnerable groups of women, such as disabled or elderly women, immigrant women, lesbian, bisexual and transgender women or minorities, and low-skilled or unskilled women caring for dependent persons. lastly, i consider it important to ensure access to support services aimed at preventing gender-based violence and protecting women from it, regardless of their legal status, race, age, sexual orientation, ethnic origin or religion, as the report also calls on the commission and the member states to do.
in writing. even a century after the first public appearances of women in the struggle for their rights, it has not yet brought the desired result. quite the opposite. the current crisis is turning the wheel of history in the solution of the equality of men and women back a few meters. women today, just as they did 100 years ago, are taking to the streets again all over the world. they are leading demonstrations in greece, spain, the czech republic and belgium, but also in north african countries. what the eu had boasted of until recently - the improvement in women's position in the labour market and in access to education, the quality of life, the provision of health care for women and greater representation in state bodies - seemed to be pushed to the margins of society's interests as the crisis deepened. anti-crisis measures are more targeted at the male population. women's needs are seen as secondary. the last hundred years have shown what women have been able to give to the world, the progress, the lives they have sacrificed in the struggle for peace. and how many new lives they've brought into the world. that's not a small thing. but the world still owes women something. not to take them at their mercy, but to clearly declare their equal status in society. these women are not fighting for themselves, but for a better, more humane world.
in writing. social cohesion and the european social model are values that distinguish europe from societies in other parts of the world. the question is how sustainable these values are in the context of the call for global competitiveness based on lower labour costs, relaxation of labour laws, reduced social protection for workers and a weakened role for the state. this is unlikely to significantly strengthen global competitiveness, but it is much more likely to irreversibly undermine social cohesion and open up the gaps in society. and let's not be wrong. women are being threatened in a fundamental way by these measures. the pay gap has a significant impact, as have budget cuts in the public sector. pension and health reforms at national level must be gender-equitable from this perspective. it is no coincidence that older women are the most affected by poverty in europe. the fight against these inequalities is not a virtual reality. it's about specific people.
in writing. − i support the final report on the review of the general product safety and market surveillance directive. i appreciate that the final text no longer contains proposals for the establishment of additional agencies and that this will not place a further burden on the budget. i would like to highlight in the report the emphasis it places on standardisation as a regulatory tool, which is essentially an example of 'smart regulation', i.e. regulation that is not designed from above but from below by stakeholders. this is why i would call for greater involvement of market surveillance authorities in the process of developing safety standards, as this is an opportunity to ensure that their knowledge is adequately taken into account in the standardisation process. in the context of greater accessibility in the area of product safety standards development, i see the need to focus on the stability of the european standardisation system, to simplify european standards and to reduce the time needed to develop them.
in writing. - i would like to thank the rapporteur for a very detailed and frank report, which contains many alarming statements. there is no doubt that the eu's common approach during the pandemic has tested the capacity of joint action, but it has also revealed significant weaknesses, especially in the areas of prevention, objective statistics and the procurement of safe and effective medicines. the eu has recently faced the challenge of a dangerous viral infection twice. the experience was not sufficiently exploited. i welcome the proposals to tighten up the verification of the efficacy of medicines, the pressure to collect objective data and the interest in protecting all eu citizens, regardless of their healthcare system. i consider the requirement of funding for research and development and the rule that pharmaceutical companies will have a consultative voice, not a decisive one, to be essential. the same is true of studies, testing of vaccines and other medicines that must be developed independently of pharmaceutical companies. i also support the call for stricter rules for experts and advisers working for the european institutions. the public must be informed in a clear and transparent manner, but in a manner that does not cause panic but provides qualified and comprehensible information. as quaestor with joint responsibility for the health protection of meps and staff, i can say that our measures in the ep have been effective, but not cheap.
in writing. the eu is still reeling from the financial crisis, which in 2009-2010 spilled over into a budgetary crisis, with the over-issuance of government bonds as a significant aspect. however, unlike the approved report, i see less market pressure than the irresponsible fiscal policies of many national governments unwilling to reform their pension, health and employment systems. i see blaming the market for failure as an alibi and turning a blind eye to the lack of courage to make the necessary reforms. we have already approved rules for stronger financial regulation and supervision of the financial market, now we should move on to the impulses to stimulate the european economy, for example in the form of funding for joint infrastructure projects and tax incentives for businesses to invest in science and research. i warn against the introduction of a financial transaction tax and various forms of bank taxes, as this would result in the deception of the financial market and distortion of competition. nor are eurobonds a solution, which would merely shift fiscal irresponsibility to the european level, to places further away from public control, where the temptation to overspend would be even more resisted than at the national level. i also do not share the idea of a pan-european carbon tax, which would disproportionately burden the energy sector in particular and ultimately reduce the eu's energy security.
in writing. financial transactions account for a large proportion of resources in the financial sector, and it was the various speculative operations in financial markets that contributed significantly to the global financial crisis. it is certainly not right to leave the burden of the financial crisis solely on taxpayers, but it is necessary to shift some of this burden onto the financial sector to share the costs as well. the report focuses primarily on those transactions that are highly risky and speculative, and therefore, as a result of future taxation of financial transactions, will lead to a greater degree of financial sector responsibility, which paradoxically will lead to a greater degree of stability of the global financial sector. another reason for introducing a financial transaction tax is also the fact that in most member states financial services are not subject to vat.
in writing. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on her excellent work and thank her for accepting my amendments. the issue of equal access to health is one of the pillars of our common policy, both now and especially in the future. inequality in the performance of economies and the current crisis must not be the limit to ensuring equal opportunities for eu citizens to receive healthcare, regardless of the diversity of health systems. i find the emphasis in the text on raising patient awareness, quality legal advice, including for immigrants, including illegal immigrants, very important. the basic principle must be that the health of individual citizens is ensured by territorial and financial accessibility, not just of medicines. ensuring coordination of education and training for healthcare professionals and establishing minimum standards of quality and safety of care will be important factors for progress. the rapporteur rightly emphasises the need to prevent both smoking and obesity and other diseases that affect active life expectancy. i support the strong call for the commission to influence member states to provide and pay for treatment for patients with diseases such as alzheimer's, diabetes, multiple sclerosis. these are diseases for which treatment is not covered in some member states. these diseases affect both the elderly and, in particular, young people and lead to social exclusion.
in writing. − i consider it important to highlight the link between eu agricultural production and international trade, which is reflected in the fact that the eu is the world's largest importer of agricultural goods and also the world's largest exporter of processed food products. in this context, i am convinced that smooth trade flows contribute to greater flexibility for producers and to lower costs of exchanging goods, which has a positive impact on both producers' incomes and consumers' expenditure. i cannot therefore agree with the statement in the resolution that trade liberalization has not eliminated the threat of hunger in the world. such a task does not and cannot belong to the business. i commend the commission for using the steps resulting from the long-running reform as a bargaining chip in the wto negotiations and in bilateral or bi-regional negotiations in the field of agriculture, while always recognising the cap parameters as an unbridgeable boundary. in this respect, the adopted text of the resolution seems to me to be unfortunately rather one-sided and to call into question the commission's actions in the current trade policy negotiations. i see a clear opportunity and benefit in any further flexibility in the forthcoming negotiations. to the criticism of some of the measures, such as the reduction of import duties on bananas or the reform of the sugar regime, i add that they were primarily based on the conclusions of the wto dispute settlement, which also influenced the parameters of subsequent measures on the eu side.
in writing. the decline in the production of protein-rich crops and the replacement of domestic production by massive cheap imports may ultimately have a negative impact in terms of loss of experience in the cultivation processes themselves. research on growing these crops in domestic conditions can be largely suppressed. i also consider it important to maintain sufficient variety in the selection or supply of this production. as in many reports and proposals before parliament, the need for quality production is mentioned. in relation to the production referred to above, it is necessary to check the samples themselves, which would be inseparable from the verification of the agricultural practices and means used locally. quality control using modern laboratory methods and the unambiguous determination of the origin of the goods at import should be very careful and rigorous. for most eu countries, there is some typical crop from this production. in the czech republic, for example, it is a game that has its long tradition here. however, its cultivation is now in decline. the position of the czech republic supports the report submitted in general terms.
in writing. as a member of the european parliament and as a doctor, i agree with the conclusions of the report. the damage caused by the anti-social policies of most eu governments will sooner or later affect the health of many socially vulnerable groups of citizens, especially women. it is precisely the concern for the healthy development of the present and future population of europe, which is directly proportional to the quality of health and to the full, equal social status of women in society, that led me to vote in favour of the report. if we are not indifferent to the fate of europe, to the viability and development of the eu, we cannot solve its problems without ensuring equal status for women. to underestimate this question is to disregard the rights of 50% of eu citizens. this may ultimately lead to the alienation of many citizens from the eu, even though the europe 2020 strategy does not forget to address these issues. the eu, its men and women. they embody her potential and creative development. when poverty and social exclusion, as statistical surveys in the eu show, affect almost one in six citizens, it is up to us as members not only to call on the commission and the member states, but also to put effective pressure on the implementation of the concrete measures taken to combat poverty, anti-social austerity measures and to prevent further social declines, especially for women.
in writing. − the ecr group recognises that a number of points in this report are necessary in order to encourage women into the labour market, for example, flexible working, childcare provisions and maternity leave provision, and we welcome the calls to eradicate the gender pay gap. the ecr group, however, believes that these are policy areas which should be addressed at national level, not eu level. the ecr group does oppose an unnecessary and bureaucratic women's charter and any moves towards the harmonisation of social security systems across the eu. we have therefore decided to abstain on the final vote on this report and have voted in favour of specific paragraphs which we feel are important, for example paragraphs 34, 58, 60, 65 and 69.
mr president, the 2010 report on turkey's humility is certainly one of the more complicated reports we are discussing here. despite all the complexity, we should not be swayed by historical prejudices. the events in turkey recently are undoubtedly matters that are unacceptable in the sense of european democratic principles. we should intensify our negotiations with the turkish authorities even more. turkey's geopolitical importance to the complex middle east region is crucial for europe. of course, this position cannot be abused. but if we have an open and principled dialogue with the turkish representatives, that may be the way to improve the understanding of democratic rules. turkey's economic development in recent years has been truly remarkable, and its industry is becoming an important partner for european companies in many areas. it is therefore important that these economic relations also correspond to the political relations of the european union with a clear outlook for the future. if turkey is really interested in joining the european union, it cannot take the steps we have seen recently. for many reasons, i strongly support the further process of turkey's rapprochement with the european union.
we're only two years away from a big anniversary. this year marks 48 years since turkey became a candidate country for eu membership. a lot has happened in that time, no doubt. the country's economic development, together with the opening up of the eu market, certainly played a role. however, the country's problem still remains a number of elements that were established already at the birth of the modern turkish state. the 1924 treaty clearly states that citizens of islam are turks, citizens of other faiths are greek catholics. other nationalities are not covered by this treaty. what was progressive in 1924, the recognition that one could be of a different faith than muslim, has a completely different connotation today. declaring a non-turkish nationality is not an easy matter in turkey, whether it is kurds, circassians or other nationalities who have obviously lived in turkey for years, but their recognition including enforcement of their nationality rights in real life will take a long time. the key elements of the current negotiating impasse are the failure to resolve the occupation of cyprus, the inaction on the un resolution on famagusta and the failure to fulfil the 'tasks' of past reports. reducing the role of the military is a positive, but only if secular forces are in power. otherwise, he could threaten the stability of the entire region.
in writing. the report on the european roma inclusion strategy lists the issues and difficulties faced by roma communities in the eu. the nature of poverty in central and south-eastern europe has changed significantly over the past two decades. political and economic changes have also disrupted traditional communities, making roma the most excluded and least educated subgroup in society. the romani population is also characterised by a typical demographic dynamic. as the majority of society ages, the share of roma in a high-growth society increases. all of this makes it clear that, without increasing roma employment and integrating more roma into mainstream society, the costs of sustaining the situation will continue to rise, such as the costs of social assistance and benefits, unnecessary education costs, additional costs of security, administrative costs of overseeing social spending, and higher healthcare costs. roma inclusion is therefore not only a human rights and moral obligation but also, and above all, an economic necessity. most of the measures implemented so far are so-called non-enforceable provisions. however, if progress is to be made, the eu roma inclusion strategy needs to go beyond this so-called soft law and become part of the instruments provided for by primary law.
the debate on industry is very important for europe today. the competitiveness of european companies is crucial to the continued economic stability of europe. we are all aware of these arguments, but on the other hand we are often tempted to endorse commitments, whether in the form of extreme environmental demands or mandatory fiscal obligations to ensure financial stability. it is certainly important to assess the environmental impact of industrial production rigorously, but we must not burden industry indirectly with some excessive regulations. in the context of a globalised world, it may be that european industrial production will be the one most affected by policy decisions. i consider the most important point in the report to be the emphasis on the importance of promoting science and research and innovation. europe has long been a leader in innovation. support for innovative projects is essential. i strongly agree that there is a need to rethink the procedures for financial support for innovative projects. we should aim to simplify administration, but we also need to change the overall approach. it is necessary to take into account the commercialisation of research and the financing of european funds.
madam president, i would like to make just three comments. the first is that europe should strive for a pioneering spirit, to have technological priorities, to be the leader in certain fields, to have not only comparative advantages but also absolute advantages, because average product quality also results in average prices. secondly, i would say that it is important to seek to mediate between large and small systems, because concentration on large systems does not produce the desired results. an example is the general motors failure, which cost the american taxpayer $57.6 billion. the third thing is that industrial policy should not be based on the so-called political and economic elites alone, but should be based on a big stream of small innovations from below, i.e. the big technological breakthroughs are based on a large number of small innovations that come from the work collectives.
in writing. greece's long-term fiscal imbalance resulted in a major crisis rescue package in may 2010 totalling €720 billion from the eu and the international monetary fund. despite the creation of a special european union financial stability facility (efsf) for euro area countries with a budget of up to eur 440 billion, it cannot be said that all the eu's problems have been overcome. moreover, the efsf is limited in its operations at the end of 2012 and there is some uncertainty among financial investors about what will happen after that date, or after the expiry of the guarantees provided, with the debts of the euro area countries in difficulty. therefore, the amendment of article 136 tfeu and the creation of a stabilisation mechanism after 2013 is desirable for the future financial and fiscal stability of the eu. however, i would point out that this mechanism will not be self-sustaining for the budgetary rescue of the problem countries (periphery) and that a comprehensive solution to the problem (reduction of public debt, restoration of the credibility of the banking sector and structural reforms, including fiscal consolidation of the peripheral countries to increase their competitiveness) should also be considered. overall, i believe that the report presented by my colleagues elmar brok and robert gualtieri provides a precise analysis of the issue, refers to the relevant eu legislation including the opinion of the budget committee, and therefore i recommend that the amendment to article 136 tfeu as proposed be approved.
in writing. the granting of candidate status to montenegro was primarily a political decision. if this issue had not been considered in relation to albania at the same time, montenegro would probably still have waited for him. but this comparison is, of course, a positive one for her. however, many of the objections to montenegro have been passed from one annual report to another, culminating in the ec's statement: politicization of the state administration, independence of the judiciary, organised crime and the environment are already among the 'evergreens'. nor is there any major shift in the fight against pervasive corruption on the horizon. let's not underestimate that. the commission has also taken into account the fact that the accession negotiations are still ongoing and that the accession negotiations are still ongoing. the eu adopted this approach to the former yugoslav republic of macedonia in 2005. the difference is that to overcome the obstacle faced by the former yugoslav republic of macedonia requires the agreement of two states. while montenegro has its fate in its own hands. i suppose all this was made clear in the recent interview of herman van rompuy, jos barros and jerzy buzek with the new and youngest prime minister in the world, igor lukšić of montenegro. because no one likes to be fooled for too long. this is true even for a country that, although not in the eu, has used the euro as its national currency for many years.
- the collection and provision of data on the socioeconomic situation of roma (mainly education, health, housing and employment) is a prerequisite for the development of any meaningful roma inclusion strategy. member states and international organisations (mop and oecd) should therefore focus on these issues in more detail, helping to set specific targets for, for example, the percentage of the roma community completing secondary and higher education, employed in public administration or represented in various areas of social and political life. it is then up to the european commission to draw up a clear and workable eu roma inclusion strategy on the basis of this data. consideration is being given to whether a performance premium for the eu roma strategy should be created under cohesion policy. in any case, more effective methodologies for monitoring the use of eu funds specifically targeted at marginalised groups need to be developed. the subsidy opportunities provided by the progress programme, the lifelong learning programme, the culture programme (2007-2013) and the public health programme (2008-2013) should also be better used to promote roma inclusion. in this context, the commission should provide the ep with a list of projects in favour of roma which it has funded since 2000 and indicate the results achieved by the projects.
in writing. − any european roma inclusion strategy must first be based on knowledge of local conditions. it is therefore not conceivable without close cooperation with local authorities and communities, where the declaration must be transformed in the daily coexistence of the majority society with the roma community. the report does not omit this element, which is why i voted in favour of its adoption.
in writing. european industry is still dealing with the consequences of the economic crisis and needs a coordinated approach at eu level to deal with them successfully. an eu industrial strategy should be developed, identifying strategic areas for investment and reflecting these priorities in future financial perspectives, annual budgets and eu policies. the development of european industry requires ambitious funding, particularly in the fields of research and energy, telecommunications and transport infrastructure (tens), i.e. public services that create a business environment. however, i do not think that it is necessary or appropriate to introduce the issuing of eu bonds (eurobonds or project bonds) for this purpose. the eu is not a sovereign political entity and its revenues should, in principle, be made up of contributions from the member states. not to mention that the idea of eurobonds invites borrowing and that could run counter to the principle of the european budget being balanced. financial support for innovation, infrastructure and reindustrialisation should be based in particular on increased funding for the 8th framework programme for research and development and the competitiveness and innovation programme, with the aim of ensuring that european industry maintains its global competitiveness and thus also makes effective use of the private investment made.
in writing. industry generates about one third of the eu's gdp, three quarters of exports are industrial goods and it employs 57 million european citizens in total. european industry was already undergoing a restructuring process before the crisis. it is currently affected mainly by the rapid development and changes in the global economy and by increasing competition. a new form of globalisation is developing towards a global society based on knowledge, research and innovation. the need to mitigate climate change and conserve biodiversity to the greatest extent possible is driving industry to produce with zero co2 emissions and to use resources efficiently. european industry should continue to maintain its strategic position in key sectors and exploit the opportunities of sustainable recovery to ensure the maximum possible employment for skilled workers. to make this possible, the rapporteur proposes a number of measures. i agree and support the creation of an innovation chain, the increase of resource efficiency, the more efficient use of public procurement, especially for smes, the further expansion of clean energy production and the overall integration of smes into the european industrial concept. the report is a benefit to european industry, the rapporteur really understands the issues involved, and i therefore support the adoption of this report by my vote.
in writing. the industrial policy report for the globalisation era is an important document in the crisis affecting the european economy, primarily industrial production. but it doesn't address the root cause of these huge economic problems. it is the global capitalism of free competition and the unbridled neoliberalism on which the european union is unfortunately built. the european union does not need more strategic documents, which are literally being thrown out by the european commission, but it urgently needs a change in its substance, first and foremost a social and peaceful union with a regulated financial sector. and when we talk about the 'strategic' document as such, as practice shows, it is not so much a problem to produce it as to subsequently develop it into individual areas and to monitor and evaluate flexibly the effects of the proposed measures. for example, the area of intellectual property rights, including industrial property rights, is evidence of this.
mr. chairman, in light of what is happening in the arab world, libya, egypt, tunisia and elsewhere, the rigidity and immobility of the iranian regime stands out even more. it's a regime that's intolerant, it's a regime that's fundamentalist, a regime that murders political opponents, and it needs to be held under international pressure. we need to support the iranian opposition in exile, which is legal, we have a european parliament resolution for that. the situation in the ashraf refugee camp in iraq, where more than 3 000 iranian refugees from exiled opposition organisations are living and where a humanitarian catastrophe is threatening, must be closely monitored and protected. i would therefore like to hear from baroness ashton a clear position on this matter, i would like to hear a clear statement on how the european union, under her leadership, will help these refugees in camp ashraf.
