in writing. freedom and pluralism of the media are certainly one of the pillars of a democratic rule of law. the hungarian media law certainly deserved criticism, but not the irritation and hasty and unjustified attacks on the hungarian government timed especially for the beginning of the hungarian presidency of the european union that we have witnessed. i am all the more pleased that the hungarian parliament has just approved amendments to the controversial media law in points proposed by the european union, thus taking the wind out of the sails for ideological provocateurs. the key requirement that 'news balance' does not apply to internet sites, blogs and debates, or to foreign correspondents, has thus been met. the european commission should now monitor the application of the law on an ongoing basis. the constructive dialogue between hungary and the eu, which resulted in the approved legislative changes, should be appreciated. foreign media and journalists operating in the country are no longer faced with high fines for breaching the hungarian media law, only 'other legal consequences'. however, fines remain in place for hungarian media owners, including those who 'formally moved their headquarters to another eu country to avoid the hungarian media law'. this arrangement, although we may disagree with it, is the full competence of the hungarian parliament and eu officials should respect it.
in writing. − the procedure which has been imposed by the situation should be conducted with great sensitivity and correctness. on the basis of a thorough analysis of the situation, further appropriate measures could then be taken, including the possibility of direct solutions and support in the social and other areas. the aim should be to find a solution that has a clear and well-founded long-term perspective, contributes to the preservation of the sovereignty of this country and is duly tolerant of its traditions. the proposed and much discussed no-fly zone is one of the steps that is declared to protect the civilian population first and foremost. in this context, i regard the first clear confirmation of the mandate as having the widest possible international base, and in particular the conditionality of the mandate of the un security council. the draft resolutions being prepared should not be influenced by the mere sight of the amount of raw materials resources in this country.
in writing. the introduction of a stabilisation mechanism for euro-paying countries is the first use of the possibility of a simplified amendment to the lisbon treaty. this is a change that non-euro countries should also support, as the stability of the euro is undoubtedly in their vital interest too. however, i believe that this mechanism should not be abused and i am therefore pleased that it has been possible to ensure that this mechanism is activated only in cases where it is really necessary. this is an important signal towards the responsibility of both states and creditors, as no 'transfer union' is created in which budgetary irresponsible states manage at the expense of the creditworthiness of countries with consolidated public finances. on the other hand, i am not concerned that the proposed amendment would create the conditions for the introduction of a stability mechanism entirely outside the union's remit. in any event, the interpretation and application of the relevant provisions of the treaty of lisbon will be subject to the control of the european court of justice. the willingness or unwillingness of states to involve other union institutions in the mechanism must be respected, and the possible intergovernmental nature of the mechanism certainly does not have to be at the expense of its quality or democratic legitimacy. if the financial stability mechanism is linked to national budgets, its intergovernmental nature would be perfectly understandable and logical.
in writing. the report contains a proposal for a european stability mechanism, which is an integral part of the overall package of measures designed to create a new framework while improving budgetary discipline and coordination of economic and financial policies of the member states, which should include support for a common european response to growth challenges, while at the same time leading to the elimination of economic and social inequalities and improving competitiveness. i think it is very appropriate to call on the commission to also look at other mechanisms to ensure financial stability and economic growth in the euro area. i think it is entirely appropriate to call on the commission to submit legislative proposals which would include a stability mechanism and measures to reduce risks to financial, economic and social stability in the european union, including effective regulation of financial markets. it is entirely appropriate to review the stability and growth pact. the report also supports the introduction of instruments to reduce macroeconomic imbalances in the euro area and measures to promote environmental recovery. it is not only for the above reasons that i have decided to support the report.
on behalf of the ecr group. – madam president, i regret to say that it seems that this european council could bring on some dangerous changes, and we should be concerned about this. under the banner of so-called economic coordination, economic governance, stability for the euro area, we see a favouring of eu-wide tax harmonisation and eu-wide fiscal unification. parliament, unfortunately, has been playing a very active role in that. let me speak clearly. the commission’s proposal of a common consolidated corporate tax base is a risky step that will undermine healthy tax competition amongst member states, prove harmful to the economy and drive business away from those who impose the policy. this has all gone too far and must be stopped. what we have also seen in the last few days is an attempt by some member states to interfere in the fiscal and tax sovereignty of others. you know what i am referring to: the very disgraceful bullying of ireland. we must not let this happen again. turning to libya, developments in libya have reminded us once again that we should not expect too much from the so-called eu common foreign and security policy. national leaders, not eu leaders, have shown international leadership. prime minister cameron and president sarkozy have made and pushed through decisions – not the eu representatives. i think that it shows clearly that there are limits to integration in this field – but not only in this field. no member state should feel obliged to do more than it feels is right. this is as valid for foreign policy as it is for economic policy. europe must stay flexible wherever possible and not be driven by rigid integrationist dogma any more, and it is time for a change in the eu integration paradigm. that is clear. that is understandable, and i hope that the european council will deal with that.
i have received one motion for resolution1 tabled in accordance with rule 110(2) of the rules of procedure. the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow (thursday, 24 march 2011). written statements (rule 149)
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, first of all, i join all those who have already expressed their sympathy to the people of japan who have been hit by the devastating earthquake and tsunami. the natural disaster has taken thousands of lives, which is a huge loss. tens of thousands more lost their homes, their belongings, their possessions, dramatically changing their lives for years to come. even though the media is focusing on nuclear power issues, let's not forget that. the problems with the damaged fukushima plant almost overshadow everything else. but i would like to remind you - in the context of the alarming scenarios - that the greatest nuclear disaster is still the atomic bombing of hiroshima and nagasaki. the greatest threat to mankind is the nuclear military arsenal. it spreads despite many good intentions without being under such permanent and careful public scrutiny as nuclear power. in addition, following the events in japan, safety requirements and standards will certainly be improved. let us therefore maintain a reasonable attitude without reacting rashly or hysterically to the events in japan. there is no alternative to nuclear power, which provides us with the much-needed, affordable, carbon-free energy. i am glad to see that the czech republic, finland, france and others are also taking a realistic approach to the issue. unlike countries where, with the situation in japan, nuclear energy is unethical, it is a card in the election campaign.
the next item is the debate on– the oral question to the council on establishment of a joint eu resettlement programme by rui tavares, on behalf of the gue/ngl group (o-000042/2011 – b7-0205/2011),– the oral question to the council on establishment of a joint eu resettlement programme for refugees by nadja hirsch, sophia in 't veld, sonia alfano, nathalie griesbeck, on behalf of the alde group (o-000045/2011 – b7-0206/2011),– the oral question to the council on establishment of a joint eu resettlement programme by manfred weber, simon busuttil, georgios papanikolaou, on behalf of the ppe group (o-000054/2011 – b7-0208/2011).
mr president, let me first of all thank and commend the work of andreas schwab for his brilliant handling of 1600 amendments and for his achievement of a sensible compromise form of report. i am pleased that it has managed to preserve the two basic principles of the commission's original proposal, namely the principle of 'better regulation' by simplifying and updating the rules in order to remove the obstacles in the internal market that businesses face on a daily basis when trading in the countries of the european union, and the second principle, which is to achieve the maximum possible harmonisation of consumer law and to remove the many exceptions applied by individual states. although we have not been able to reach agreement on full harmonisation in all chapters of the directive, this new regulation will represent a very significant step forward in removing barriers to cross-border trade and ultimately in increasing competition in the internal market.
mr. chairman, in the old days, the motto was "our customer, our master", lifetime warranties were no exception and quality was a matter of honour. clearly, the times are different today. today we must think about consumer rights, consumer protection and the legislation to ensure it. customer satisfaction no longer seems to be the best business policy, and too many examples from everyday life suggest otherwise. this is why it is so important to discuss this legislation in depth, which must not lead to a deterioration in consumer rights and must respond to new challenges. it is necessary to bear in mind the different starting positions of consumer rights in the different member states of the european union. our aim should be to improve consumer protection where there is still a legal jungle and uncertainty that attracts various dishonest practices and threatens confidence in the internal market. on the other hand, we must maintain a high level of consumer protection where it effectively exists. this is the fundamental point of view from which i view the consumer rights directive and i therefore fully support the position of my group. and of course, thank you to all the reporters for the tremendous work they've done. consumer protection is not really against the interests of honest traders. it's the opposite.
mr. chairman, i want to thank my colleague schwab for his excellent work and for supporting my proposals. the fact that consumers are now entitled to receive, under certain conditions, goods and services ordered via the internet in each member state is a major change in online commerce. a third of retailers refuse to deliver goods across borders, especially to the new member states. i hope that the council will also accept our compromise text, including my proposals, on the obligation to indicate the interoperability of computer equipment and electronics, the legibility of contract terms, the rights of consumers in organised sales tours or the possibility for member states to extend consumer rights, including guarantees, to non-profit organisations and small traders. the meaning of this directive is clear. a european consumer with clear rights is the best catalyst for fair internal trade in the european union.
in writing. the proposal for a directive on consumer rights harmonises in one legal document a total of four directives on off-premises contracts, on unfair terms in consumer contracts, on consumer protection in distance contracts and on certain aspects of the sale of consumer goods and guarantees. the aim of merging these four directives is to make the internal market more efficient and to promote crossborder trade. a high level of consumer protection improves the quality of products, increases consumer confidence and thus promotes the efficiency of the internal market. the attempt to set a guarantee period strictly at two years should be rejected, since such a provision would mean a weakening of consumer protection in many states, since member states would have to reduce the guarantee period in their national legislation, thus weakening the position of the consumer. the aim of enhancing consumer confidence in cross-border shopping is a valid one, where consumers feel that language problems, lack of trust in the payment system and reluctance of businesses to trade across borders are the biggest obstacles. the rapporteur of the economic and monetary committee is quite right in her criticism that the draft directive does not take account of new products arising from changing product developments and innovations, such as digital products. in the entertainment and consumer electronics sectors, products that include software and other intangible services are becoming more common.
in writing. for years, canada has been in the throes of a battle between conservationists and investors, who, unfortunately, have become less concerned about conservation as global prices and demand for oil rise. the multinational oil companies see environmental protection only as a cost item to be reduced. their ruthlessness will not stop even before the actual genocide of the local population, which is vitally dependent on the surrounding environment. the alarming increase in cancer among the local population is a direct result of reckless mining. we used to value everything in money. we only see short-term returns in the worldwide version of the board game monopoly, which we call economics. we don't consider what we're ultimately paying for further deforestation of the temperate belt and poisoning of entire ecosystems in a much longer and more serious game of survival. under current economic and political conditions, resources will simply be extracted and the expected environmental damage will be done. the yields are too attractive, the energy demand is too great. who decides then? citizens and their democratically elected representatives or capital and the management of oil companies? if europe does not take a firm stand on the issue of unsustainable tar sands and oil shale extraction, then we are playing political theatre to show that we care about the problem, but in reality we have no will or power to change the situation.
madam president, the 2012 budget of the european union must continue the tradition of balanced budgets. on the contrary, we must not continue the disputes we saw when the 2011 budget was approved. we must not allow a budgetary interim to develop. this would have a very negative impact on the economy of the european union. to avoid disputes and to get the budget approved early, it is necessary to draft a budget that is reasonable, which in today's economic crisis means a budget that is frugal. the public in the member states expects the european union to be well and sparingly managed in a time of spending cuts. if we don't remember this, we'll lose credibility.
in writing. in her report on the general guidelines for the 2012 budget, mrs balzani identified five key eu budgetary priorities. i fully share its view that it is the europe 2020 strategy that should help europe to overcome the global economic crisis and emerge strengthened through the creation of new jobs on the basis of the priorities mentioned, i.e. promoting employment, improving the conditions for innovation, research and development and their public funding, meeting climate change and energy objectives, raising education levels and promoting social inclusion and, above all, reducing poverty in europe. but if we are to succeed and really implement this strategy, we must allocate sufficient funds without major compromises and cuts. the current multiannual financial framework already offers very little room for manoeuvre and i therefore agree with the rapporteur that further attempts to restrict or reduce budgetary appropriations related to the implementation of the key priorities are not appropriate at present. i support the rapporteur's idea that meeting the key priorities can act as a catalyst for increased investment, growth and job creation across the union. i agree with the overall guidelines proposed and recommend that this report be approved.
in writing. − an eu-canada air transport agreement would normally have my full support, as i am a strong supporter of removing barriers to free movement both within the eu and between the eu and third countries. however, in a situation where canada still applies a unilateral visa requirement to citizens of the czech republic, i see this agreement as an inappropriate eu concession to a country that does not deserve it. the eu should have the courage to link the two issues - the visa requirement and the international agreement - because this may be one of the few ways to put effective pressure on canada on the visa issue. it is striking that canada allows eu airlines to operate direct flights to canada from anywhere in the eu on the one hand, and prevents citizens of one member state from travelling freely to canada on the other. it is generally considered that the eu-canada relationship has gradually developed into a strategic partnership and the agreement concluded confirms and reinforces the strategic nature of the partnership. however, as long as canada unilaterally applies the visa requirement to czech citizens, this partnership will have a very bitter aftertaste.
in writing. i consider the air transport agreement with canada to be a fundamental trade instrument that brings benefits to both parties, but in view of the discrimination against czech citizens and the opinion of the members of the chamber of deputies of the czech republic, i abstain. the foreign affairs committee of the chamber of deputies of the czech republic has suspended the negotiations on the approval of the ratification of the treaty until the commission has made progress in negotiations with canada on the abolition of the unilaterally introduced visa requirement for czech citizens. for almost two years, the eu has maintained a dual citizenship situation, whereby czech citizens cannot travel freely to canada like others, while canadians can travel freely to the czech republic. the czech republic cannot introduce reciprocity with regard to the eu's common visa policy, which the commissioner does not seek to ensure.
in writing. the benefit of the approved directive is that it creates a uniform procedure for processing applications by third-country nationals for authorisation to reside and work in a member state and provides third-country workers legally resident in the european union with a common set of minimum rights. unfortunately, the horizontal and framework nature of the directive is undermined by exceptions to its scope and by derogations concerning the rights of certain specific groups of the population. the principle of equal treatment for all third-country workers legally employed in the union in relation to community nationals is thus called into question. this equality should reflect the recognition of the contribution of migrant workers to the eu economy through their work and also through the payment of taxes and social contributions. its concomitant effect would be to reduce unfair competition, to make it more difficult to engage in illegal employment and to prevent workers from third countries from falling victim to exploitation and social exclusion. contrary to the commission's proposal, it is therefore necessary to ensure that no specific group, and in particular not a group of temporary staff, is excluded from the scope of the directive. directives must specify the conditions of entry into the union and, where appropriate, specific rights, but must not create an obstacle to the enjoyment by all legal migrant workers of fair and equal treatment, but should, on the contrary, ensure and guarantee such treatment.
in writing. the adopted report removes the fragmented nature of existing consumer protection rules, which in turn discourages consumers and businesses from engaging in cross-border trade. in particular, consumers often complain that they cannot fully benefit from the single market, especially when trading online. i consider that an agreed set of common definitions, such as the concept of consumer, trader or distance contract, is the right answer to these complaints. it is also beneficial to establish a list of the basic information that the trader must provide before concluding all types of consumer contracts and to harmonise the withdrawal period to 14 days for distance or off-premises contracts, including the creation of a uniform withdrawal form. unfortunately, in the case of this report, we have seen that the left did not support the compromise agreed by the rapporteur at the last minute, and that is why a forceful vote had to take place. i do not see this as a disaster, but rather as a demonstration of the fact that the ep is a political body in which there is an open and genuine ideological clash between the european right and the left. it would be useful if the ep could profile itself in this way more often.
i welcome this report from the european parliament on its own initiative and i believe that member states must introduce the legislation necessary to stop violence against women. domestic violence is a very serious form of violence against women. domestic violence is a problem that is not a private family matter. the ecr group is committed to supporting families and especially children. and that's where the problem of domestic violence comes in. domestic violence has disastrous consequences for all family members, especially for children. children who repeatedly witness domestic violence often accept violence as a normal norm of behavior. they are likely to commit these acts themselves in school or later in life. violence against women, in my view, reflects and reinforces inequalities between men and women and therefore often shapes the position of women in society. it is common for women who are subjected to domestic violence to become economically dependent and subjected to psychological pressure from the perpetrator. there is a need to give due attention to domestic violence in order to detect it and help its victims, women and children. there is therefore a need to raise awareness of the occurrence of such violence. there is a need for a community-wide discussion of the phenomenon and for prevention campaigns, as women are often reluctant to share their traumatic experiences with the public for fear of their own safety and that of their children. in this context, i also support the introduction of a european protection order, which could, among other things, help victims of domestic violence at european level if it has a solid legal basis.
mr. chairman, whenever we see examples of women and children being mutilated in the media, we wonder how it is possible that the community didn't know about it for so long. it is therefore necessary that the commission's agreed strategy to combat domestic violence includes specific diagnostic mechanisms binding on healthcare facilities, but also on social workers, which will allow for really timely assistance to these victims. violence motivated by religious rituals must be punished with high rates, which, together with education, including training of judges, will lead to a society with zero tolerance for violence against women, including female organ mutilation. the strategy should also consider free legal services and social assistance for victims of violence. i want to thank mrs svensson for her very detailed report, but i will vote against the part of recital j because it invokes the right of women to abort, and i do not agree with that.
in writing. in the past few months, this report is the third contribution to the fight against violence against women, after the european protection order and the directive on combating trafficking in human beings. violence against women is undoubtedly a serious problem throughout society. the report's figures speak of one in four women who have experienced physical violence as adults and more than ten percent of women who have been victims of sexual violence. these are alarming numbers, but they hide the true destinies of life. at the same time, only 1 in 10 victims of violent behavior seek professional help. the main reason is that most of them have no idea how to handle their situation. violence against women not only has immeasurably devastating effects on their personal lives, but also has negative consequences for those around them, especially children. i support proposals to improve awareness and education of people who may be exposed to this form of violence in the course of their work. at the same time, better care and assistance for victims must also be ensured. i welcome the report also because it needs to send a clear and strong signal to society that violence is not tolerated.
in writing. the migration report addresses how to prevent migration flows. the rapporteur believes that migration needs to be prevented by investing in the countries from which migration flows will originate. but in times of economic and financial crisis, the eu cannot massively 'finance' the third world at the expense of the european citizens' standard of living. there were, are and will be large economic differences between the eu and africa. if these differences were erased and third world citizens no longer had a reason to migrate to the eu, let us believe that we were helping to decline the standard of eu countries. let us not be under the illusion that migration is only a consequence of violations of basic human rights. a large percentage of migrants are heading to the eu simply for a 'better' life. sociological research shows that the higher the percentage of migrants in the eu, the greater the dissatisfaction of local citizens with their way of life. if the number of migrants exceeds a certain percentage, they lose their interest in being part of european society and instead create their own society within the eu. the eu should focus on human rights violations only in its migration policy. financial and economic differences between the countries of migration and the eu cannot be a reason for financial injections into these countries. migration prevention policy needs to be linked to migration prevention policy at the eu's external borders.
the european council's main theme was the economy, the stability of the euro area. you know very well that in the ecr group there are most meps from countries outside the euro area and even most meps from countries that have not even joined the euro pact. but that does not mean that we wish the eurozone to fail. on the contrary, we want a stable and prosperous european union, including the eurozone. we want responsible national governments that stop borrowing, that change the structure of public spending, that embark on fiscal reforms, and therefore take steps that lead to the restoration of financial discipline we believe is the right thing to do. what we do not think is right and what bothers us is the hidden agenda that is being pushed here by some member states, partly by the commission and some groups in this parliament. it's an attempt to use this crisis to further shift the balance of power. it is an effort to further fiscal and tax harmonisation. it is an attempt to take the next step towards creating a fiscal union and therefore the next step towards creating a full political union. we say no to such a union, we do not support such a union. i have been sitting here since 2004 and whenever there is a difficulty, i hear the same phrase: 'we need more europe, we need more community methods' and i say no, we don't need it. it is the attempt to solve everything at the level of the european union that has led us to the problems we are solving how? again, at the level of the european union. so please understand at last that the current model of european integration has run out. stop living in the 50 year old idea of post-war european integration of the last century, the european paradigm has changed and the sooner some of you come to terms with it, the better for you.
madam president, what happened at the european council meeting on 24 and 25 march 2011 and the content of the text that was adopted is a story of pushing fiscal union through the back door. the creation of the single currency, the euro, and the period of its existence is a history of ignorance of economic laws, of breaking the rules, of lying about the size of public deficits, and of cheating in national statistics. the stability and growth pact was adopted as a last chance break against the notorious black passengers and has been violated by all for years. why do we think that with the different characteristics of national economies, different rates of inflation, different rates of unemployment, that the europlus pact will be different? i consider the proposal to harmonise the corporate tax base particularly inappropriate.
in writing. − i agree that a comprehensive, long-term eu strategy for fragile states is needed to address the root causes of migration flows. however, i do not share the view that the council should present a burden-sharing action plan based on the principle of solidarity as set out in article 80 tfeu. resettlement of refugees from the region should be the responsibility of the countries to which they are going and, if the influx becomes unbearable, the response should be to tighten eu asylum and immigration policies. the challenges to the creation of a common eu asylum system and a common eu resettlement programme are, in this context, of concern to me. the creation of a comprehensive commission approach to legal migration is a good idea, but it should not be followed by considerations of taking into account the labour needs of the european labour market and the capacity of each member state to receive and integrate migrants. the management of migration flows should remain primarily the responsibility of the member states.
in writing. the report on the effectiveness of eu funding in the field of decommissioning of nuclear power plants in the new member states provides a summary of eu funding for the decommissioning and dismantling of the ignalina nuclear power plants in lithuania, bohunice in slovakia and kozloduy in bulgaria. lithuania, slovakia and bulgaria undertook, in the context of eu accession negotiations, to close and subsequently decommission the designated nuclear power plants which, according to the g7 negotiations, could not be modernised within acceptable financial costs and where minimum safety standards could not be ensured. the eu has committed to finance these closures and decommissioning and is implementing the financial framework. i welcome the eu's support for such activities. the decommissioning of old and unsafe nuclear power plants in europe is essential for the sustainable life and health of european citizens. i hope that the oldest nuclear power plants in france will be shut down and decommissioned in the same way. germany took similar action immediately after the accident in japan. i shall vote in favour of the report, as the process of closure and decommissioning of the ignalina, bohunice and kozloduy nuclear power plants needs to be completed with the corresponding financial contribution from the eu.
in writing. it is an achievement of the ep that the 2011 budget for the first time shows payment appropriations of eur 47 608 950 under the egf budget line. the egf was created as a separate specific instrument with its own objectives and deadlines and therefore deserves its own allocation, thus avoiding the transfer from other budget lines that has occurred in the past and could hinder the achievement of different policy objectives. i believe that the egf has proved its worth, i only regret that it is not yet being used more by the new member states. in the end, it may appear that the egf is intended to bring to the old eu member states resources from the eu budget that they were used to, for example, in the context of cohesion policy. i believe that the first ever czech application for an egf grant is the famous first water-skiing, which will be followed by others, because czech industry has also been affected by globalisation and the global financial crisis, and czech redundant workers deserve european help in their efforts to find a job in the labour market.
in writing. − the european globalisation adjustment fund was set up in 2006 as a flexible instrument to provide rapid, one-off, time-limited individual support to help workers made redundant as a result of globalisation or the financial and economic crisis. this is the first time that the fund has been used for the czech republic since the outbreak of the global economic and financial crisis, to alleviate the consequences of the dismissal of 634 employees of unilever, which closed its factory in nelahozeves. given that 52.4% of the redundancies are women and more than 13% of the redundancies are over 54 years old, i consider the approval of the release of appropriations for the czech republic to be very correct and that is why i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. i recommend that we vote in favour of the report by mrs barbara mater on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf), under which the first ever application for support from the czech republic has been submitted. i think it is important to stress the importance of this fund, whose cradle was laid by the czech social democrat commissioner, vladimir špidla. the financial and subsequent economic earthquake left many people out of work across europe. the egf shall finance only those measures which help people who have been made redundant, enrolled in vocational training programmes and acquired skills relevant to the labour market or who have become self-employed to find a new place on the labour market. according to the report of mrs mater, i can summarise that unilever has met all the criteria mentioned. unilever čr was forced to close its production plant in nelahozevsko under the impact of the economic crisis and the layoffs affected most of its employees. unilever offered a comprehensive job search support programme to all those leaving and worked closely with the local employment office to run the programme. i can therefore agree to the release of the funds as set out in this report and to the commission's proposal to pay the czech republic an amount of eur 323 820 from the egf reserve.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, i voted in favour of the report on the release of funds from the european globalisation adjustment fund for the benefit of the czech republic, specifically for former unilever employees, and i am glad that the report has been adopted. i regret that the british conservatives voted against and that the czech chairman of the ecr group was unable to change their decision, even though this is the first time the czech republic has used funds from this fund.
in writing. the mobilisation of the egf will support the reintegration of 460 workers affected by the closure of the unilever plant in the municipality of nelahozeves in central bohemia, czech republic. as this will be a significant help in addressing the social impact of the mass layoffs in this small community, i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. the control of trade in dual-use goods is an important area with security and trade aspects which would not be sufficiently effective and feasible without the associated flow of information exchange or flexible international cooperation itself. in this context, i would also like to emphasise the role of the relevant control authorities, such as customs, which bear considerable responsibility for the direct control of exports or transit of dual-use goods. i believe that assistance should also be given to these bodies, for example in terms of access to modern control technologies, the acquisition of new knowledge from the control procedures carried out or, last but not least, through support for vocational training and exchange placements. a sound implementation of the dual-use export regime, with full respect for international obligations, should always be accompanied by a sound security policy.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen, it is important to combat violence against women, which takes many forms and leaves its mark on the body and soul, and in some cases leads victims to commit suicide. i am glad that we are creating a new framework for the eu to fight this unacceptable phenomenon, which is why i was happy to support the report. i could not, however, vote for certain parts of the report which support the termination of a pregnancy, which i do not agree with, because even the unborn human being has the right to life.
on behalf of the ecr group. – unlike some of my colleagues, i still believe that the european union is primarily an economic project and that the single market is the core of all that. we have heard a lot about economic governance in recent days and weeks, perhaps too much for my own taste, but very little about growth and competitiveness. as you know, mr barroso, the british conservative party forms the most substantial part of my group, and the uk prime minister, mr cameron, and eight other eu heads of state and government sent a letter to you and to president van rompuy setting out very clear ways of getting europe back into growth after the financial crisis. last week, mr cameron also published a pamphlet entitled ‘let us choose growth’, which i can only encourage all members of this house to read. so what are your particular intentions in the foreseeable future to deliver on growth, on competitiveness and on completion of the single market of the european union? what practical measures do you intend to adopt in order to attain these goals?
on behalf of the ecr group. – mr barroso, i do not want to jump into some kind of controversial debate and i believe in your good intentions. however, you know the institution you are running. the european commission is sometimes a bit of a difficult animal to run. so could you please tell us also how you would guarantee that the european commission, under your presidency, would avoid the tendency – that sometimes is all too visible – of over-regulating rather than deregulating or better regulating?
madam president, i welcome this report, which seeks greater transparency in the fight against corruption and which deserves our support. as early as last autumn, at the presentation of the european court of auditors, we discussed the responsibility of the member states for the overall control of the disbursement of european union funds. i think it is right that each member state protects the financial interests of the european union by ensuring that there are reliable checks at national level to detect irregularities and fraud. in this context, cooperation between member states and the commission in providing complete and reliable information is also necessary. mandatory reporting declarations at national level shall be duly checked by the national audit authority. the convention on the protection of the european communities' financial interests is certainly a suitable and complementary instrument. however, the czech republic, which is mentioned in this report, has not yet ratified the convention, as it is necessary to introduce criminal liability for legal persons. the legal order of the czech republic is traditionally based on the criminal liability of natural persons only. but the matter is now being discussed in the czech parliament.
i have already supported the report presented in the committee on budgetary control, but i would like to draw attention to a number of important findings in the report. i would like to stress first of all the appeal contained in the report to the states which have not yet ratified the convention on the protection of the european communities' financial interests, which, apart from malta and estonia, has also not been ratified by the czech republic. unfortunately, the current czech government is doing nothing to increase the fight against fraud and corruption. on the contrary, i would like to appreciate that the report states that the czech republic, together with estonia, latvia and slovenia, had a zero fraud rate when drawing on the sapard pre-accession fund. i find it alarming that the court found that more than 30% of the errors identified by the court in the 2009 sample could have been detected and corrected by the member states before the expenditure had been confirmed to the commission, on the basis of the information available to the member states. i would like to join the call for olaf to include in its next annual report, in line with the request made in last year's report on the protection of the community's financial interests, a detailed analysis of the strategies and measures put in place by each member state to combat fraud and prevent and detect irregularities in the use of european funds, including cases where they are caused by corruption.
mr president, i would like to thank the commissioner for his 12 measures for the single market act and for having been inspired by the maria monti report, but i want to mention one name which is important here and i am sure we will agree with all the colleagues with whom we have worked on the imco committee in the context of internal market activities, and that is the name of malcolm harbour. that's a name that's certainly one of the most important for this session today. the commissioner is not offended because he sat with us in committee when malcolm harbour came up with the initiative on the internal market, he came up with an initiative which reopened the whole debate on the internal market and all the colleagues, regardless of political faction, whether they were socialists, greens, liberals, ecr as such, i say, almost all supported this idea, i.e. i want to talk about the name which is malcolm harbour. i think we have to fight economic nationalism today, and one of the enemies we have in this area is time. i am putting my hopes in commissioner barnier, in the short time available, that he will translate the 12 measures into legislative action.
mr president, if we are to revive the european economy and improve competitiveness in the global environment, our vital objective must be to finally remove, after 20 years, all the obstacles which prevent, in particular, small and medium-sized enterprises from developing their businesses. it is therefore necessary to complete the harmonization of technical and accounting standards, to promote electronic commerce, to link business registers, to introduce interoperability of online identification systems, to recognise qualifications, to eliminate discrimination between undertakings on the basis of their place of origin and to make the conditions for participation in public contracts more effective. the 50 points that the uniform poison act now contains are really crucial and we need to be able to put them into practice. different national legal regimes create bureaucratic obstacles for businesses, slow down investment, limit economies of scale and synergy benefits, but also affect areas already harmonised by directives but implemented differently in the member states, thus persisting obstacles to doing business in the single market. let us learn from this: is it better to adopt regulations that allow for uniform legal interpretation and uniform implementation in all countries, rather than limiting directives that allow for differences in national legislation? i very much appreciate that commissioner barnier has made the completion of the internal market his number one priority and he has our full support. thank you to all the colleagues who are participating and working towards this goal.
we now come to the catch-the-eye procedure. unfortunately i have about 18 names on my list, so not everybody will get the floor because we do not have enough time. however, i will try to divide that time fairly and evenly.
mr president, i am convinced that the key to a successful single market is information and public confidence. this, however, requires, inter alia, communication that is accessible and understandable to citizens. i would again call on the commission to really think about the way it explains its intentions to citizens. the public consultation of the single market document itself has shown what people want and where they see the greatest weaknesses in european integration. most people identified as a top priority proposal 48, which commits the commission to strengthening consultation and dialogue with civil society. the commission will then pay particular attention to taking consumer views into account in the draft legislation. i hope that the results of the public consultation will be taken into account in this case as well. i would like to thank and congratulate all the rapporteurs and wish you, commissioner, a lot of strength for your future work.
the debate is closed. vote: 6 april 2011. the vote on the commission statement on public procurement will take place at the next part-session. written statements (rule 149)
in writing. i welcome the ep's decision today to release funds from the eu solidarity fund for the central european countries affected by last year's floods. in the case of the czech republic, this is the amount of czk 125 million to compensate for part of the damage caused by the floods in northern moravia in may and june last year. since the total damage was estimated at czk 5 billion, representing less than 0.6% of czech gdp, the disaster was not considered by european legislation to be a 'serious natural disaster' for which assistance from the solidarity fund is virtually a matter of course. in the case of the czech republic, it was necessary to request an exemption on the grounds that our territory was affected by the same natural disaster as neighbouring poland, where its consequences were much more devastating. in the original draft resolution of the european parliament, only poland was referred to as an affected country, and it was only after i pointed out that other countries had also been affected by the floods that its scope was extended to the whole of the central european region. i am pleased that the czech government, after initial laxity and hesitation, has been able to submit an application for financial assistance from the solidarity fund within the deadline, which will no doubt come in handy now.
in writing. − unlike the european globalisation adjustment fund, i consider the solidarity fund, which is intended to use funds to cover damage caused by natural disasters, to be a true expression of european solidarity. i very much appreciate that this report proposes to allocate funds to cover flood damage to public property also in the czech republic. specifically, northern moravia, which was hit by the same flood wave last may, which then caused damage throughout the central european region. i appreciate the fact that the czech republic received funds from the solidarity fund to cover the damage, but i appreciate even more that the surrounding countries were hit by this flood wave more than the territory of the czech republic. nevertheless, in the context of european solidarity, the czech republic was not left out of the compensation. i would also like to thank my colleagues březin and tošenovski, with whom we successfully worked to include the czech republic in the group of countries compensated by the solidarity fund as a result of this flood wave. a special thanks goes to mr buzek, president of the european parliament, for visiting the affected moravian territories with us and personally supporting the czech republic.
in writing. i was very pleased to vote in favour of the proposal for a decision of the european parliament and of the council on the mobilisation of the eu solidarity fund for poland, slovakia, hungary, the czech republic, croatia and romania. all these states were hit by severe flooding last year due to torrential rains, which caused landslides and significant damage to human lives, damaged residential buildings, businesses, agricultural areas, road and rail networks and other infrastructure. although the amount of the proposed aid represents only a few per cent of the overall injury assessment for most countries, it is nonetheless significant aid, amounting to eur 182 million in total. i would remind you that the eu has set up a solidarity fund to support the people of regions affected by natural disasters. i thank my colleagues for their support of this report.
in writing. − several european countries were affected by floods in 2010 to varying degrees. for example, in the czech republic, the floods were smaller in terms of territory, but their consequences were as devastating in this territory as in neighbouring poland. i therefore supported the release of funds on the basis of the option for these exceptional situations provided for in council regulation (ec) no 2012/2002. i voted in favour of the report, including the release of funds for the czech republic and croatia, which is a similar case.
in writing. i agree that the health claims to be added to the union list of authorised claims under the commission proposal include the claim that 'reception of docosahexaenoic acid (dha) contributes to normal visual development in infants up to 12 months of age'. it is generally accepted scientific evidence that not only dha in breast milk but also synthetic dha added to infant formulae and other foods intended for infants contributes to the development of infant vision. the refusal to include dha in the union list of authorised health claims could set a dangerous precedent, whereby the work of the european food safety authority (efsa) would be frustrated simply by having any health claim on any substance that has undergone the demanding and rigorous assessment process of the authority vetoed for ideologically motivated reasons.
in writing. - dear colleague, health claims checks were introduced to ensure that consumers were not misled by false claims. each of these should serve to improve consumer awareness. i therefore voted in favour of the commission's proposal to include a health claim on dha in infant formula, which would provide positive information for mothers who cannot breastfeed their babies for health reasons. the moment these women decide which skim milk to buy, they need positive product information. we do not undermine the importance of breastfeeding for the development of the child, which every mother is well informed of by her pediatrician.
in writing. i share the rapporteur's view that the commission's report on the protection of the eu's financial interests and the fight against fraud has not provided much needed data on even estimated fraud rates and disparities in the eu member states in the management of eu funds. this is a very serious shortcoming, given the, without exaggeration, enormous scale of corruption in some member states, including the czech republic. when dealing with fraud and so-called irregularities at eu level, it is usually only a certain segment of corruption and fraud at the level of individual member states in general, but a very significant segment. in my view, it is urgent to consider whether the current level of work of the european anti-fraud office is sufficiently effective and whether this work should not be subject to structural changes and changes in its methods, including greater offensiveness.
in writing. − european political parties, which play an important role in shaping democracy in the eu, should be granted a common and uniform legal status. european political parties should be bodies with legal personality in order to be able to overcome the distinction between european political parties and european institutions in terms of tax procedure. as regards the creation of european parties, it is appropriate that the statute of european parties considers european, national and regional elected representatives to be equivalent, provided that regional representatives are composed of members elected to regional parliaments. in addition, each european political party should have at least one representative among the members of the european parliament. after all, this is also a condition for a european political party to be able to be funded by the european parliament.
in writing. − i support the final version of the report on governance and partnership in the single market, in particular its key priorities. i believe that a regular assessment of the situation in the internal market will improve its functioning. the improved functioning of the internal market is promised by broader, more interactive and more transparent public consultation of the legislation under preparation. if the member states are persuaded to publish comparative tables on single market legislation, there is a chance that the implementation gap of single market directives will be reduced to 0.5% for legislation not yet adopted and to 0.5% for incorrectly implemented legislation. however, a key prerequisite for success is a more active approach by the commission in enforcing the transposition of eu law than is currently the case, for example with the services directive in the internal market. this directive is one of the cornerstones of a functioning internal market. unfortunately, however, many states have implemented it too late and often incorrectly, in an attempt to apply the principles of economic nationalism, with the commission's tacit approval. when i voted on this report, i voted in favour of adopting it.
in writing. the single market for europeans report contains a number of positive proposals, such as promoting citizens' mobility or eliminating double taxation of european citizens, but it also contains a number of proposals whose implementation would lead to excessive regulation, which would ultimately hamper and stagnate the single market. personally, i do not support the proposals to create a more regulated and controlled single retail financial market, nor do i support the proposal to automatically extend the regulation on roaming regulation, as this regulatory measure was only temporary and was to lead to the alignment of roaming prices. regulation of final retail prices, as is the case with the regulation of roaming, is the last resort in the event of market failures. calling for politicians to automatically extend this regulation is more of a political populist gesture than a rationally justified move reflecting the changes in the telecommunications field that have taken place in the eu since 2007. the proposal to launch a european television competition 'european crossborder enterprise of the year' is only a waste of european funds in my view. that is why i decided to abstain in the vote on this report.
in writing. i consider the single market for business and growth report to be very balanced, and i believe that the implementation of the proposed measures can stimulate the growth of the single market and, ultimately, of the member states' economies. in particular, i agree with the key priorities of this report, such as the creation of an eu patent and a single dispute settlement system, the emphasis on the need for long-term investment in innovative sectors, the increase of business and consumer confidence in e-commerce and the revival of its development within the single market, the removal of bureaucratic obstacles for smes to operate across borders and the rationalisation of public procurement procedures. i am in favour of all the above measures and i am convinced of their usefulness and usefulness, so i voted in favour of this report.
mr. chairman, the disaster in japan has several possible implications. first, the energy sector should be under state control, and reliable backup sources should be at a sufficient distance from each power plant. tepco was being irresponsible. secondly, we should do everything we can to support the environment and say no to the green fear mongers who have been blackmailing europe for a long time. thirdly, we need a change of mood in our parliament and in the commission. there has long been a certain anti-civilization atmosphere in the european parliament, and we need a commission of courageous men and women who are not afraid of new solutions, who have a pioneering spirit, and not of alibistic figures who, like a certain beetle, roll a ball of small personal interests in front of them and in european positions.
mr. chairman, for several weeks now, we have been watching with excitement the incredible struggle in japan with the aftermath of a giant earthquake and subsequent tsunami. i admire the japanese, too, but i've never heard of them. people have learned from every disaster. such experience has always led to technical improvements and safety measures. after major air disasters, people didn't stop flying. reliability and safety have been improved. the disaster in japan is an important example of the need to build up the experience of nuclear power plants. this extreme natural stress test needs to be properly evaluated and thus to gain as much experience as possible for technical and safety measures. nuclear power plants must be prepared to withstand extreme catastrophic natural events. rescue procedures also need to be learned, as part of the operation and safety of the facility. politicians need to limit themselves to quick and easy trials. maximum re-inspection of nuclear power plants is certainly reasonable. the japanese experience is an opportunity to make a responsible assessment of existing safety standards, without unnecessary political history.
in writing. the lesson of the fukushima 1 nuclear accident is also that it is dangerous to put energy entirely in private hands. private capital maximizes profits even at the cost of neglecting safety measures. how many more "world economic crises", "gulf of mexico" or "fukushima" will it take for us to finally admit it? the irresponsibility of private financial investors has now brought the world into a financial crisis. because of the irresponsibility of the private oil companies, there are millions of tons of oil in the sea. as a result of the irresponsibility of private companies, japan will have to deal with radiation pollution of its land, sea and food chain for decades. nuclear energy should always be under national and international control. the planned stress tests shall have uniform standards, transparent and independent control bodies and a cross-border character. although there is a legal framework in europe that initiates continuous improvement of technologies, large-scale investments in research and training will be needed to guarantee the highest possible level of safety and health and environmental protection at the level of the latest scientific and technical knowledge. large investments will also be needed in the management of radioactive waste and spent fuel, which are currently stored under more or less provisional conditions. the lessons of this disaster must be directed towards ensuring safety, not the elimination of the only truly sustainable, climate-neutral energy source we have available.
the latest evaluation report on the fyrom is neither worse nor better than the previous ones. but we all know that the content of these reports is not the main obstacle to opening accession negotiations. there's a dispute with greece over a name. without it, the proceedings would probably have already begun. the eu should therefore play a more prominent role in tackling it. it could be decisive. after nearly 20 years of unsuccessful search for a way out, it is entirely appropriate to have a thorough debate on what to do next. including any changes to the negotiation mechanism. the commission should make appropriate proposals, including alternative ones, to this end. by our long-term passivity, we all "sign" the uncertainty of the fate of an entire country. a country that still wants to join the eu - after all these years. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' in the definition of the term 'competent authority'. this 'dialogue of the deaf' between the ep and the ec on the one hand and the council on the other does not add any weight to anyone's life. because it reduces the credibility of the eu as a whole. let us be clear - support for eu membership is still high in fyrom. but in the long run, it's declining. the patience of the people of fyrom, macedonians and albanians, may have its limits. being for each of them for different reasons.
madam president, i hope that there is no one from the eastern partnership countries here, because our participation might make them think that we are not interested in the eastern partnership. let me also stress the importance and the need for a discussion on the eastern partnership. the importance of this is that east of the european union's borders is a space for business, an opportunity that is irreplaceable for maintaining the union's competitiveness in the decades to come. it is also a huge source of culture that co-creates the european tradition and without which it is impossible to understand european identity. the need to discuss the eastern partnership is also due to the fact that it is not entirely clear whether we all attach the same importance to this project. all the multilateral initiatives linked to the eastern partnership, democracy, stability, economic integration, energy security and people-to-people contacts are undoubtedly beneficial both for the european union and for the six neighbouring countries. some politicians are embarrassed by statements about black sea synergy or a democratic sanitation cordon. at that point, the eastern partnership ceases to be an instrument... , in writing. the report on forest protection addresses the issue of deforestation. the european forestry sector is in a challenging and risky situation, with further losses due to climate change to be expected. several member states (e.g. germany, austria) have a balanced forestry policy. however, a large number of countries - especially the new member states, including the czech republic - allow unsustainable forestry on their territory, which can be defined as the absolute clearing and plundering of forest cover. the ratio of logging to forest growth is even more than 100%. if the politicians in these countries are so incapable or corrupt that they cannot or will not stop this trend, it is imperative that deforestation and forest clearance be managed and regulated at european level. the czech authorities have not provided any evidence that the forestry sector is a viable business. there is no question of conservation or sustainable forest management. it is also quite common for some citizens to cut down forest growth without a permit, because the cost of topical is worth the expense. i have to appeal to the authorities who are not punishing or even addressing this, because deforestation has become a common practice in the czech republic. i welcome the news. it is a further step towards sustainable forestry in europe and stopping deforestation in some member states.
madam president, this debate covers a very broad area, since it concerns the discharge of a number of european institutions, each with its own specificities. it is therefore very difficult to summarize all these issues in one article. the cooperation between the court of auditors and the committee on budgetary control, of which i am a member, is at a high level and has resulted in some recommendations on how the european parliament should vote in the discharge. however, there are continuing shortcomings in the evaluation of individual institutions, in particular european agencies, such as in the recruitment process, in public procurement, problems with transparency or problems with internal control. for a number of reasons, it is therefore very difficult to agree to a discharge. on the contrary, we will support reports that suggest a delay in discharge.
madam chairman, we're discussing seemingly routine matters, plus money already spent. the annual repetition of this procedure should, in particular, lead to a more careful and conscientious assessment and preparation for the future. for example, in the case of the two largest european union projects, the global navigation satellite systems and the european joint undertaking for nuclear fusion energy, iter, i find it intolerable that such important projects should be 'digging' into the shortcomings that every capable manager must be able to address. the irregularities in the involvement of smes in satellite projects, the shortcomings in recruitment and the breach of the principles of equal treatment, the iter's difficulties in complying with budgetary principles, auditing and granting rules, are just some of the errors identified. but one of the major causes of the problems is undoubtedly the lengthy decision-making process on the projects themselves. without clarity on priorities, including funding, in such key activities, half-hearted and uncomplicated decisions will continue to cause not only the problems mentioned above, but also reduce the overall quality of projects.
in writing. − i agree that the european preservation order (eopa) and the european access order (eoda) should be separate remedies, complementing the remedies available under national law, and should apply only in cross-border cases. the issuing of an eopa should be entirely at the discretion of the national court. moreover, the burden of proof should be on the applicant, who should provide appropriate evidence of the 'fumus boni juris' and 'periculum in mora' of the case. those criteria should be assessed by national courts on the basis of the existing case law of the european court of justice. the effect of an eopa order shall be limited to the freezing of bank accounts and the temporary freezing of bank deposits and should not confer on the creditor any form of ownership rights over the debtor's property. further consideration should be given to whether the order may cover other types of property such as immovable property or future property (short-term debt or inheritance).
in writing. − i am personally in favour of the european preservation order and the european asset recovery order being part of the eu legal order. this is particularly the case in view of the legislative developments in recent years, which have seen institutions such as the european enforcement order, the small claims procedure and the european payment order being introduced into the union legal order, as these institutions cannot be fully implemented without the institution of the european preservation order and the european access order. although i support the call for the commission to submit a legislative proposal from both institutions, this does not automatically mean that i support the commission's proposal. in assessing the proposal, i shall mainly focus on its specific form, with an emphasis on the exclusive use of cross-border cases, the jurisdiction for the use of these institutes as well as the limited use of these institutes only for cases of strict necessity.
in writing. the adoption of the approved regulation is necessary because the current system, which is composed of a significant number of overlapping and sometimes even contradictory bilateral investment treaties concluded by member states, needs to be replaced within a reasonable time by a new system of modern investment treaties to be concluded by the eu and which are consistent with the general objectives of its policy. a regulation is therefore the logical and only possible response to the entrenchment of foreign direct investment policy as an exclusive competence of the eu, which is part of the eu's common trade policy. its important task is to ensure a high level of legal certainty during the transitional period. i therefore wholeheartedly support the commission's proposal, which envisages the coexistence of contracts. it is necessary that, on the basis of the approval procedure, existing investment treaties of the member states can be maintained in force and that member states can negotiate amendments to them under clear conditions, complete ongoing negotiations and open negotiations on new treaties.
in writing. − as a member of the ep delegation to the eu-armenia, eu-azerbaijan and eu-georgia parliamentary cooperation committees, i welcome the decision to provide macro-financial assistance of eur 46 million to georgia to support the stabilisation of the georgian economy and cover the balance of payments needs set out in the current imf programme. i consider it appropriate that eur 23 million of this amount be provided in the form of grants and eur 23 million in the form of loans. it is important that the relevant measures to be taken by georgia to prevent and combat fraud, corruption and other irregularities in connection with this assistance are set out in the memorandum of understanding, the credit agreement and the grant agreement to be negotiated with the georgian authorities. in order to ensure greater transparency in the management and disbursement of funds, the memorandum of understanding, the loan agreement and the grant agreement should also provide for the possibility of controls, including on-the-spot checks and inspections, by the commission, including the european anti-fraud office. in addition, the possibility of audits, including on-the-spot audits where appropriate, by the court of auditors should be envisaged.

mr president, i think that in connection with the 25th anniversary of the chernobyl disaster, a message of thanks should be sent to two countries that the european parliament does not like very much. first, belarus, which was affected as much as ukraine by the consequences of this disaster and which is patiently and without any violent gestures financing the compensation for the consequences of this disaster. a lot has been done and not much attention has been paid by the european union institutions. secondly, to the government and people of the republic of cuba, which has taken in a large number of children from families affected by the chernobyl disaster for treatment. they were given the best possible treatment and the best possible care at a time when the cuban republic was doing very badly economically.
mr president, it has been one week since the very complex proposal for labelling textile products, as proposed by our committee on the internal market and consumer protection, was voted on by a large majority in the first reading. i very much appreciate the work of rapporteur toine mander and others who negotiated with the council until the last minute a compromise at second reading which would allow for the faster introduction of new fibres into european production and the improvement of product safety. i must, however, express my regret that the council is so obstinately opposed to the introduction of country of origin labelling and the harmonisation of size labelling. i am very disappointed in the council's approach to denying consumers the right to know where a substantial part of a product was made before they buy it. it's not like it's a secret. this will enhance the promotion of the quality of european brands and rightly make citizens proud of what europeans can still produce in the face of competition from cheap labour. even dangerous products, which are still very common despite improved controls, are easier to avoid if shoppers know where the goods were produced. this is not protectionism at all. i am glad that at least the commission is prepared to present an impact assessment by september 2013 which will focus on harmonised product treatment, size labelling, information on hazardous substances, allergens, flammability, etc. in addition to the country of origin. the council, which has been more protective of the interests of traders importing textiles from third countries than of european producers and consumers, is now in the stars. but it is also up to us to convince ourselves, not just here in brussels, but our governments at home, if we are to achieve these goals. in closing, i would like to thank once again toine manders and all the others and the commissioner for today's result.
