, in writing. the draft resolution makes it clear that the implementation of the relevant provisions of the treaty of lisbon still faces serious shortcomings. it is not a good sign for the member states when the ep 'urgently requests' the european council to draw up a 'european foreign policy strategy' or when it calls on the european council to approach this task on the basis of a political dialogue with the ep. if there were a european foreign policy strategy today, the eu's record would be much better in this respect, for example with regard to the shifts in the arab world and the double or even triple standard applied to countries in the region. another problem is the eu's attitude to the increasingly turbulent developments in the balkans. the eu has not yet found the 'right key' for this region, or a system approach is lacking. the result is, among other things, a free fall in the eu's prestige and the attractiveness of eu membership in the western balkan countries. the reflections of the high representative of the international community and the european union special envoy for bosnia and herzegovina, valentin inzko, on the course of action towards the republic of serbia do not address this situation, but rather the opposite.
, in writing. the common foreign and security policy remains a major issue within the eu. on the one hand, the great powers have traditionally relied on their own strength, and france and britain in particular still feel like great powers. this is also reflected in their approach to foreign policy issues. the latter, especially in recent times, has focused more on military solutions to any disputes, and has neglected peaceful solutions to disputes in favor of intervention. i believe that the world today is moving towards multipolarity and that a peaceful foreign policy is in line with international law and the un charter. the world today needs, instead of military adventures, the latest of which is unfolding in libya, to put all its efforts into fighting poverty, illiteracy and other serious problems. to this end, arms control and disarmament, including a general agreement on the elimination of nuclear weapons, are absolutely essential. disaster relief should be an important component of eu foreign policy. however, these operations should not be linked to the deployment of the military. police deployment is only possible as a last resort under a un resolution, to protect workers in the social, health and public welfare sectors and to train police. the cfsp must be strictly separated from nato. for these reasons, the gue/ngl refuses to support this report.
in writing. i supported the report because it is a step towards better consumer protection with an emphasis on the quality of textile products. it is, however, regrettable that the requirement to indicate the country of origin has not been enforced, which is one of the elements of information that complement consumer choice and can help to reduce the occurrence of various inaccurate and misleading claims of origin. the absence of harmonised rules in this area also deprives european producers of the benefits of eu production. i trust that the commission will continue to seek an acceptable solution to this matter.
in writing. the digital agenda targets of broadband coverage for all european citizens by 2013 and high-speed coverage of 30 mbps or more (up to 100 mbps for half of european households) by 2020 should be considered as minimal and ambition should be to achieve the best capacity and the highest broadband speeds in the world in europe. in this context, the provision of wireless broadband is essential to ensure that all citizens have access to new and innovative services. i fully agree with the deadline proposed by the commission for making the 800 mhz band available for electronic communications services by 1 january 2013. any exemptions or derogations should be granted only on technical grounds, but must take into account specific public security and defence needs. the other zones should be liberated by 1 january 2012 as set out in the commission proposal. i believe that we should strive to allocate more frequencies to mobile services, with a minimum target of 1 200 mhz. it is necessary to establish appropriate principles to allow for the release of additional spectrum bands in the future. i also think it is appropriate to address other issues of concern, including in particular the cost of liberalising the belt.
in writing. the proposed amendment to the rules of procedure responds to the corruption revelations in recent months, when 3 meps responded positively to a corruption bid by investigative journalists. it is unacceptable that members should not be subject to public scrutiny as to whose interests they are defending in their capacity, or what pressure they are subject to or who is putting pressure on them. i therefore very much welcome the introduction of a common register of lobbyists and lobbying organisations. the european commission has already established this register in the past, and therefore the european parliament will only extend its scope to members, officials and other staff of the ep. the register will contain information on contacts between the above-mentioned persons and the lobbyists and will be publicly accessible. a similar system already exists in the us congress, and experience there has shown that it is an appropriate anti-corruption measure. i firmly believe that the extension of the register will be the right inspiration for the czech parliament as well. its introduction in czech conditions would certainly contribute to the cultivation of czech politics.
in writing. i am concerned that more than a year after the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, there are still no clear signs of a comprehensive eu approach to foreign and security policy, one that would overcome the traditional procedural and institutional obstacles to eu accessibility. i regret the reluctance of the eu member states to define a common position on the crisis in libya, on un security council resolution 1973 and on the ways of implementing it. i consider it dangerous to consider the creation of ad hoc coalitions of countries as possible substitutes for a common security and defence policy, because in the 21st century no european state has a real chance of being a major player in the field of security and defence. in this context, i would like to recall that the treaty of lisbon provides for the possibility of entrusting the implementation of crisis management operations to a group of member states, but only within the framework of a council decision defining the objectives, scope and conditions of their implementation. a common response to developments in libya is essential to create a new credible approach to the neighbourhood policy with the southern countries. the same emphasis should be placed on the development and deepening of the eastern partnership, which has recently been joined by a parliamentary assembly of meps and members of the participating states. in this context, we should maintain a firm and principled stance against the regime of president lukashenko of belarus.
mr president, today we are here for the second time to discuss the innovation strategy in plenary. in many of our discussions on the position of european countries on world markets, we regularly draw attention to the need to increase the competitiveness of the european economy. the position of the european union on world markets will continue to have a major impact on the success of innovation and the speed of innovation cycles. european countries are certainly at a high level in research projects. but what's a long-term problem is the slow implementation of this knowledge in practice with complicated bureaucracy. the eu's science and research fund is one of the largest. our aim must therefore be to simplify the administrative transfer of research results as much as possible, thereby helping innovation and thus strengthening the european union's competitiveness. we need to think about how to deal with the commercialisation of research results in the context of innovation steps. if we make it difficult for european research to be commercially viable, we will have a hard time speeding up innovation cycles.
mr president, europe and innovation. my colleague migalski and my colleague toschenovsky talked a little bit about this. where are we, anyway? the united states is ahead, japan is ahead, brazil, india and china are catching up. i see the innovation union program as the one that comes in at minute twelve. most likely, we have a last chance to get involved in innovation and not let these countries catch up with us. any failure of europe as a continent will have fatal consequences in this area - and all the other colleagues have said this - for europe's competitiveness, for the quality of life of its citizens and, ultimately, for the values that europe represents for the world. i am therefore pleased that the european parliament has managed to launch concrete legislative measures such as enhanced cooperation on the introduction of the european patent. i also see the area of standardisation as very important in the implementation of innovation and its application by smes.
, in writing. according to the ecdc, 400 000 patients in europe are suffering from infections with multidrug-resistant strains of bacteria. i therefore welcome the resolution of the committee on agriculture and rural development in which i support in particular the requirement for the proper collection and analysis of comparable data on the sale of veterinary medicinal products and the subsequent use of these products in animals. the collection of these data should be mandatory for all eu member states. if the european union adopts strict measures on the use of antibiotics in farm animals, legislation in third countries can also be expected to change, mainly because of the economic losses that would be the limitation of meat exports to europe. for example, in the us, 74% of all antibiotics used are applied in the livestock sector to prevent disease or promote the growth of healthy animals. half of all meat products tested in u.s. stores contain dangerous bacteria (mrsa). this problem has been known in the us since 2008 and has not been solved in a major way. non-pathogenic multiresistant strains of bacteria are also at risk due to the possibility of transgenic transmission and, according to recent research, also the sharing of resistance. the emergence of new multidrug-resistant strains of bacteria may deprive the next generation of the ability to use antibiotics effectively. such a scenario would have catastrophic consequences for the health of the population. antibiotic resistance is a very serious problem that needs to be addressed immediately and effectively.
the draftsman of the committee on regional development. - mr president, the position of the cultural and creative industries is part of the european innovation strategy and the eu 2020 strategy and is closely linked to measures aimed at making more effective use of heritage sites, museum and gallery collections or urban and landscape features in tourism and related activities. the main opportunity in this respect is provided by the digitisation of historical and cultural content. digitalisation should increase the access of the general public to materials necessary for study, learning and education and for the strengthening of cultural identity. investment in these areas has a near-certain return, not only in tourism, which is certainly a significant part of many regional economies, but also in foreign policy. it is worthwhile to raise awareness of the deep cultural traditions of european countries and at the same time promote individual towns, cities, regions, member states and the european union itself in a global context. in the new programming period after 2013, we should therefore continue to finance cultural projects from the european structural funds, strengthen the twinning programme for cooperation between local authorities and maintain the autonomy of the community programme for projects in this field called culture.
mr president, the report on early learning is not, and cannot be, an attempt to formulate a uniform approach to this highly individual matter. however, i see it as an important contribution to the discussion on common european objectives and values in early childhood education. research in this area highlights the important role that early childhood care plays in the healthy development of the child and in the child's further involvement in society. it is shown that early childhood education has a positive impact on children's further education and that investment in early education in the broadest sense is much more effective than any later intervention. it is therefore in our interest to promote this type of education, also with a view to achieving the objectives of the eu 2020 strategy, namely to increase the number of people with a high-quality higher education education and at the same time to reduce the rate of under-education. quality early childhood education can bring us much closer to these goals. more than we're willing to admit, perhaps.
, in writing. this own-initiative report addresses the issue of student mobility of young people, which is based on the strategy 2020. it is certainly right to strengthen the competitiveness of the younger generation by creating conditions within higher education for the completion of at least one year in another member state. mobility, the credit system and other measures will prevent the rising unemployment rate among the young generation, which now stands at an alarming eu average of 21%. i personally strongly support the call on the commission and the member states to introduce into legislation measures to recognise voluntary work by the unemployed as time worked. this measure will lead to the consolidation of work habits, increase competitiveness and increase motivation to seek work, especially among the younger generation. i consider it equally important to link labour market requirements to vocational training on a permanent basis, and to be very flexible in this respect, in order to ensure that future graduates have the best possible access to the labour market and to reduce gradually unemployment among the younger generation.
in writing. the early years of children deserve great attention and careful approach from the outside world. it is important to keep in mind that this is a unique and unrepeatable period of development during which an individual's personality is formed. it should be pursued with more intensity and should never be underestimated or neglected in time. adults should be role models to help children develop different types of skills and abilities. it's a period of personality development where the learning process is, so to speak, nonviolent, natural, and the world around you is perceived, accepted and absorbed to the fullest extent possible. although a number of factors, both genetic and environmental, enter and contribute to a child's development, the influence of parents and the immediate environment is the most important factor in shaping a child's personality. i would also like to remind and stress that adequate and well-rounded physical activity targeted at this age group should not be overlooked in this context, as it could help the individual's development and contribute to better health in the long term.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution because i welcome the single market act and in particular the legislative measures that allow small and medium-sized enterprises (smes) to benefit from all the benefits of the single market, such as the european rules on venture capital funds, the revised vat rules and the simplification of the accounting directives. at the same time, i believe that dialogue between smes and contracting authorities should be strengthened in order to facilitate the participation of smes in procurement procedures. in this context, i would consider it appropriate to explore possibilities to help smes form partnerships and consortia and participate together in public procurement. the commission should carry out an impact assessment and consider increasing the ceilings for eu public procurement, which would allow smes to participate in contracts that would otherwise be subject to specific requirements and therefore not be able to participate. i also invite the commission to consider how the publication of all public procurement notices in europe could be improved and the administrative burden that prevents european companies from participating in cross-border public procurement contracts removed. the member states are then tasked with applying the european code of good practice to facilitate access to public procurement by smes more systematically.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the resolution on the innovation union because, among other things, i believe that action needs to be taken at european and national level to improve the entrepreneurial skills and training of europeans by integrating entrepreneurship, creativity and innovation into all areas of education. the commission's programmes such as 'erasmus for young entrepreneurs' need to be used more, while ensuring that craft trades are protected as sources of innovation. i believe that the commission should work more closely with the member states to develop medium and long-term forecasts of the skills required by the labour market and to promote partnerships between universities and the business sector in order to facilitate the integration of young people into the labour market and to help create an innovative knowledge-based society, develop applied research and improve the prospects of graduates on the labour market. in this context, i support the committee of the regions' proposal to create a 'virtual creative network' open to all (business, local and regional authorities, private sector and citizens) providing advice, support and access to risk capital and technical services. i also draw attention to the current lack of better and proactive interaction between universities and businesses and call on the commission to launch a new europe-wide programme for the training and education of university managers and technology officials.
in writing. the report on unlocking the potential of the cultural and creative sectors is closely linked in many respects to the innovation union report. i would like to draw particular attention to the importance attached to the education of citizens, including a better understanding of the issue and respect for the protection of intellectual property rights. this widely neglected area of not only education but also education needs to be strongly supported and developed both at eu level and in the member states. i have reservations about the common labelling of cultural works as goods and services. this is misleading and can be seen as an expression of economic neoliberalism in the cultural sphere. for example, a literary work, but also a work of art or music, is first and foremost an essential expression of the creator's talent and social reflection. to view them as merely goods or services is not only uncivilized but also wrong.

the author. - mr president, in evaluating, we must not forget that just as we seek to assess the situation in our own countries objectively, our partners or neighbours deserve exactly the same approach. as a doctor, i want to remind you that violence is violence, no matter where it happens. it's against our shared values. we should seek to ensure that those currently in prison are provided with adequate health and dignity treatment, wherever they are in the world. the newly established euronest assembly should serve as an instrument for exchange and cooperation. in the case of belarus, the measures taken make it impossible to fulfil this objective in full. the overall outcome of the decision is a breach of the prague declaration and a slowdown in the possible positive changes in mutual cooperation. our efforts are directed towards creating a united europe, which young people in particular deserve, including in belarus. therefore, we should reconsider our position on whether restrictions are the right tool to be seen by civil society in belarus as genuine friends. in the context of the 25th anniversary of the chernobyl disaster, i would like to ask you to try to look at belarus as a country that has suffered so much, through a different lens. this disaster has affected the belarusian people first and foremost, and i consider the fact that belarusians are prevented from participating in official religious ceremonies to be an insult to this nation. one more note to my colleague tannock. i know it's hard to speak english fast. i like english as the language of shakespeare. next time, try speaking quickly in the language of johann von goethe, perhaps we'll understand you better. i say it as a czech.

i adjourn the session of the european parliament. (the sitting was closed at 4.40 p.m.)
i welcome the proposal that research supported by the 7th framework programme should address eu problems in the areas set out in the chapter cooperation fp7. this includes, in particular, healthcare, including clinical and preventive research and medical technology. cancer is the second most common cause of death in europe. with an aging population, it is expected that by the age of 75, one in three men and one in four women will have had direct experience of the disease. cancer research is on the verge of breakthrough discoveries, especially in the area of more accurate, cheaper, and even patient-friendly methods of both prevention and treatment. cancer research funds can save lives and treatment costs. i appreciate the political will that has been shown to justify the funds that have already been allocated to cancer research under this programme. in the current economic situation in the eu, these resources must not be reduced, but we must go further. i'm saying that if we can work together to accomplish ambitious projects like tearing down national borders or building space stations, we should find a way to make cancer a disease that no one fears anymore. to do this, we need to support and coordinate research into both treatments and prevention. the fight against cancer is a european issue that should be our priority, starting with fp7 funds and ending with the european umbrella agency.
commissioner, i am in fact supporting certain reservations of my colleagues from the inta committee and the question is whether to deal with this in the framework of this agreement or, as my colleague mr rübig says, in other agreements. but i want to draw attention to a serious problem here, and that is the ratification of this agreement. for example, the ratification of the transport agreement has already been blocked in the czech parliament. the reason is the ongoing disagreement with canada on the abolition of the visa requirement for czech citizens. this is a very serious problem and unless the commission is able to deal with it properly, there is a risk that ratification of this agreement will be blocked. please take this seriously.
mr president, i am presenting a report on the external dimension of the european union and corporate social responsibility. i have drawn up this report with a very critical eye, because there is a very high degree of hypocrisy in dealing with countries outside the european union. if we want to require them to respect what we respect within the european union, then we must first of all behave in the same way. it is unacceptable that, while demanding respect for the basic conventions of the international labour organisation, some member states should be found to be in breach of those conventions. the report also states... (the president cut off the speaker)
the rapporteur. mr president, as i said in my speech a moment ago, the external dimension of social policy includes the european union's activities and initiatives aimed at promoting labour and social standards in third countries. although the lisbon strategy and the eu2020 initiative place much more emphasis on social policy than ever before, competitiveness and economic considerations remain at the forefront of the agenda. that's why i wrote the report very critically. if we want the third countries with which we conclude agreements to respect what is the principle and what is the very structure of the european social model, then we must respect that model within the european union. if we want the countries with which we conclude international agreements to respect the fundamental conventions of the international labour organisation, then we must respect them ourselves. and that there are violations of the ilo conventions in some member countries is easy to see. as for corporate social responsibility: corporate social responsibility is undoubtedly a good idea, and it started to be talked about sometime in the early 1970s. the problem is that corporate social responsibility cannot be enforced. when we discussed the comments and amendments to this report, we also heard the opinion that a directive should be drawn up because some companies simply do not behave in a socially responsible manner. more than 150 amendments have been tabled to this report, but i think it has retained its critical tone. the european union must give more support to the international labour organisation, because it is absurd that, while we are expressing support for it, fewer and fewer countries are ratifying and respecting the conventions. in fact, during the last parliamentary term, the commission put forward proposals such as the working time directive, which was in direct contradiction with the international labour organisation's convention no 1 on working time, adopted in 1919, which established a 48-hour working day. i am very pleased that parliament has rejected the directive. so much for my explanatory statement. it is useless for me to speak any longer, because this is a matter that everyone understands. i would like to see respect for social rights and respect for what we proclaim as the great achievement of the european union come first.
commissioner, a voluntary contracting institution, even if it brings legal problems in its implementation, is a way of eliminating the different contract law that contributes to the high 60% of cross-border consumer order failures. unfortunately, there are many other pitfalls for entrepreneurs. not only contract law but also consumer law is not fully harmonised. there are significant differences in tax regimes and accounting standards. payment is often burdened by extra bank charges and cross-border use of digital content is often blocked by licensing reasons. in addition, in 10 countries, consumers cannot obtain half of the 100 tested products on the domestic market, while in 13 countries, consumers found prices in cross-border offers at least 10% lower than in their own country. i therefore support the publication of standardised voluntary contracts translated into all languages, which will be legally binding, but i call on the commission to tackle other pressing issues more actively, so as to eliminate the fragmentation of the internal market.
cooperation in the field of vocational training at european level is also essential for the functioning of the single european internal market. quality vocational education and training must respond to the needs of the labour market and create the conditions for worker mobility. highly qualified and educated people are a competitive advantage for the union and support for vocational training should be matched. we need to focus on close links with practice and also to allow quality training placements, including overseas, in european mobility programmes. at european level, there is a need for greater synergy between the education systems in the member states and, consequently, for the mutual recognition of professional qualifications. the report contains these aspects and as rapporteur for the committee on the internal market and consumer protection i am satisfied with the final version.
for the group gue/ngl. mr president, allow me to thank the rapporteur for completing a very difficult task. the 'eurovision' is one of the directives designed to allow for a gradual alignment of conditions between road and rail transport. for many years, rail transport in the european union has been disadvantaged by the fact that the user of the transport route pays for this use. in the case of the road network, payments for the use of infrastructure are for the time being rather symbolic. if we look at the matter purely from a technical point of view, apart from any other context, we find that the high-class roads, i.e. motorways, expressways, first-class roads, are best adapted for heavy-duty truck traffic. the directive does not go so far as to set maximum tolls for the lowest classes of roads, i.e. local roads, in accordance with the logic indicated above. on the contrary, it focuses only on charging for certain external costs, on the basis of the 'polluter pays' principle, only for the highest class roads, i.e. motorways, expressways, first class roads and parallel roads. the payment should limit the exceptions as much as possible and should cover the so-called external costs of road transport: noise and, above all, emissions. i am sincerely curious to see how the individual countries will justify their reluctance to introduce payments, at least according to table 1, which is a poor reflection of what the real environmentalists want. despite the contradictions between the logic and the wording of the recommendation discussed here, the gue/ngl group supports this view of the rapporteur. (the president agreed to answer the question put to him by mr leichtfried by raising his blue card, pursuant to rule 149 (8) of the rules of procedure.)
i think this is just the first step towards leveling the playing field, because if i pay for infrastructure on the railway significantly... (speaker continues in german) it is the first step towards the alignment of railway conditions with road conditions. today, we pay a lot for the railway, but very little for the road. also, this is a small step towards the angleichung der bedingungen zwischen eisenbahn und straße.
mr president, in the directive under discussion, the commission has proposed the possibility of charging external costs such as air pollution, noise, congestion, based on the desire to internalize external costs according to the 'polluter pays' principle. i support this principle because i consider it important in terms of environmental and human health impacts and the use of multiple modes of transport at national, regional and local levels. this is also in the interest of municipalities, which are often affected by the negative effects, in particular transit traffic. at the same time, in view of the deepening economic crisis and the increasing financial burden on carriers, i believe that member states should be given the possibility to spread the introduction of external costs over time and by type. the new system should also allow for the replacement of an outdated fleet with lower external cost rates for investments in green vehicles.
rapporteur. − mr president, just briefly, i was very honoured to be a rapporteur when we had the chance to repeal, instead of adopting, a piece of legislation. this is a redundant piece of legislation. i think it fits perfectly with our long-term goal, which is to legislate and to regulate better, so i can only recommend all of you to support this repeal. i would like to thank you for that in advance.
in writing. - the area of the drug problem itself that we are now talking about is a contemporary and at the same time a very historical problem. the illegal drug trade goes beyond and has always been beyond the borders of one country. i believe that the establishment of high-quality international cooperation in the fight against illicit drug trafficking, not only at european level but above all at global level, could be one way of improving the effectiveness of the fight against this kind of trafficking. as a physician, i also state that drug abuse can have the most serious and irreversible consequences for human health. i therefore support effective international cooperation in the fight against illicit trafficking in drugs or their precursors, while recalling the need for prevention.
in writing. the global navigation satellite system (gnss) action plan outlines the steps that are necessary to ensure that gnss passes the critical point that will guarantee the success of the project. it is important that the plan does not get bogged down in a glut of initiatives and is not limited to consultations and is actually implemented by 2013. egnos (european geostationary navigation overlay service) has to cover the whole of the european union and should be extended to northern, eastern and south-eastern europe. in order to be fully effective for transport purposes, it must not only cover the whole union but also our nearest neighbours. in the field of civil aviation, the development and use of the egnos application for landing manoeuvres should be supported. this is a strategic prerequisite for the effective creation of a single european sky. the field of safety, environment and smoother road transport also represents a significant potential for egnos and gnss, as they can be used for toll collection.
in writing. i endorsed the report. this is a compromise based on the 'polluter pays' principle, i.e. the principle of charging for certain external costs of transport, such as environmental pollution, noise and congestion. the realisation of external transport costs is a step in the right direction.
for the ecr group. mr president, as you know, my group is made up mainly of non-euro area members, but as i have said several times here, we certainly do not want the euro to fail. our economies are too interconnected for that, and so i have to say that our feelings about what's happening are mixed. we're getting mixed signals here. the european council says one thing, the european central bank says another, the international monetary fund says another. and i think that political views still prevail over economic ones. as if it were possible to fool the markets with some promises and avoid a real solution to the situation. i want to ask, as far as the question of restructuring the national debt is concerned, what is the european commission's position? do you really still prefer to pour more and more money and give more and more endless loans to economies that are in serious trouble and on the verge of collapse, or is there already a sense of common sense here and a serious consideration of the possibility of restructuring their national debt, because in my opinion we are not going to avoid this in the long run anyway.
mr president, in the last few months we have been preparing the multiannual financial framework for the years 2014 to 2020. we are like the dog and the cat in the fairy tale by the famous czech writer karel čapek. they decided to make a cake, and to make it really tasty, they put everything they liked in the pot. the result was a cake that made their stomachs ache. i fear that the same will happen with our budgetary framework. we also put a lot of ingredients in the pot and sound commitments and recommendations, but we're not at all sure that it's going to work together. we all know how badly the lisbon strategy has gone. now we have a 2020 strategy and we're trying to make a budgetary framework for it. the problem is both in the objectives set and in the instruments recommended by the report. i consider the worst of the objectives to be the fight against climate change, and i see a major problem with the instruments in terms of so-called own resources and the possibility of issuing bonds. the own resources are in fact a new european tax, and i do not want that. eurobonds are then a way of putting the european union into debt in addition to the member states, which is something i do not want. for these reasons, i cannot support the report.
in writing. the ongoing debate on the next programming period shows that this time there will be a need to focus more on a more comprehensive approach to this main economic instrument of eu policies. the global financial crisis, the acute global competitive pressures from the economic tigers of china and india show us that the mff will need to be aligned with the fundamental objectives of agenda 2020. the rapporteur's idea of merging all the policies relating to agenda 2020 into one area of the mff seems particularly bold. the rapporteur is right to propose that it should be legally binding to review the limits within each constituency at the mid-point of the programming period. the idea of aligning the duration of the mff with the political cycles of the eu institutions, thus increasing democratic control and accountability, is also of interest. in principle, the rapporteur is undoubtedly right to suggest that the eu's revenue reform should be considered. the gradual transition to the eu's own resources system, i.e. the replacement of the existing system of member states' contributions, is entirely appropriate, as it tends to compare the balance between the eu and the member states, which leads to erroneous conclusions in the general public about the meaning of membership of the european union, which is, however, a completely wrong conclusion given the current interconnectedness of economies and policies.
in writing. meps have in the past had only limited influence on the negotiations on the union's multiannual financial framework. the fundamental change brought about by the lisbon treaty concerns precisely this long-term budgetary programming, which must be re-established by the council after obtaining the consent of the european parliament. this is why the sure special committee was set up, whose report defines the political priorities of the european parliament before the start of negotiations on the next multiannual financial framework after 2013. the report by mr salvador garriga polled makes it clear that if the union is to continue to focus on meeting the ambitious targets of the europe 2020 strategy, it will be necessary to ensure adequate funding for eu policies. without sufficient new resources beyond 2013, the union will not be able to meet both the current political priorities and the new tasks and objectives of the europe 2020 strategy. in view of the lack of traditional eu own resources, a technical discussion on new own resources for the eu budget was launched in the sure committee. the next financial framework is a great opportunity to show european citizens that the union is able to think and act in their long-term interests and deliver efficiently in terms of internal cohesion and solid growth. i therefore fully agree with the rapporteur's priority statement that the amounts allocated to the cap and cohesion policy in the next period must not be lower than under the current framework.
in writing. although europe continues to lag behind the us and lose the leadership it once had with emerging economies, the results of the 7th framework programme demonstrate the added value of this european programme for research and development in europe. unfortunately, there is still no method to assess how far the projects funded by the 7th framework programme have advanced scientific knowledge. it should also be noted that there is a considerable imbalance in the participation of entities from the old and new member states. the participation rate of smes in particular also remains relatively low. simplification of administrative and financial rules could improve the situation. the importance of joint technology initiatives (jti) for the competitiveness of european industry should be emphasised. at the same time, it is necessary to point out the legal and administrative obstacles (legal personality, financial rules and sometimes intellectual property) that can discourage participation by many key research actors and smes. the situation in which research funding in europe is too fragmented, with multiple sources of funding from member states and the eu using different priorities, evaluation criteria, definitions and procedures, leading inevitably to overlap, confusion and errors, needs to be changed.
in writing. − the recent financial crisis has highlighted three key problems in the rating agency sector, namely the lack of effective competition, the over-reliance of the regulatory framework on external rating assessments and the lack of guarantees provided by rating agencies for their ratings. i am not sure that the rapporteur's proposed establishment of a european rating agency (era) is the appropriate solution. there is no guarantee that the era will establish a good name and become a genuine new player among rating agencies, that it will be credible, that it will be able to convince the market of its absolute independence from any public authority, be it the member states, the european commission or any other public body. the rapporteur's proposal to establish a network of european credit rating agencies seems to me more meaningful, as the cooperation of nationally active credit rating agencies, using the available human and financial resources, could increase competition in the sector by covering a wide range of assets and different markets, allowing them to reach the same level as large credit rating agencies with a global reach.
in writing. the report deals with a subject of great importance in today's world. it's a workers' rights issue. the so-called social dumping is a great danger to workers, because it undermines their rights in a fundamental way. the main actors in the application of social standards are international companies. it is therefore essential to define csr clearly and unambiguously in order to avoid current divergent interpretations and to set out minimum obligations in this area and how they will be monitored. there are many companies that come to the regions for a temporary economic advantage, mostly because of cheap labor. as a rule, businesses refuse to form long-term links in the region, i.e. to participate in the shared social responsibility of the region. the report calls for strengthening the core labour standards enshrined in the ilo conventions, including freedom of association and collective bargaining, the elimination of forced and compulsory labour, the elimination of discrimination in employment and the abolition of child labour. it calls for changes in the global governance system and in the eu to better protect workers' rights and working conditions. the rapporteur succeeded in highlighting the clear social dimension of the report presented, which is why i supported the report.
in writing. the intention and much of the content of mr falbra's report is, in my view, very positive. i also see the focus on employment and decent working conditions as a key element of sustainable development not only in eu countries. like the rapporteur, i regret that there is no uniform definition of a 'social clause' which would be applicable not in bilateral trade agreements but in the creation of decent working conditions for employees. the social models of some european countries can also serve as a good example for developing countries. i also share the view that we should fight together to eliminate child labor. despite the above, i abstained from the vote because social requirements cannot be implemented in bilateral trade agreements in the absence of a clear definition of respect for human rights. this criterion cannot be used as an argument against the conclusion of agreements.
in writing. − there is no doubt that education is a complex, long-term process, the quality of which, and in particular the results, ultimately influence the future employment of young people. it should not be neglected as such. in this context, i would like to point out that the development of new modern technologies, the setting of higher quality or safety standards, the demands on professional competence in a wide range of fields and activities, and thus the requirements for more targeted training of young people, are increasing. but i am talking about quality education based on a concept with modern teaching elements, education that is attractive in a certain way and that shows the need for the given field of education with a view to further opportunities for upgrading qualifications. the result of the training process should be a professionally prepared young person who has a vision of prospective growth and, above all, who uses his acquired knowledge, skills and abilities in the field of activity for which he has been preparing and has acquired the qualification he controls and for which he is available.
in writing. the impact assessment process contributes significantly to reducing the bureaucratic burden that is a by-product of legal regulation. a special impact assessment board has been established at commission level for some time, assessing both ex ante and ex post impacts. the commission also conducts public consultations on its legislative intentions. the european parliament has yet to have a working group to assess the impact. i therefore appreciate the fact that the report calls for the creation of an infrastructure to enable impact assessments of proposals and intentions formulated on parliament's premises. i would also like to call on the commission to focus more on transparency of the whole evaluation process when carrying out impact assessments, to strive more for its independence, to not focus its proposals on one solution to a problem but to independently assess multiple solutions to a specific situation. i would also like to call for the simplification of the public consultation process, the extension of the deadlines for submitting specific proposals, including the possibility of submitting proposals in all the official languages of the union.
in writing. the impact assessment tool proposed by the rapporteur is a good tool for improving the quality of legislation at european level. however, in our mutual assessment we should not only consider the financial impact, but we must also focus on other aspects such as economic, environmental, social aspects. as a doctor, i emphasize those that have an impact on people's health. we must also not forget the very important link in the impact assessment with the member states, which have to incorporate the proposed legislation into their legal systems.
in writing. i do not support the proposal to create an entirely new legal instrument, which should be european contract law. the creation of this instrument is the first step towards the creation of a pan-european civil code, which should gradually replace the contract law of the individual member states. although the proposed concept refers to the voluntary application of this instrument, there are many indications that this voluntary application is only a kind of prelude to the subsequent mandatory application in cross-border commitments. if this instrument is created, we will introduce a completely new type of transnational law, which will be at the borderline between eu law and international private and commercial law. not to mention that the potential mandate to create a european contract law for consumers and businesses lacks sufficient support in eu primary law. therefore, we should stick to the standard instrument for which there is sufficient legal support in primary law, and that is harmonisation.
in writing. − i fully support the autonomous european global satellite navigation system and agree to take all necessary measures to ensure its full operational capacity. this is the view of all the groups in the european parliament, as shown by the fact that no amendments were tabled to the report. in the report by mr remek, we call on the european commission to address the problems that have arisen and to ensure that the funding of the programme is carried out in such a way that full operational capacity can be achieved in 2018. the launch cannot be delayed in order to preserve the eu's competitive advantage, or other countries like china, india and japan will overtake us.
the debate is closed. written statements (rule 149)

for the group gue/ngl mr. chairman, mr. commissioner, i'm looking in vain for an answer to the question of why we are debating the resolution for the summit with russia on the eve and voting on it on the day of the summit in nizhny novgorod. moreover, as recently as the beginning of last week, this subject was not on the agenda of the plenary. it all sounds inconceivable, and from my perspective, it's a formality today. moreover, instead of pursuing a principled approach to a new treaty on relations with russia, which we consider an important partner, we are notoriously repeating proclamations and demands that clearly have no hope of resolution. we should focus on the issues that are moving forward. the progress in the agreements on the easing of the visa regime and, eventually, the possible abolition of the reciprocal visa regime with russia is an example. instead, we criticize russia's position on the eu's vegetable imports, for example. but what does our own actions look like from outside the union? in the search for the source of the deadly bacteria, we blame each other without indisputable evidence, restrict imports and exports of commodities, demand compensation. how much trust we inspire, and not just in russia, is for everyone to answer.
commissioner, as you know, russia is a confident state and russians are a confident people. the european union's relationship with russia must take the form of a genuine partnership, not just a sham partnership. if we have excellent relations with the eastern partnership countries, then the relations between the european union and russia must be at least on the same level. i am therefore very pleased that you did not forget to mention in your speech the importance of the abolition of visa relations with russia, because the relationship between the partners must be based on trust and honesty. we should tell russia clearly and in a binding manner what the timetable is for visa waivers and what the conditions are for them to be met. commissioner, thank you for not neglecting any of the agendas that are important for the european union's partnership with russia in the preparation of the eu-russia joint summit.
austria voted against the nuclear power plant in zwentendorf to get rid of the regional chancellor. it has created a model of a nuclear power plant, one-to-one, unique in europe. i think it's an expensive experiment and that the austrians can't force anyone in europe to go down that road.
madam speaker, parliament has received over 400 petitions, and i have also received a number of complaints from businesses, non-profits, schools, charities, even government agencies that have been victims of catalogue fraud. the updated data show that they are subscribing to high financial commitments. the activity of fraudulent directory companies is intentionally directed across borders or from third countries. we need a clear europe-wide solution and despite all the efforts of the european parliament, the european commission, which has the legislative initiative, has been inactive for years. as shadow rapporteur, i proposed a number of solutions in the report to parliament. it would be sufficient for the commission to propose that those practices be included in the so-called blacklist of prohibited misleading advertising in the relevant directive. we need not only a robust european legislative solution, we need it quickly. i call on the commission to use the accelerated legislative procedure. the commission's reprehensible laxity in this case has cost many honest citizens a considerable sum of money. i think europol should also start investigating.
madam president, the unfair practices of some so-called catalogue companies remain a serious problem. their activity is always based primarily on circumvention of the law and very poor enforcement. the commission has also found that the aid granted to the beneficiaries was compatible with the internal market within the meaning of article 107 (3) (c) of the treaty. however, fraudsters rely mainly on the lower level of legal knowledge of the responsible institutions and, in many cases, unfortunately, on their lack of willingness, courage and a certain comfort. a formalist interpretation of the law allows you to not intervene, not punish these cases, and wash your hands of it. paradoxically, this leads to protecting the interests of the fraudsters instead of the victims. the question put to the commission correctly asks how the member states approach this matter. how did they apply the directive on misleading and comparative advertising? without an active and not just formal role for the state, this problem is truly insoluble and petitions from deceived victims will increase. yet, we are not helpless in this matter.
madam president, commissioner, the move away from nuclear energy, as we can see in germany, for example, is a politically incorrect decision with potentially disastrous economic consequences not only for germany itself but also, and least of all, for the central european area. i am not criticizing the german decision, but i would like to point out that each member state of the european union has the right to determine its own energy policy with all its economic, social and political implications. i insist, however, that my country, the czech republic, has the same right to determine independently how it will produce electricity. one third of the czech energy mix is energy obtained from reliable nuclear sources. i believe that we will further strengthen our nuclear resources, that we will complete additional nuclear power units while complying with all the essential safety and control parameters and requirements. (the president agreed to the question by raising the blue card under rule 149 (8) of the rules of procedure.)
first of all, colleague, i want to emphasize at this point that i have no contact with this lobby. secondly, i have not seen in any statement by the czech government that the czech nuclear power stations should be allowed to escape any control, as was suggested by commissioner oettinger.
madam chairman, just three brief remarks on the commissioner's speech. first, by adopting the stress test methodology proposed by the association of western european national regulators. they assume a probabilistic approach, from which the commissioner deviates towards an absolutist approach. secondly, i would say that for this whole approach to citizen safety to be credible, stress tests should be installed in all areas where energy is produced. most people died in the power plants where the dams broke. in 1976, for example, 26,000 people died when a dam dam burst in china. and the third note. the commissioner seems to be taking part in a green pilgrimage, but polish satirist jerzy lec says that even attending a pilgrimage does not relieve you of sweating feet and the smell.
in writing. the more than 400 petitions that have reached the ep so far highlight the widespread deceptive business practices of some 'catalogue companies' which persuade businesses, but also non-profit organisations, to register for free, only to find that they have signed a contract that also involves a fee. however, directory companies are often established in a member state other than that in which their victims are established, making it difficult for victims to seek protection or compensation from national authorities. unfortunately, directive 2006/114 on misleading and comparative advertising, which applies to business-to-business transactions, does not provide for a sufficiently effective remedy or is not properly enforced by the member states. since the commission is obliged to ensure that this directive is properly applied by the member states, it should assess the situation and, where appropriate, take appropriate corrective measures. it is primarily its task and responsibility to put an end to the deceptive commercial practices practised by 'catalogue companies'. at the very least, a blacklist of these companies' deceptive practices could help.
in writing. the motion for a resolution tabled on the question for an oral answer (b7-0315/2011) on misleading directory companies is brief, but it touches on two areas that require constant attention. the first area is the support of smes by the eu institutions and bodies, in the context of their protection against deceptive commercial practices, the second area is the protection of competition by preventing the widespread unfair competition, which includes misleading and comparative advertising. the commission's anticipated efforts are welcome, as this resolution calls on it not only to review and improve directive 2006/114/ec on misleading and comparative advertising, but also to implement corrective measures aimed at countering inconsistent enforcement of this directive by eu member states.

the author. - mr. chairman, there are moments when i can't help but wonder. one of those moments for me was the opportunity to read the draft resolution on ukraine. i had no idea until this moment that ministers and prime ministers of some countries are endowed with special immunity for life. it is undeniably remarkable that the european parliament, on the one hand, demands a consistent action against corruption and, on the other, at the very moment when a member of the former government, mr danilišin, is accused, he is granted political asylum, even in my own czech republic. and that's without any prior investigation of the case. by the way, mr. danilišin was never a member of any political party, so his accusation is purely economic. we demand that there be strict punishment for corrupt acts both inside and outside the european union. that's absolutely correct, but let's read the constitution of the country concerned and try to find out the facts. and when the corruption in the european countries is really eliminated, let's try to return to the imperial councils granted to the various countries in us, unfortunately, adopted resolutions. the gue/ngl group agrees with the general parts of the resolution, but has serious doubts on the specific points mentioned and will therefore not support the resolution.

mr president, i listened carefully to mr verhofstadt, the chairman of the liberal group, and i wanted to ask him if i was right in thinking that his slogan might be that greece is everywhere, that greece is not a single member state of the european union, but in fact it is a state of mind, and therefore greece is everywhere.
on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, it is very entertaining today in the house. there are still, let me remind you, a lot of us who experienced communism and who since then have made enormous and swift progress to prepare ourselves for membership of the european union. we were rightly made to prove ourselves, to prove that we met the copenhagen criteria, to prove that we enjoyed fully-functioning market economies and so on and so forth, but unfortunately this level of scrutiny was not applied to some european countries when entering the euro zone. why? simply because it was constructed as a political and not as an economic project, and therefore the criteria were ignored; figures were changed to bring in countries that were not yet ready. that is the core of the problem, and now we are living out the consequences and we are at a turning point. believe me, despite the fact that most members of my group are from non-euro zone member countries, we do not want the euro to fail. at the same time, it cannot be defended at any cost. we are surely on the final pages of the bail-out chapter. this european council should send a clear signal that if a country’s position is unsustainable within the euro zone then, unless it makes dramatic changes, the euro zone should not hesitate to raise the possibility of restructuring its debt or even – however cruel it might seem – of its leaving the club. mr barroso, if you do not do that, then i will tell you what will happen next: your opponents – or rather the opponents of your successor – will not be nice, decent eurosceptics like me, but really nasty anti-europeans. and why? because they will be elected by angry german taxpayers and by desperate french and dutch private entrepreneurs fed up of paying other people’s debts, and i know that neither you nor i want this to happen. so let us act accordingly. in the same way, i would ask you to please stop using or misusing every crisis as an opportunity to pursue further the supra-nationalistic agenda towards something like fiscal union or tax union, because it leads us nowhere. this is not a crisis of europe. this is just a crisis of one wrong, outdated concept of european integration, and that is all.
mr president, i would like to ask mr swoboda if he agrees with the view that in some member states the crisis is being used as a tool for the silent dispossession of the population. does he see it that way?
madam president, the time is finally approaching when the old and new member states of the union will be able to harmonize their agricultural policies and end ten years of discrimination against farmers from the new member states. i welcome that development. i hope that in the new period no new discriminatory conditions will be applied to farmers. i regret to say that the dess report contains such a thing. i am thinking in particular of support for the so-called direct payments to larger farms. i consider this to be discriminatory towards countries like the czech republic, where we have more farms than anywhere else in the european union for historical reasons. i therefore reject the introduction of direct payments as a measure which will reduce the efficiency of farming throughout the european union. agriculture has always been primarily about food production. now, however, a number of politicians, particularly from the greens and the left, want to turn farmers into biofuel producers and global warming fighters. i don't support that kind of development.
i am pleased to note that the new cap is ending a period of discrimination against farmers in some member states, including my own. thank you, mr. speaker, for your work, which shows the hand of a man who understands the issues. i am even more appalled by the attempt to create new discriminatory conditions. the direct payment poisoning is a new discrimination against member states with a specific historical development, which has resulted in the predominance of farms farming on larger areas of land. however, these farms comply with the same rules and provide the same services as small farms in other countries and are equally well placed to receive direct payments. discrimination against large enterprises due to congestion can put rural jobs at risk and stifle innovation potential. can we accept discrimination against certain member states in the form of differences in payment per hectare or overcrowding if we want to talk about the single market in a credible way? the farmers of the new twelve are now at the end of a difficult ten-year period, which they have entered into with the understanding that it is a transitional period which will not be extended. we must not continue to perpetuate inequalities that are contrary to the spirit of the accession negotiations, nor undermine the eu's credibility in the eyes of some of its citizens. we are playing with the future of the eu itself. i therefore call on all colleagues to support amendments nos 10 and 11, which reject the backlog of payments.
a basic condition for fair competition in the internal market is the establishment of fair and transparent conditions for all farmers within the eu. the cap future report is moving in this direction. a number of compromises negotiated are clearly to be welcomed, for example on simplifying administrative procedures in the various cap areas or maintaining full funding of direct payments from the eu budget and rejecting their renationalisation. on the contrary, i consider it problematic in view of the historical specificities of the individual member states to favour or disadvantage european farmers on the basis of other criteria, such as size.
for the ecr group. mr president, the package of six proposals to improve discipline within the euro area is based on the belief that member states will be more compliant with the new stricter architecture of transnational supervision. i find this problematic to consider, particularly because some members have so far been unable to comply with and effectively enforce the soft rules. let me mention four points which i consider to be controversial. the first point is reverse qualified majority voting, which has been discussed many times here. we consider the reverse qualified majority vote to be an exceptional instrument, the use of which must always be properly and consistently explained and its use properly investigated to ensure that it does not conflict with primary law. we do not agree with the increase in the number of areas of supervisory procedures where the use of reverse qualified majority voting is proposed. the procedure proposed increases the political power of the commission and the european parliament at the expense of the council and the national authorities, which is not what we want. as regards the monitoring missions, i have serious reservations about the proposal for monitoring missions, since they will be composed mainly of officials of the european commission and will be entrusted with significant powers, without the members of these missions having any political mandate. that's a policy. people, however well-intentioned, who are not subject to public scrutiny by their electorate, cannot exercise oversight over the heads of national political bodies or the highest national institutions. the scoreboard, an attempt to compile a list of macroeconomic indicators at the transnational level and to assess the ability of national governments and their economies to deal with economic imbalances, is questionable. the current situation in greece is a good example of how recipes for solving a situation that are dictated from outside arouse resistance among the domestic population and are one of the sources of escalating social tensions.
in writing. a crisis can be an opportunity to change or create new rules and institutions. economist joseph schumpeter spoke of what he called creative destruction. the eu therefore has a unique opportunity at this time to create a new mechanism that should lead to greater macroeconomic stability, fiscal and monetary, of the member states in the long term and contribute to their increased competitiveness. experience of the functioning of economic and monetary union so far shows that such rules need to be anchored in legislation rather than relying on the voluntary action of individual member states. the legislative package of six proposals to create a better framework for the management of economic affairs is therefore a step in the right direction. four proposals concern fiscal matters, including reform of the stability and growth pact, while two new regulations seek to identify and address emerging macroeconomic imbalances in the eu and the euro area. however, in order to enforce these new rules effectively, it is and will be desirable that national parliaments and the european parliament are also more closely and more timely involved in the whole process. overall, i consider the legislative package presented to be an adequate response to the global crisis and the current economic situation in the eu, based on relevant legislation and in line with the europe 2020 strategy and other eu strategic documents, and therefore recommend that it be adopted as proposed.
draftsman of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety. - madam president, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on the results of her work. as rapporteur for the opinion in the envi committee, i had the opportunity to see firsthand how a technical proposal became a highly political topic overnight, and i would also like to thank all my colleagues who managed to maintain a factual approach under the pressure of the disaster in japan. in particular, i would like to thank the shadow rapporteurs of the envi committee for their contributions to the quality of the opinion of this committee. i believe that the directive will set the legal framework of the european union for the management of spent fuel and radioactive waste and will be an integral part of the legislation ensuring the safe use of nuclear energy in those member states which retain it in their energy mix. among other things, i very much welcome the requirement that member states ensure adequate funding for the costs associated with spent fuel management. i have personally visited some radioactive waste storage facilities and i am convinced that the lack of funding could be a direct cause of a reduction in their safety, and this must be prevented in europe.
for the ecr group. madam president, as the shadow rapporteur for the ecr, i would like to commend the report by my colleague jordan cizelj. this report comes at a very difficult time, with the hysterical debate following the natural disaster in japan and the impact of this disaster on nuclear power plants. after much discussion, a rational approach to radioactive waste management was maintained. the report also stresses the importance of the obligation to inform and disclose about these activities in order to increase public confidence. all these activities must be subject to strict controls in accordance with international standards. it is important that both intermediate and long-term storage is ensured in accordance with these standards. these are very expensive activities and facilities that have been in operation for decades, if not centuries. it is therefore necessary to take into account further technological developments in the coming decades. the storage technology must allow for some flexibility into the future. in my view, it is unwise to pursue the absolute conservation of nuclear waste in a permanent repository.
in writing. the message in its original form reflects a great deal of secular enthusiasm and a somewhat unrealistic attempt to disconnect the reality of today's electricity supply from a set of wishful thinking. probably all eu citizens will applaud the bold plans for 80 and 95% reduction of emissions by 2050. but this general plan, when examined more closely, must be supported by the very real possibilities of present energy sources. it's not possible to eliminate the use of coal and natural gas overnight. by the way, the use of natural gas has been expanding significantly in recent years in the eu countries. in the wake of the hysteria that erupted after the earthquake in japan and the problems with the reactors at the fukushima nuclear power plant, citizens in some eu countries decided to move away from nuclear energy in a relatively short time. so we don't see any problem at all. in particular, the eu does not have transmission lines that could provide sufficient electricity in the north-south and east-west directions. as is well known, wind does not always blow in northern germany either, and solar cells currently convert only the solar energy that hits them into electricity with an efficiency of about 11%. the commission therefore concluded that the union industry did not suffer material injury within the meaning of article 3 (5) of the basic regulation. the conclusions of the gue/ngl report are supported, a detailed analysis of the plans must necessarily follow.
the report also contains a number of recommendations on the future of the urban dimension of cohesion policy. this report focuses on cities and their role in cohesion policy. however, i would like to stress at the outset that this does not mean that we should forget about the countryside when distributing european funds. on the contrary, we need to work together to develop both large cities and small rural communities in a synergistic way. i have, incidentally, emphasised these points in my amendments to mr siekierski's report on the future of the common agricultural policy. i would like to make a few points in my opening remarks. first, we must recognise that cities are an important platform for the implementation of the eu 2020 strategy, and for their unquestionable importance in the economic development of europe's regions. it is therefore essential that cities are given a strong mandate in implementing the objectives of this common european strategy. secondly, i would like to emphasize that in a number of cities we need to complete basic infrastructure. i believe that the appropriate solution is not simply to rebuild, but to modernise and invest technologically in infrastructure through information technology. they should ensure that the leap in growth and the improvement in the quality of life in cities really happens. it is this type of growth-promoting investment that we should prefer, also in view of the principle of concentration. thirdly, let me bring up the subject of partnership. the concept of partnership is often an empty concept. i would therefore like to see us providing cities with real support and call on the commission to make it mandatory for member states to formally involve the political representatives of the most important urban areas and associations of local and regional authorities in all stages of decision-making in cohesion policy. it is desirable that municipal representatives have the opportunity to participate in the negotiation of planned national strategic development contracts. fourthly, and this is the last major point of the proposed report that i would like to highlight today, and that is the tools of financial engineering. there's been a lot of talk about jessica. but there's not much going on in the real world. i would therefore like to invite the commission to assess the experience with these instruments and to adjust their rules so that they are competitive with other instruments in the financial market. at the same time, i would like to call on the member states to implement jessica at the level of cities, not regions or states. because that's the only way that cities can be truly owned and have long-term impacts.
the rapporteur of the committee on transport and tourism. - madam president, interreg iii, the crossborder cooperation programme for regions, is a whole development tool. i consider the flexibility provided for in article 21 of the european fund regulation to be an important element, i.e. the possibility of providing funds for expenditure not covered by the programme. i believe that both the stabilisation of the rules and this flexibility will help to facilitate the implementation of the programme. the european groupings for territorial cooperation (egtcs) can play an important role as legal entities. i believe that their status will soon be clarified and their position will be greatly strengthened. this can also contribute to the involvement of private actors, and where appropriate contributing organisations, and thus to the efficiency of the use of funds. this enhanced territorial cooperation programme is supported by the gue/ngl group.

the reporter. - mr president, allow me first of all to thank all my colleagues for the very good cooperation and the very constructive debate. let me close with two provocative remarks to this whole discussion. the structural funds were mainly based on bridging the gap between regions and should therefore be directed mainly to the most backward parts of the european union, i.e. to convergence objective 1. wealthier regions will benefit by increasing market opportunities for their products and services. on the other hand, any proposed attempts to boost investment in objective 2 regions or to create a strong transitional category of regions without knowledge of funding should be rejected. because i don't see any reason to redistribute money from the rich to the rich forever. however, i see a certain transitional mechanism between objectives 1 and 2 as a suitable complement. secondly, i think it is crucial that we stop distorting the market through funds. the fact that one entrepreneur receives a subsidy for the construction of the hall and the other does not logically leads to a distortion of the market. the second party must also include the cost of building the hall in the price of the products sold, and thus logically has them more expensive or has a lower profit. it would be much more appropriate if the final beneficiaries were only the public authorities, i.e. the state, the counties, the prefectures, the cities and the municipalities, which invested the money in pro-growth measures. i realize that such a radical change is not possible. but it is possible to at least try to go down this road and take a small step in that direction. in the words of neil armstrong, it would be a small step for european funds, but a big leap for the european economy.
