ladies and gentlemen, dear colleagues, let us take our seats for the official signing of a very important agreement between the european parliament and the european commission: the ‘lobbyists register’. we voted on the casini report at the last plenary session in strasbourg and now we will have the official signing. we voted on 11 may 2011. for the official signing, i should like to welcome minister győri and maroš šefčovič, vice-president of the european commission, and our colleagues diana wallis, vice-president of the european parliament, who was in the negotatior’s team, vice-president isabelle durant, vice-president rainer wieland and carlo casini, chair of the committee on constitutional affairs and also the rapporteur.
madam president, i am very grateful that mr schwab has managed to reach a compromise with the council, in the sense that consumer rights will be strengthened and that the compromise includes my proposals on online trading. i voted for this report, of course, but i regret that the hungarian presidency blocked my proposal, which had previously been supported by the whole of parliament, to give consumers the right to receive goods from another country on agreed terms. unfortunately, this accounts for one third of all cross-border orders rejected in this way, according to eurostat. online shops like itunes and others do not deliver their goods or services to the new member states. this discriminates against czech customers as against, for example, french or dutch customers. the internal market is thus still not unified in terms of the digital environment and the council has thus missed its exceptional opportunity.
the european parliament adopted today, madam president, a report on the common agricultural policy until 2020. this report, like myself, has long supported the drive to establish a fair playing field for all farmers in the eu, which means a departure from the historical criteria for direct payments. i did not support the report, however, to express my strong disagreement with the adoption of the proposal to allocate payments according to the size of farms. this will discriminate against, for example, czech farmers, or force them to artificially divide farms into smaller units in order to obtain subsidies. i am personally in favour of the development of small farms, but these are measures which will, in turn, make european farmers less competitive in certain countries.
in writing. - i have no objections in substance to the eu-canada civil aviation agreement. i still abstained from the vote. the reason is the unresolved situation in eu-czech republic-canada relations regarding the lifting of the unilateral visa requirement for czech citizens. i repeat that the parliament of the czech republic is blocking the ratification of the sister air transport agreement for the same reasons. i ask the european commission to finally address this problem with all seriousness. commissioner malmström's lukewarm stance is the reason that the situation has not been resolved after two years, and has contributed to the deteriorating relations between the czech republic and canada.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report by mr vlasák, rapporteur, on the european urban agenda and its future in cohesion policy. i welcome the main objective of this report, which is to promote the urban dimension of cohesion policy in the new programming period and to take account of the current developments in the urban agenda in the european union. however, i would also like to mention the recently published white paper on the future of transport by 2050, which sets out the way forward towards a single european transport area. i consider that the persisting disparity in the level of infrastructure and transport services in the different member states is one of the major obstacles to achieving the ambitious objectives of this white paper. cohesion policy instruments must therefore continue to play an important role in bringing these levels closer together towards sustainable and safe european transport. this is true for both urban and rural areas, especially cross-border ones, where prosperity and employment depend on the development of infrastructure.
in writing. on 23 june 2011, parliament approved a proposal for a directive on consumer rights, which replaces the existing four directives in one legal instrument. this is the first step towards the incorporation of consumer protection legislation into european standards. the directive is primarily a response to the fragmentation of crossborder trade regulation, where traders are not willing to follow different rules in another member state, especially when trading via the internet. in particular, the directive introduces new obligations to inform consumers of the place of business, telephone and fax numbers and email addresses for easier communication with the trader. the new obligation for traders to pay the direct costs of returning goods in cases where the price of the goods does not exceed eur 50 is also new. in the czech legal order, the proposed directive does not, apart from the above-mentioned innovations, involve any substantial interference with the legislation on consumer protection. the new rules concern mainly the conclusion of distance contracts. i therefore supported the proposal for a directive.
in writing. − i voted for the consumer rights directive because i believe that it will ultimately strengthen the rights of european consumers. in particular, my group has succeeded in putting forward a number of key points which maintain a high level of consumer protection in countries where this protection would be reduced by the introduction of common rules. it was important for the social democrats and for me personally that the directive should apply to digital products as well as, in part, to passenger transport. another important point is that the directive contains a huge list of information requirements that must be provided by the trader to the consumer at the pre-contractual stage in the case of off-premises and distance contracts. another important point is that immediately before ordering anything online, the consumer must be informed of the full price and main characteristics of the product and only he himself must expressly confirm that he wants to pay this price. in addition, the right of withdrawal will be fully harmonised in all member states, i.e. it will provide for a period of 14 days. the new directive also abolishes the exception for distance or off-premises contracts, which has been widely abused in the czech republic, for example by street vendors or at senior events, and which deprives consumers of their rights, for example to withdraw from the contract.
in writing. the draft european budget for 2012 will be only the second after the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. after the complicated negotiations last year, it is to be expected that the budgetary battle will be tough again this year, right up to the last minute. however, the real institutional struggle will only begin once the union has decided on a new multiannual financial framework. this is why the preparation of the current trialogue will need to focus on better coordination between all the parties involved in the negotiation process, so that the european budget can provide sufficient resources to meet the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy. these objectives promote smart, sustainable and inclusive growth and, therefore, investment in decent jobs. i can fully agree with the priorities set by the rapporteur, mrs balzani. i also believe that the eu budget should play its role as the main mechanism for strengthening and restoring growth and employment. i would also like to emphasise that the rapporteur's conclusions are fully in line with the dynamics of the european semester, which as a new instrument for strengthening european economic governance aims to increase coherence and complementarity and also to coordinate the eu budget and national budgets in the implementation of joint eu investments to achieve the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy.
in writing. i regret that the report on the new eu agricultural policy post-2013 adopted by the ep today failed to remove the intention to block direct payments to farmers. the approval of the stacking of subsidies in practice means that a limit will be set for direct subsidies depending on the size of the farm. i would point out that the czech republic in particular, with an average farm size of 85 ha (the european average is about 15 ha), has the largest farms in the eu, so they would pay dearly for the obstruction of direct payments. the introduction of direct payments would not only be disastrous for czech agriculture. in the worst case scenario, czech farmers would lose up to czk 13 billion, which would result in a weakening of their competitive position on the market. the result of today's vote, in my view, is therefore a blow to the farmers of the old member states, who are typically smaller farmers. i insist that the obstruction of direct payments would be an indisputable discrimination against large farms. it would lead to large farms artificially splitting up to minimise financial losses, even at the cost of increasing bureaucratic burdens. the artificial and forced crushing of farms would then ultimately cost european agriculture as a whole.
madam president, we are discussing today another of the measures prompted by the experience of the global economic crisis. the effort to monitor the otc derivatives market and, where necessary, to regulate extreme behaviour is probably reasonable. however, as with other rules adopted at european level, the need for maximum administration must not be allowed to undermine well-functioning mechanisms which, on the contrary, greatly facilitate rapid trade without significant risks. the financial instruments used, particularly in the energy sector, are in most cases not speculative but based on a real commercial interest and hedging of risks. the new rules for otc derivatives in the energy sector should contribute to strengthening stability and transparency in financial markets, but should not increase costs and prices for the end-user. this also means that the obligations of the new rules should only apply to future contracts. i am a strong advocate for ensuring that all these procedures are simplified.
the debate is closed.

the next item is the report by sylvana rapti, on behalf of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, on the universal service and ‘112’ emergency number (2010/2274(ini)) (a7-0220/2011).
mr president, i do not want to repeat what my colleagues have said, but the real problem with 112 is that only 26% of europeans are aware of it, so this communication strategy must be a priority for the member states in cooperation with the european commission. the location of the caller on this line must also be improved, since it is crucial to the chance of saving a life. i also support the idea of a so-called reverse 112 line, which would be a system of early warning of citizens by means of telecommunications services about natural and other disasters that are already increasing in our country. i also strongly support the expansion of possibilities for disabled users of telecommunications services and i welcome, of course, the commission's commitment to extending high-speed internet access to european citizens beyond urban areas.

the next item is the report by charles goerens, on behalf of the committee on development, on the future of eu budget support to developing countries (2010/2300(ini)) (a7-0206/2011).
mr president, this report contains substantial arguments for deepening the budgetary form of development aid, instead of the older project form. however, it would be desirable for the international monetary fund and the world bank to work with the commission to develop independent indicators to assess the effective use of financial support from european taxpayers' money. given that some countries, such as rwanda and uganda, demonstrate better public finance management than others, it would be desirable for the governments and parliaments of development aid recipients to share this good experience and to cooperate in the development of those evaluation indicators. in conclusion, i would like to highlight the high quality and wide coverage of both reports by my colleagues goerenz and subsequently kaczmarek. i'd like to comment on this report later.

the next item is the report by filip kaczmarek, on behalf of the committee on development, on increasing the impact of eu development policy (2011/2047(ini)) (a7-0205/2011).
mr president, i am fully in favour of the european development fund finding its place in the overall budget of the union, and i like that mr kaczmarek has formulated a loose position, not only in terms of the pressure for better control of resources, transparency and efficiency of public administration in the countries receiving development aid. the largest sum of money for development is useless if it disappears into the abyss of corruption. i also share his concern that focusing on a very limited number of safe countries at the expense of those with greater security risks is a problem, because that is precisely why assistance in those countries may be needed more. in conclusion, i would like to emphasize the warning contained in paragraph 84 of mr kaczmarek's report. it's about buying farmland, mostly from chinese investors. this could seriously threaten the local population, especially their food supply and the development of agricultural activities.

mr president, mr president of the commission, mr prime minister, hungary certainly deserves recognition for its excellent performance during the presidency, let me say it from this place, especially to the secretary of state, who spent many hours with us. it was the next new member state, after slovenia and the czech republic, to hold the presidency of the european union, and it was not an easy task. in domestic politics, for example, hungary faced an unprecedented eurozone shock, and in external relations, it faced social and political changes in north africa and the middle east unprecedented in decades, challenges that would have shaken the knees of even the most seasoned players. and it is also worth noting that hungary has maintained internal political stability during its presidency, unlike, for example, my own country, where during our presidency the opposition irresponsibly overthrew the government and thus damaged my country's image within the european union. but let me take a breath. and this is not about the hungarian presidency, but rather about the overall role of the new member countries in the european union, which is reflected in the exercise of the presidency. i do not entirely agree with prime minister orbán here, i thought that the enlargement of the european union to central and eastern europe would give the european union new dynamism, new impulses, new experiences. in my opinion, that hasn't happened yet. the new member states, in my view, have very quickly embarked on the comfortable trajectory of the old masters with all that entails, including european new speak, including federalist ideology, and the presidency of the european union is increasingly becoming a kind of technical and organisational exercise in administration rather than political leadership. so i dream of the day when a prime minister will rise from that seat and say: "we need a new european paradigm, we cannot continue with the fifty-year-old, dysfunctional, outdated concept of european integration. let's look for a new concept. of course, when someone like that is found, mr cohn-bendit will write him off, say he is anti-european, but i am sure that out in the streets the citizens-voters will applaud him.
for the group gue/ngl. mr president, i would like to say that i like hungary and hungarian culture, and that is why i was curious to see how hungary would implement its strong europe, erős európa, programme. i must say that i was disappointed because i have been involved in several trialogue meetings, tripartite negotiations between the council, the commission and the european parliament, and i have found a complete lack of preparation and a complete lack of opinion. it's what my colleague zahraedil said, which means continuing a certain comfortable, worn-out stereotype. if i could sum up the results of this presidency, i would say, unlike those who are thanking the hungarian presidency, that it was a 'zero presidency', a 'zero presidency'. it is a great disappointment to me also because it was really a presidency that was facing challenges both internally and externally. so where did mr. orbán's group or team take this strong approach? this strong approach was applied to the own media, the hungarian media, the hungarian intelligentsia and the hungarian population. there was a strong approach. i believe that there is an unnecessary complacency in this parliament and that we really need to wake up and look for other ways. an opportunity that the new member countries have so far squandered, including my own country. and so, perhaps, that awakening will come when we face even greater challenges. perhaps in this context i can recall the words of the great hungarian poet endre ady, in his poem we need mohács, nekünk mohács kell.
for the ecr group. mr president, i believe that the report by mrs lepage goes far beyond the commission's proposal, as the commissioner has stressed. it is clearly driven by opposition to gmos and under the banner of strengthening the role of national states, it seeks to ban gmos even if the product is approved and scientifically tested by the fsa. we know that genetically modified food is becoming a common product in the rest of the world, and our precautionary measures can and are worsening the position of our farmers on the world market. this report would be fair if it allowed member states and our citizens, on the other hand, to grow genetically modified food which is not approved at european level. but this report only increases the existing restrictions. i cannot support this report unless amendments are adopted which make it a little more liberal.
in writing. member states need a clear legal framework for gmo cultivation that does not jeopardise existing legislation. the proposal also serves eu citizens who do not want gmos to be grown on their territory. given the lack of unity in europe on gmo cultivation, member states need to share responsibility for how they release gmos into the environment. the environmental risks and the threat to biodiversity can be high, and the monoculture nature of gm crops is also a danger. in some countries, so-called "superweeds", which have been created by artificial resistance to herbicides, are already a problem. the precautionary principle must be strictly observed in the case of gmos. it is necessary to address the current situation where studies required by the applicable legislation are not available. therefore, the powers of the state should be strengthened in the absence of relevant data on impacts at national, regional or local level. the rights of member states to use gmos on their own territory should be respected, which is a matter of subsidiarity, but without prejudice to the rules of the internal market and the free movement of goods and products within the union. i welcome parliament's decision and i call on the council to accept it as soon as possible.
mr president, i would just like to thank all the political groups, and particularly the leaders of the political groups, for a very smooth arrangement that allowed us to fill these two vacant places. it sends a very good signal that the second half of our term will go as smoothly as today.
in writing. following the floods that affected, among others, the liberec and ústí regions in august 2010, the rapporteur, mr böge, presented a report on the further mobilisation of the european solidarity fund. i welcome the positive outcome of today's vote to support those regions in my home country affected by natural disasters. in conclusion, i would also like to stress the importance of the existence of this fund, which is available to help member states in the recovery from natural disasters. it is an effective expression of solidarity that really helps the citizens of the european union in times of urgent need. i thank all my colleagues for their support of this report.
in writing. i was pleased to support the report on the mobilisation of the european solidarity fund to rebuild infrastructure damaged by the floods that devastated the territories of slovenia, croatia and the czech republic in september 2010. the water and landslides have claimed human lives and caused extensive damage to buildings and agricultural land, and have damaged road and rail networks and other infrastructure. although the aid amount represents only a few percent of the total damage assessment, it is nevertheless a significant aid to finance repairs. the czech republic is drawing 11 million euros from this disaster relief fund, more than double the amount it received the first time.
in writing. − in general, the report objectively describes the current situation regarding universal service in electronic communications. i particularly appreciate the fact that parliament is aware of the importance of preserving the market environment in the field of electronic communications. this is indeed borne out by the report itself, which stresses the need to avoid market distortions in the enforcement of the revised directive. although the report is fairly balanced, i am slightly concerned by its call for the commission and the member states to step up measures to promote demand and the introduction of new services rather than simply providing connectivity. i believe that this is a misunderstanding of the role of the state in the field of electronic communications and broadband policy. if the state is to operate transparently and not distort competition and market mechanisms, it can naturally only support the development of broadband by providing quality e-government, e-health or e-learning services, i.e. a quality offer. any further measures increasing the demand for broadband almost always undermine the functioning of the market and distort competition to a greater or lesser extent.
in writing. − i support this report because it very accurately points out the current shortcomings in the functioning of the internal market. in particular, it addresses a number of administrative obstacles that effectively impede the free movement of goods across the union. i appreciate that the report stresses the need for the commission to intervene against any member state which violates the principles of the internal market. the report also recognises that member states are responsible for their trade-placement policies and that the union should not interfere with the arrangements that member states make for themselves in this area. i consider the report's key message to be the emphasis on competition law as a tool to defend against the abuse of a dominant position or significant market power. the report does not, therefore, have the trend of resorting to the creation of new legislation in this area, but prefers to use standard legal instruments. i appreciate that, unlike many other reports coming from the european parliament, the report addresses the essence of european integration, namely the functioning of the internal market, while respecting that the essence of market relations is to be contractual freedom and consumer protection, and that market dynamics must not be distorted.
in writing. i agree with the need to review the regulation establishing a union-wide network of public authorities responsible for enforcing consumer protection laws in the member states (cpc network), which became operational in december 2006. the revision takes the form of an update of the content of the annex to reflect the latest developments in consumer protection legislation. the latest commission report on the application of the cpc regulation 2009 reveals a number of doubts regarding the system for cross-border enforcement of the consumer acquis, such as the fact that a significant number of notified bodies are not connected to the system or are not actively using it. another problem is the increasing length of processing requests. in addition, the continued fragmentation of procedural rules for enforcement needs to be addressed. different systems of national legislation and enforcement make it difficult to effectively combat infringements within the eu. some public authorities have effective means to stop infringements, while others have to go to court and face lengthy procedures. the costs of legal proceedings may also cause difficulties, in particular if the requesting authority is unable to bear the costs of legal proceedings, if any. the fact that applications are taking longer than they are closed (currently over 150 days) can be partly explained by the greater complexity of cross-border cases, but is often a sign of a failure to meet deadlines.
in writing. the eu should focus on energy infrastructure that is outdated and poorly connected in the coming financial period. moreover, quality infrastructure is essential to achieve the eu's long-term goals, such as reducing carbon emissions, guaranteeing security of supply and price stability for consumers, and developing a single energy market that ensures access to energy resources at affordable prices for businesses and citizens. as regards electricity, the commission should make progress in defining and implementing its proposals for priority corridors to link european transport networks by 2020, in particular the proposals on infrastructure and interconnections necessary to integrate the production of electricity from renewable sources. the key is to accelerate the identification and construction of electric highways. the new network infrastructure should include 'smart networks' thanks to the resources offered by new information and communication technologies. smart technologies should enable citizens to control their household appliances, thus preventing waste and reducing their individual and collective costs. natural gas is a particular problem. i find our reliance on a single source in eastern europe worrying, as it can lead to problems of security of supply, as has happened in the past. i therefore believe that diversification of resources should be the main feature of the new policy aimed at securing supply.
in writing. − i believe that eu activities in the area of social services of general interest should focus on the exchange of best practices in promoting further modernisation of social services of general interest in individual member states. i expect the commission to play an initiating and supporting role, including training regional and local authorities in the implementation of eu rules in relation to social services of general interest. following the 2007 communication, a reform programme containing clarifications of this aid and recognition of the specific non-market nature of social services of general interest should be put in place to ensure full compliance not only with the provisions of the single market but also with the social obligations contained in the treaties. the objective should be to create a flexible framework for social services of general interest, using all the tools at our disposal, to ensure that the union's social objectives do not restrict or even paralyse business activity within the internal market.
mr president, i am one of those colleagues who are satisfied with the result of the compromise reached. i would like to wish and thank the rapporteur for the results of her work. there's been a lot of talk about country of origin, especially with fresh meat. it was a little bit funny to me how we can be european and how our europeanness disappears the moment we have to buy a piece of meat. but i personally have no problem with that, and i know that it is often a demand of our citizens as well. some of the proposals that were there assumed that every eu citizen was a fool and had to be constantly educated and instructed. i'm glad we agreed on an acceptable font size. it will probably have to be sufficient within the limits of the size of the packaging and within the limits of our consumers. i also think that some of the drastic changes in the final product will be a burden on consumers, but that's to be expected. so i consider the result an acceptable success and i'm going to vote for it.
in writing. the debate on the proposal for a regulation on the provision of food information at second reading is a good illustration of the irreplaceable role of parliament as the body closest to the citizens of the union. we cannot allow economic interests alone to prevail. it is logical that industry lobbies their governments to reduce the information requirements to the minimum, as this saves costs. personally, i consider it most important not only that information be provided as widely as possible, but that, for example, the obligation of food manufacturers to label products containing substances that pose risks to allergy sufferers or other sick people be strictly observed. i consider this information on the packaging of products to be essential. it is also totally unacceptable to provide information on the composition and use of foodstuffs on packaging in as small a print as possible. this practice poses an unacceptable risk to the older generation, whose rights are being curtailed by this practice. most seniors have trouble reading this type of writing. i therefore welcome further detailed definition of the obligations of manufacturers and distributors in the labelling and provision of food information to consumers.
mr president, i would first of all like to congratulate the rapporteur on her really hard work on this report. the compromise reached should be taken as a first step to be followed up as soon as possible. as things stand, european drivers abroad feel they can get away with it, and for the most part they can. according to a eurobarometer survey last june, cross-border enforcement is the third highest priority for european citizens in terms of road safety, after improving transport infrastructure and enforcing traffic rules at national level. i therefore strongly support the rapporteur's view that the commission should propose as soon as possible harmonisation in the area of road traffic enforcement. only if we set up sanctions at european level will we achieve that road traffic rules apply to all drivers without exception. the compromise we have now reached is to be welcomed. the exchange of information between member states can make a significant contribution to reducing the number of serious road accidents. the estimated 5,000 victims spared speak for themselves, and that's why i'm calling for us to support this compromise tomorrow.
in writing. road safety consists of three circuits. the first is infrastructure safety, i.e. the design of the roads, the visibility, the placement of signs, etc. the second circle is the safety and security features of the vehicles and the third is the driver of the vehicle. if the white paper set out a significant reduction in the number of road deaths (the target was a 50% reduction) years ago, then according to various studies the driver is the weakest of the three factors affecting accidents. it is therefore important to take all possible measures to 'improve' the weakest of the three elements of road traffic. one of these measures is to improve the enforcement of traffic regulations. according to various investigations, improving enforcement of road transport law is one of the important elements of improving the current situation, i.e. non-enforcement of fines for offences committed in another state. in some states, law enforcement agencies are easily found, especially in the case of bad parking. in other cases, the matter is more complex, and enforcement is lacking, especially when automated means are used, without the transfer of information between states. the subject of the report is therefore very topical and i am personally sorry that the amendments of the european commission and the council omit a number of parts of the original text. however, i believe that we can accept the material after the rapporteur's proposal has been approved.
mr president, the threat of terrorism in the world today is unfortunately real and security is a priority that cannot be taken lightly. the introduction of technical measures aimed at improving safety at european airports is, of course, understandable. however, the feasibility of their implementation may mean legal uncertainty for european citizens and, in a way, less effectiveness of these measures. i therefore consider it appropriate to adopt framework rules on this sensitive issue for their possible application at european level. i'd like to thank the reporting officer and the shadow reporters for the work they did on the report. i'm glad that human health issues have been brought into the spotlight. ionising radiation and the risks of repeated and long-term exposure, as well as the different legislation in different member states, are just one of the many aspects of this issue. the others have already been mentioned. protection of personal data, privacy, human dignity, the way it is presented and so on. the financing of these facilities is also a key issue. unfortunately, no technical measure can guarantee 100% safety. that's just a concluding statement.
for the ecr group. i would also like to congratulate you, mr president, because i have two small children at home and i know what a joy it is. now, on to the subject. as a general supporter of equal opportunities for women and men in all areas, i welcome the initiative in this area and believe that it is necessary to raise awareness of this issue and to put it at the forefront of the concerns of the member states. the established and prevailing monocultures in company management are no longer economically or socially viable. i was very supportive of this report when it first started, because the rapporteur initially focused on creating a suitable framework for women in their career development. however, during the legislative procedure in the committee, this report has changed considerably. in its current form, it calls for the introduction of specific binding measures at european level. some member states have already developed their own initiative with regard to the introduction of quotas and it should, in my view, be left to the others to go in that direction. i am convinced that, as much as we all wish it, without the creation of appropriate working conditions, quotas alone will not help women in their work and careers.
in writing. the report by mrs pervenche berès sends a clear signal for a lasting solution to the current crisis and sets out a series of ambitious recommendations. i can agree with the rapporteur that it was the crisis caused by the high levels of public debt in the various member states that revealed the risks of intra-european imbalances. the eu must act and respond in unison by putting more pressure on closer coordination and harmonisation of fiscal policy. i share this view and i also agree with the rapporteur's idea that it is deeper european integration that can act as a protective shield against the next crisis. i agree with the rapporteur that the asymmetry of developments in the individual euro area member states must not be further exacerbated in the future. the rapporteur is very bold in calling for the creation of a single european finance ministry, whose remit would include the joint management of the debt. this european super-ministry could contribute to improving economic policy coordination not only in the euro area. such a revolution would be revolutionary, but it is not at present clear whether the union or the eurozone is capable of such a fundamental ideological and organisational revolution. at the same time, it is already clear that the euro area cannot continue to function in its current form after the current crisis and will need deeper reform.
madam president, i supported the resulting compromise of the trilogue because it is a sensible piece of legislation that can lead to a strengthening of road safety. i think it is right that this directive should only provide for the exchange of information and not for the harmonisation of sanctions and the enforcement of fines, because that would, in my view, place an undue burden on the administrations of the member states. however, i would like to point out that the final text presents certain problems from a legal point of view. according to article 9, the commission should develop guidelines for road safety at union level within the framework of the common transport policy. however, this directive is based on a police legal basis and therefore it is not possible to propose harmonisation measures in the form of the proposed guidelines in the area of law enforcement, where there is exclusive competence of the member states.
madam president, i and our groups welcome and support the desire to increase the representation of women in management positions and on company boards, which is still very low at present. however, we could not support this report because it adopted proposals which support and call for binding targets and legislative action at european level. the principle of competitiveness in the labour market is our guiding principle. the selection of women for management positions and their appointment to the boards of directors shall not be based on gender but on qualifications and appropriate experience. we oppose the introduction of binding legislative measures and quotas for women at european level and believe that national governments and employers are better placed to decide when and how to support women in increasing participation in these positions.
in writing. - i support the extension of broadband coverage as i believe it will facilitate communication, especially for people with reduced mobility or living in isolation, and improve access to services in rural areas and support the development of small and medium-sized enterprises. i am therefore disappointed that the eur 1 billion of funding promised in 2008 in the european economic recovery plan for 100% broadband coverage by 2010 has not been allocated and thus has not yet been achieved. i have no doubt that broadband services are key to the competitiveness of eu industry and make a major contribution to economic growth, social cohesion, job creation and to increasing the participation of all social groups in the digital life of the eu. i believe that the successful implementation of the broadband package is crucial to tackling unemployment, especially among young people, in the context of the europe 2020 strategy. in this respect, i therefore support the commission's cooperation with the eib to improve the financing of high-speed and ultra-high-speed networks, with the view that this financing should be oriented towards open infrastructure projects that promote the diversity of services. i also support the commission in its efforts to explore the possibilities of new financial resources and innovative financial instruments.
in writing. i think it's a positive development that the nutrition facts don't have to be on the front of the pack. this leaves producers with more scope to display the name, logo and various marketing communications necessary to ensure the competitiveness of the product on the market. the consumer also receives new information on the origin of poultry, pigmeat, sheepmeat and lambmeat from the packaging, which is an important prerequisite for making an informed decision on the purchase of products containing meat, especially in the context of the recent avian influenza outbreak. overall, i believe that the compromise reached balances the interests of consumers and producers, which is the exception rather than the rule in european legislation. the weakness of the new food labelling rules is, in my view, the lack of response to the problem of obesity, which is becoming the epidemic of the third millennium. this is a sensitive issue especially for the czech people, who are over two thirds obese and currently ranked second among the eu's most obese nations. in this context, i could imagine, for example, the introduction of an obligation to put a warning on the packaging of products with an excessive fat or sugar content. unfortunately, the new food labelling rules do not go that far.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, i am pleased that the compromise text of the regulation on food labelling has been adopted by parliament. this compromise is close to the position recommended by my committee on the internal market two years ago. finally, a minimum font size (1.2 mm) will be introduced for mandatory nutrition information, allergen warnings, warnings on the use of substitutes or pressed or minced meat and other information to help consumers make better choices in food shops. this information includes the indication of the country of origin for all meat and fish or information on when the food was first frozen. i appreciate that mrs sommer has managed to negotiate a compromise with the council for the second reading. the advantage of this is that the commission will assess within two years the benefits and costs of any extension of the mandatory nutrition information.
in writing. the studies carried out show that foreign drivers are responsible for a disproportionate number of road accidents caused mainly by speeding. sanctions imposed for offences committed in the territory of a member state other than that in which the driver who committed them is resident are very often not enforced, in particular for offences that were recorded automatically, the number of which has increased significantly with the expansion of automatic radar systems on european roads. this relative impunity undermines the achievement of road safety objectives and calls into question the legitimacy of these controls in the eyes of european citizens who demand that resident and foreign drivers be treated in the same way. on the one hand, i welcome the council's change of legal basis from transport policy to police cooperation at first reading, because i consider the new legal basis to be more legislatively clean. on the other hand, i regret that it has used this amendment to weaken the integration elements by removing references to enforcement procedures and limiting its proposal to data exchange. enforcement thus remains optional, subject to the decisions of individual member states and consistent with national procedures. i consider this a missed opportunity to effectively help improve road safety in the eu.
in writing. air safety is a topic of endless debate. this concerns the security of passenger transport (checking of persons and luggage), the transport of goods and postal items. what substances can be potentially dangerous? liquids, gels and aerosols of a volume of > 100 ml, firearms and explosives, solid objects of defined shapes and dimensions (e.g. small nail scissors). the author of the report overlooks the fact that the european commission has found no one to justify the technically pointless regulation a general ban on the carriage of lags on board aircraft requested by the ep for the seventh year running. incidentally, neither the subway nor the high-speed railway have similar regulations, even though the number of passengers and the danger of attack are comparable. security scanners have two important unofficial functions. these contracts are of interest to suppliers and provide employment for a significant number of workers. their reliability is not 100%, despite considerable technical progress. the question is, what are we looking for? are we able to detect the target objects with the required probability? i'm still guessing the problematic location of the whole thing, and i have strong doubts about it. as for the lag affair, i can see in the background the current prices of drinks on board airplanes and at airports. i can't think of any other rational justification for the 100 ml fluid limit. despite my reservations about scanners, i believe that many parts of the report are rational and that is why gue/gnl supports it.
madam high representative, if the russian federation wants to be a respected partner for europe, then it must abandon the totalitarian practices by which it is now eliminating opposition, including the already-implemented exclusion of the parnas party from participating in elections. i lived in the former eastern bloc, and so i'm very sensitive to the way russia is gradually moving from totalitarianism through authoritarianism to democracy. but the current government, despite the ruling of the european court of justice, is systematically changing the electoral rules and stifling the free competition of political opinions. unfortunately, it's proof that the government is only pretending to build a democratic russia. and president medvedev in particular is tightening the reins again. i trust that you will continue to insist on respect for human rights in the russian federation and on putting an end to these practices, which otherwise also become an obstacle to cooperation within the eastern partnership policy. i believe that these practices are designed to ensure one-party rule for all time, as one of my colleagues here has said. i am, however, afraid that the autumn elections to the duma will not be declared free. so let me ask you a question in advance. what action do you propose to take on behalf of the european union, madam high representative? what can we do now to prevent or at least minimize such a situation? what is happening in russia now shows that russia is indeed moving towards an authoritarian system rather than a democracy. and that's bad for europe as well.
madam president, my colleague and friend méndez de vigo, i can say this, is certainly a friend of mine and i have a great relationship with his country, he probably lives in the mountains. a large part of the voters in the european union, about half a billion, live in the valley, and voter turnout is steadily decreasing year by year, and i think that's a big problem for the european union, because the european parliament is the only institution that is directly elected by the citizens. and the legitimacy of the citizens in this chamber is diminished year by year by those elections. i feel that the very essence of the measures is being lost when i read them, namely citizens within the european union. here in parliament, measures are being proposed where no one asks the citizens what they themselves want from the union. the engineering proposal is to create a single european constituency whose members would be even further removed from their citizens than the members currently elected in the member states. let us therefore try to get closer to the citizens, not invent pan-european circuits, but strengthen direct democracy and let the citizens themselves decide more often. let's think about how to allow them to participate in elections, for example, via the internet.
today marks the sixth anniversary of the terrorist attacks on london's public transport system, which left 52 people dead and nearly 800 injured. we remember and remember what happened in london on july 7, 2005, an event that we will never forget. our thoughts are with the families and friends of the victims of the terrorist attack. the london bombing was one of the worst terrorist attacks ever committed on european soil. let me say to the bombers and the terrorists: whatever they do, they will never achieve what they seek, for we truly value every human life. no terrorist or terrorist attack can ever cripple or destroy our faith in fundamental values such as basic human rights. terrorism is an attack on all of us, on the very essence of a democratic society. it will never be justified. europe will always be united in the fight against terrorism in all its forms. i'd like to ask for a moment of silence. in writing. the commission has proposed that 2012 be the european year for active ageing. the aim of this european year is to stimulate and support the efforts of member states, regional and local authorities, social partners and civil society to promote active ageing and to do more to mobilise the potential of the rapidly growing population over the age of 55. in the committee on regional development, as shadow rapporteur, i promoted and supported the idea of adding a subtitle to the european year of active ageing, and it was successful. the subtitle is 'solidarity between generations'. active ageing aims to create better opportunities and working conditions for older workers so that they have a place in the labour market, to combat social exclusion by enhancing active participation in society and to promote healthy ageing. all these aspects are fulfilled in the decision, which is why i supported the declaration of 2012 as the european year for active ageing.
in writing. − i think that a single european electoral system is a good idea, but unfortunately it is too far ahead of political and social reality. it is clear that there is no european political nation at present that expresses its will in a unified way in the european elections, and european political parties are more a forum for discussion than a tight-knit organisation striving to gain political power. in this context, the decision to add 25 new meps seems to me to be an artificial step, which does not come from below, from the needs and wishes of the electorate, but reflects the wishes of a group of eurofederalists who are trying to push the eu towards a federal state with a single political and electoral system. the solution adopted remains in many respects half-way. it does not guarantee that all member states will be represented among the 25 pan-european meps, and the number 25 does not even correspond to the number of member states. it is more of a symbolism, but it is the symbolism that has traditionally played a major role in shaping citizens' relationship with the eu, which is why i am surprised that the authors of the proposal have so incomprehensibly underestimated it. voters in smaller member states in particular will have a legitimate feeling that their vote is not counted.
madam president, war crimes such as mass rape, sexual slavery and sterilization are among the worst forms of humiliation, which in the congo means the lifelong exclusion of these victims from society. it is alarming that offenders from the ranks of paramilitary groups and the military are usually not punished at all. the extension of the mandate of the blue helmets by the security council for another year is necessary, but it is also necessary that the monusco troops really start protecting civilians, that president kabila declare zero tolerance for rape and also that an impartial investigation into these crimes begins. the congolese government must have a program to reintegrate the victims into society. the commission should not only allocate funds to shelters, but also propose legislation to label products containing minerals whose extraction is a source of violence in the kivu region.

the next item is the report by reinhard bütikofer, on behalf of the committee on industry, research and energy, on an effective raw materials strategy for europe (2011/2056(ini)) (a7-0288/2011).
the availability of non-energy and non-agricultural raw materials is a key condition for the transition to a sustainable economy. for example, china currently controls 97% of the world production of rare earth elements and has been systematically reducing its exports since 2005, while the growth of world demand is driving up its prices. the commission has not established that the chinese exporting producers are in a position to export the product concerned to the union market. it is therefore clear that a strategy for the efficient use of raw materials will define the competitiveness of european industry. we need to focus on reusing and recycling the material that is already available. for example, recovering aluminium from recycling is 95% more economical than producing it from new material. millions of tonnes of potentially usable material lie in european landfills. an effective waste policy is not just an ecological symbol, but above all a prerequisite for ensuring resource independence. there is no guarantee that development policy will not become a diplomatic tool for getting raw materials from developing countries with large reserves. the so-called raw materials diplomacy threatens to give rise to a hidden neo-colonialism, which sees countries with vast reserves as mere exporters of raw materials. europe is asleep at this moment and it is necessary to take such measures as soon as possible to ensure that there is enough strategic raw materials for it technologies and equipment, renewable energy and other key sectors. it is no longer just an environmental problem, it is also about the economic freedom of europe.
madam president, i believe that mediation, as well as other alternative dispute resolution methods, is a useful thing. its benefits are undeniable. i would like to emphasise the main advantage of mediation, which is to achieve a peaceful solution acceptable to both parties. on the other hand, there are also many disadvantages associated with mediation. its main characteristic, voluntariness, can also act as a negative aspect. the other party cannot be forced to complete the process to the final form. the adoption in 2008 by the european parliament and the council of a directive on certain aspects of mediation in civil and commercial matters, after four years of painstaking negotiations, in my view broke another taboo on european integration. while this directive does not aim to harmonise or even create a single set of rules for mediation across the european union, it does regulate the field of mediation. at the same time, it indirectly creates pressure on states that have no regulation of the mediation process at all. article 5 also allows for the use of mediation to be made compulsory, which in my view is contrary to the fundamental principle of mediation, namely its voluntariness. like mediation itself, the european directive does not only bring positive results. all eu member states should now have measures in place to transpose eu legislation into their own law. however, nine countries, including the czech republic, have not yet notified all the national measures necessary to fully implement the directive. why is that? it should be noted that mediators in the czech republic are already operating, but they work on the basis of a professional certificate as advisors. the mediation law was discussed by parliament once, several years ago, but the mps did not approve the proposal. they were taught the passage that couples unable to agree on a settlement would have to use the services of a mediator. in mid-june this year, the government approved a draft new law on mediation in non-criminal matters. the controversial points of the draft law included, for example, the insurance of mediators, the way in which they were tested, or what qualification requirements mediators should meet. we have to wait for some time for the legal regulation of mediation in non-criminal cases like this in the czech republic. the question must be asked whether it is necessary for european regulation to put pressure on the states to accept something that works in those states and which should in principle be voluntary.
- advanced technological development, the development of more advanced means of production or technologically sophisticated products opens up possibilities not only for their use but also for their misuse for purposes other than those for which they are intended. the control of this type of goods and their classification under international control regimes is not only a sensitive commercial issue but also, by its very nature, a security issue. i believe that it is appropriate to promote effective and transparent control regimes that allow the monitoring of international trade in such technologies that could be misused for military purposes and in particular for the development and production of weapons of mass destruction. but i also add that transparency, design and efficiency should be the basis.
in writing. − i welcome the establishment of a public regulated service (prs) to be offered under the european galileo satellite navigation programme, the continuity of which is to be ensured in particular in severe crisis situations. since non-compliance with security rules may also have an impact on other participants and users (e.g. security deficiencies could lead to unauthorised and hostile use of the prs), the use, management and control of access to the prs should be based on common standards. i believe that the agreed safety mechanism offers the necessary balance between defining common minimum standards and enforcement at eu level on the one hand and decentralising the existing control function of the member states on the other. given that participation in the prs is voluntary for each member state and that member states decide independently on the nature of the use of the prs and whether users should pay for this service (it should be borne in mind that the gps signal is free of charge), it would seem fair to me that only those member states participating in the prs should cover the national operating costs.
in writing. − i was the shadow rapporteur of the epp on this legislation within the security and defence subcommittee and i am pleased that the eu council and the european parliament have managed to agree on the details of the operation of the galileo public regulated service. i am pleased that my proposal for guarantees of respect for human rights, including religious freedoms, by third countries wishing to use this service has been implemented despite the opposition of some member states. no totalitarian and authoritarian country will be able to participate in the project. however, i also draw attention to the risk that some of the galileo frequencies could be jammed by the chinese navigation system compass and i ask the european commission to address this issue in bilateral negotiations with china. i also appreciate the high level of data and privacy protection and security standards, which, contrary to the european commission's proposal, have been successfully enforced after pressure from the european parliament. unfortunately, the financing has not been finalised, and the european commission has yet to prepare any proposals for charging for the use of the service. on the contrary, it is positive that the european producers will retain exclusivity for the production of safety modules. as a doctor, i welcome the possibilities this service will bring to the integrated emergency system, including shorter response times for emts.
in writing. − i supported the report because i believe that the conditions of women approaching retirement age need to be highlighted and that this group of very vulnerable people in this age group should be given due, non-discriminatory attention, whether in the context of demographic trends, employment, pay conditions, pension provision, health care, etc. the feminization of poverty at retirement age is, unfortunately, a reality. the report highlights a number of serious phenomena, which is why i welcome it and have voted in favour of its adoption.
in writing. parliament's plenary today adopted an own-initiative report on the implementation of directive 2008/52/ec of 21 may 2008 on certain aspects of mediation in civil and commercial matters. this directive provides for the establishment of a mediation institution with a wide range of dispute resolution applications. the mediation procedure envisaged by the directive is an amicable, out-of-court settlement of a dispute between the parties, with the ongoing mediation procedure interrupting the running of limitation and prescription periods. it does not in any way restrict the rights of all to a judicial settlement of the dispute, but rather motivates the parties to resolve the dispute through mediation. it turns out that where states have introduced mediation, there has been a significant decrease in litigation. unfortunately, the report adopted by parliament reveals that the czech republic, together with austria, finland and sweden, has not yet transposed the directive into law, which is very surprising, especially in view of the czech government's stated efforts to speed up judicial proceedings. the overload of czech courts in civil disputes is alarming. the report notes that in italy, romania and bulgaria in particular, the introduction of mediation under this directive has led to a significant reduction in the number of court disputes and, for example, in bulgaria, up to one third of all disputes are resolved through mediation. i supported that report with the intention of calling on the czech government to start working urgently on the harmonisation of the czech legal order and to introduce a mediation procedure, in the light of the very positive experience of mediation in other member states.
in writing. - the eu is highly dependent on imports for a wide range of raw materials. the commission considered that the chinese exporting producers were not in a position to provide sufficient evidence of the existence of the subsidy. in this context, the raw materials initiative is an important first step. the eu needs to develop a coherent and targeted strategy with firm priorities, especially for the most critical types of raw materials. to this end, the european commission should distinguish between focusing on the raw materials initiative and on commodity markets, and in addition, it should establish a working group on raw materials linking all relevant directorates-general. such an inter-agency working group should identify priority actions for critical raw materials such as rare earths and can ensure strategic coherence. france and the united states already have such working groups. in this context, the creation of an innovation partnership focusing on raw materials is becoming more urgent. another key aspect that needs to be better embedded in the european commission's raw materials initiative is substitution. the eu should establish mutually beneficial partnerships that bring mutual benefits to resource-rich countries and to the eu itself. in addition to the eu-africa partnership, the eu should also promote partnerships with countries in eastern europe, latin america, asia, the united states and japan.
for the group gue/ngl. mr president, when i was elected to the european parliament more than seven years ago, i thought i knew something about energy and the issues surrounding it, that i was well-versed in it and that i understood it in principle. after my first term in brussels, i realized i knew almost nothing. and in this second term in parliament, i have a growing feeling that i'm beginning to lose sight of that. why would you do that? because the main idea that i hear all the time, over and over again, is that liberalization is supposed to bring cheaper energy prices to consumers. but i have to say, in seven years, that's never happened. if so, please correct me. and yet, under this central slogan of a more stable, more reliable and cheaper energy supply, we are constantly adopting new and new rules. in the end, overall globalisation and market liberalisation do not ensure more reliable and cheaper energy. in the end, the main costs are always borne by the citizens as consumers. unlike the big players in the market, who can't complain about the small profits. the document presented is rather technical and complex and i cannot help feeling that it is best oriented and influenced by the major players in the european energy market, namely the large member states. however, it is also a fact that the report contains elements of enhanced transparency, which colleagues have already spoken about. it's also because of the reporter's work, which i would like to rate highly and appreciate, because it was extremely challenging. as is the fact that energy markets are cross-border, certain rules are necessary. in the itre committee we made a number of amendments to the commission's proposal to focus even more on preventing abuses and manipulation in the wholesale energy market. despite all this, we still have a lot of work to do in this field. for example, because there is no pan-european energy market yet, because it is not interconnected, and because we cannot, or only to a limited extent, react flexibly and effectively to possible crisis situations.

the next item is the joint debate on better legislation with three reports:– the report by eva lichtenberger, on behalf of the committee on legal affairs, on the twenty-seventh annual report on monitoring the application of european union law (2009) (2011/2027(ini)) (a7-0249/2011),– the report by sajjad karim, on behalf of the committee on legal affairs, on better legislation, subsidiarity and proportionality and smart regulation (2011/2029(ini)) (a7-0251/2011), and– the report by judith sargentini, on behalf of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs, on public access to documents (rule 104(7)) for the years 2009-2010 (2010/2294(ini)) (a7-0245/2011).
mr president, i would like to thank all the rapporteurs for their excellent report and their work, and in particular my colleague mr karim. i too am very concerned that the commission will not be able to deliver on its commitment to reduce the administrative burden by the 25% target by 2012. i therefore place high expectations on the edmund stoiber group, whose mandate has been extended until the end of 2012. the group is due to publish a major report on best practices and the easiest way of implementing european law in the member states in november this year. this report could speed up the process of reducing bureaucracy in order to achieve the original aim of reducing administrative burdens. the commission itself is not the major obstacle to reducing bureaucracy and administrative burden, but the european parliament and the member states, which lack qualified methodological support for the implementation of european law. i feel that the commission lacks a strong partner on the european parliament's side to fight bureaucracy. we should therefore consider setting up a european parliament committee on the removal of bureaucratic burdens.
mr president, leading economists agree that the main problems of the european market are in particular excessive regulation, administrative, bureaucratic and legislative obstacles, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises. instead of creating a business-friendly environment and encouraging human activity, we're on our way to becoming the world's champions in regulating everything. this is also demonstrated by the european union's efforts to regulate, for example, so-called cookies, which paradoxically do not refer to cookies, but to information about the internet user stored in the temporary memory of the computer. the question most frequently asked, however, as to what should or should not be regulated in european policy, does not, in my view, go to the heart of the matter. it's more about why and whether to regulate at all. i therefore believe that for every newly proposed standard, the european commission should provide an impact assessment, including what would happen if regulation did not exist.
in writing. the issue of public access to eu documents should also be covered by the public consultations launched by the commission. the vast majority of these are only available in english, which in my view limits the ability of citizens from other speaking countries to express themselves fully on the topics of the public consultation. the accessibility of public consultations in general should also be improved, because if we want the widest possible participation of the public in the decision-making process, which was, among other things, one of the main requirements resulting from the consultation of the priorities of the single market act proposals, we should create the corresponding, not just the formal, conditions for this.
in writing. the current unflattering situation in the peripheral euro area countries calls for further consideration. one of the prerequisites for european integration was the establishment of a single internal market within the eu based on the principle of the four freedoms, to which the single currency, the euro, was also intended to contribute. however, over time it has proved difficult to create a monetary union without an adequate fiscal and institutional framework. i believe that a comprehensive solution to the current crisis should address the following three areas: the financial sector, fiscal policy and the competitiveness of the emu and the eu respectively. first, there must be a restoration of confidence in the eu financial sector, where the key roles should be played by both the ecb (providing liquidity to banks or buying government bonds) and the eba, the banking regulator, which should, among other things, guarantee the credibility of the eu banks' stress testing. second, eu fiscal policies should be strengthened by the issuance of eurobonds and the introduction of the ftt (including a stronger role for the ep and national parliaments in this process). third, in the long term, sustainable economic development and the eu's competitiveness will be important. the euro plus pact will need to be strictly adhered to, including an emphasis on the efficiency of the allocation of european funds after 2014, and also the conditions of conditionalities. overall, i believe that the eu has the tools to deal with this situation and that it will be able to successfully tackle this crisis.

in writing. − i welcome the commission's proposal to prevent abuse of the energy market. however, i consider it a mistake that it does not include co2 markets within the eu ets. significant players in the carbon markets, in particular power companies, are also participants in wholesale electricity and gas markets. gas and electricity markets are also linked to and affected by developments in the oil and coal markets. however, oil and coal markets are global in nature and cannot be adequately regulated at eu level. the market participants in this market should be invited to provide information without being subject to controls and enforcement mechanisms. the agency for the cooperation of energy regulators should be provided with sufficient financial and human resources and should be able to establish liaison offices close to the places where significant transactions are concluded. in addition, the agency could in future act as a trade repository for energy-related financial transactions. this would simplify the work of energy traders as they would not have to provide entries in the financial sector trade repository established under the european market infrastructure regulation (emir).
i would like to stress, madam president, first of all, that i consider it of the utmost importance to maintain a coordinated and uniform approach by both the european institutions and the individual member states in the discussion of the various items on the agenda for next year's world radiocommunication conference. this is in particular a common approach to promote the uptake and protection of mobile technologies and services in the context of the exploitation of the digital dividend in the 800 mhz band, to enable the implementation of the sesar project for unified air traffic management and to create the conditions for a faster uptake of mobile technologies for networks referred to as 4g. this will allow the necessary speed increase in access to internet services. there is also a need to create the conditions for the deployment of technologies for the protection of the european galileo satellite navigation system and other air navigation services, for the development and deployment of intelligent transport systems, scientific research and climate monitoring and earth observation services. in this respect, it is also necessary to repress efforts to increase the number of global regulatory measures. ladies and gentlemen, the use of radio spectrum is becoming increasingly global and strategic in terms of the all-round development of society. the outcome of this conference will therefore undoubtedly have a major impact, in particular on the further development of high-speed wireless technologies and services, and will have a practical impact on the quality and conditions of life in our towns and cities.
in writing. the use and management of radio spectrum is a strategic policy and a limited resource. the commission shall be empowered to adopt delegated acts in accordance with article 26 to supplement this regulation by laying down detailed rules for the application of this regulation. the objective of radio spectrum policy is to create a competitive and sustainable wireless broadband industry. the adequate allocation of spectrum, which should be in the interests of the majority of citizens, is problematic. it is easy to be misused by different economic operators and therefore control of its allocation and use is necessary in this context. i welcome the long-term agenda of this policy and its strategic commitment to provide adequate legal certainty at high initial development costs. i support the creation of harmonised frequency bands, the release of spectrum in the 790mhz to 862mhz range for mobile broadband by 2013 and the improvement of conditions for higher capacity frequencies. europe must not forget the further development of the radio spectrum in the wake of the rapid development of conventional mobile communications. it is essential for the european union to keep up with the rest of the world and to remain fully competitive in this area as well.
in writing. the report on the european parliament recommendation to the council, the commission and the european external action service for the negotiation of an association agreement between the european union and the republic of moldova seeks to set priorities for the possible future association of moldova and the launch of deeper cooperation with the eu. in the areas of foreign and security policy, energy and trade cooperation, most of the negotiating chapters have already been successfully concluded. however, further improvement is still hampered by the different standards of moldova compared to the eu. it is requested that a dcfta negotiation programme be established, which should aim at removing barriers to bilateral trade and investment and differences between technical, hygienic and phytosanitary standards. in this context, i welcome the transnational initiatives within the eastern partnership, in particular on customs cooperation. however, the dispute over the transnistrian region and the ongoing internal problems in moldova currently prevent the development of even deeper cooperation. i welcome the report as a whole, as it seeks to assess the value of cooperation between the european union and moldova and also highlights the many difficulties that hinder its further development. for these reasons, i shall vote in favour of this recommendation.
before we come to the first item on this afternoon’s agenda on belarus, i give the floor to véronique de keyer for a very brief but important statement.
mr president, i also want to join the voices that are calling for an ever-better respect for human rights in belarus. every state must abide by the human rights conventions it has signed. i also believe that the european union has a duty to draw attention to any violation of human rights. i too wish the laws were humane and strictly enforced. but don't you think we're still looking in one direction? i would remind you that last may, an israeli commando strike in international waters against a humanitarian convoy killed nine and injured 60 turkish civilians. i recall that in january this year, two people were shot dead by police during anti-government demonstrations in albania, and many others were injured. the protesters demanded new elections. i remind you that in may of this year, several member states of the european union launched an undeclared war against libya. the civilian death toll from the bombing and insurgent actions supported by some of the union states will now be in the hundreds, perhaps thousands. when will the european parliament address these tragedies? doesn't it seem to you that we're succumbing to an organized campaign against belarus at a time when there are other threats? let me make two little technical remarks. firstly, the un declaration on human rights defenders was not adopted in 1988, but on the occasion of the 50th anniversary of the adoption of the universal declaration of human rights, ten years later than the pathetic preamble to the proposed text of the resolution states. you have to be more careful. secondly, i'm sad that there are so few of us here when it comes to human rights. for the ppe group. mr. chairman, the case of prisoner dawit isaak is just the tip of the iceberg of brutal human rights abuses in one of the youngest countries in the world. even in times of economic crisis, we must not give up on the fight for human rights in third countries. the european council did well to follow the un in imposing sanctions on the eritrean regime last year. the eu's ambassador to eritrea, ms paola amadei, should personally request the release of dawito isaak from the eritrean regime, not just because he is a citizen of the european union. only with the joint pressure of all europe will we achieve the release of a man who has not been charged or convicted. of course, let's not forget the thousands of other victims of the eritrean regime. the sanctions that have been implemented so far are insufficient.
