i adjourn the session of the european parliament. (the sitting was closed at 4.40 p.m.)
madam president, commissioner, i think it is quite clear that innovation and technological progress are the driving forces behind all economic progress. however, in the context of the report, two things must, or at least i must, make us think more deeply, two points which i will take the liberty of quoting here and of expressing my opinion on them. first, as regards individual projects and their approval and support: the business plan and market potential are positive factors to be taken into account but not a necessary condition for approval. what else but the market and market enforcement can encourage innovation and invention? what parameters other than profit are to be taken into account? there's nothing derogatory about the words "profit" and "marketing". on the contrary, they are the engines of overall progress. secondly, and i've just run out of time, which i'm very sorry about, but i'm going to respect the one minute i've been given.
mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, tourism generates about 10% of the european union's gdp and employs some 12 million workers. in addition to these figures, it should be noted that european destinations are regularly among the most visited and also the most popular countries. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's policy on tourism and the development of the tourism sector. in particular, the bric countries are already showing an increase in travel to the european union. data published by eurostat show that visits from the russian federation increased by 18%, from china by 19%, and from brazil by 46% compared to the first half of 2009 and 2010. china has the most potential of these countries in the long run. the country of 1.3 billion people increased its outbound tourism spending by 21 percent in 2009, according to world tourism organization statistics. however, in order to fully exploit this growing potential, we need to provide tourists with easier visa policies, such as tourist group visas, and to promote individual tourism. at the same time, the seasonal nature of tourism needs to be phased out and the europe brand introduced as a year-round destination that can offer both natural, cultural and industrial heritage. i am very pleased that the commission has taken this step, and i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr fidanza, for his excellent report.
in writing. tourism accounts for 10% of the european union's gdp and provides jobs and livelihoods for 12% of european citizens. the aim of the project is to develop a european tourism strategy that will enable tourism to be more effective in its contribution to the development of the european economy. to promote the sustainability and competitiveness of european tourism, it is necessary to work on the education and training of people working in the tourism sector and to invest in innovation and new technologies. in this context, i consider the erasmus for young entrepreneurs programme to be a suitable tool for developing new skills and abilities of young people who should then be able not only to provide for themselves but also to create jobs for others in their environment. it is essential for european tourism to gradually extend its seasonality. the calypso programme could be an appropriate means of ensuring access to tourism in the off-season for certain categories of persons (young, elderly, disabled). in the present rushed times, there will be an increasing interest in rural and agro-tourism, nature tourism and spa and health tourism in the future. we need to be prepared for this change and european tourism offers adequate leisure opportunities not only to european citizens.
in writing. tourism is one of the most important factors in the tertiary sector and is a sector whose absorption capacity has not yet been adequately appreciated by the individual countries of the european union. it is good that more and more actors are involved in this sector and that the european commission and the european parliament have recently paid attention to it. in particular, the changing composition of the population, whether in europe or worldwide, brings a number of new opportunities for tourism. there are significant groups (disabled, retired) that place special demands on tourism operators. in recent years, there has been a lot of talk about social tourism, and new customer segments are emerging in developing countries. all this places great demands on the eu as one of the most important destinations in terms of improving the skills structure of the sector's workforce, promoting the diversification of the tourist offer and coordinating the development of the entire sector. the 21 steps outlined in the fidanza report can provide a good basis for concrete action by individual member states and for coordinated efforts at european union level. in particular, new 'trademarks' and innovations in the packages offered for various types of 'roads', whether combined transport or cycling, road, rail or water transport, can be a significant impetus for further development of the sector. the eu must step up its efforts to maintain its position as the world's number one destination.
in writing. the report seeks to provide a very comprehensive overview of the experience gained during the current programming period with the mobilisation of funds under cohesion policy. the rapporteur, in my view, has very rightly identified the three fundamental shortcomings on the part of cohesion policy beneficiaries. this is due to a lack of financial resources for co-financing, a lack of human resources and a lack of administrative support necessary for the proper use of the available funds. the rapporteur rightly points out the difficulties at the beginning of each new programming period, which are accompanied by difficulties in the implementation of the management and control system, which is usually caused by delays in the development and implementation of eu and member state rules or related guidelines and by incomplete or inaccurate rules for the use of cohesion policy funds. very often, the problems are caused by insufficient allocation of competences between the authorities in the member states, problems with the hierarchy of the institutions and internal problems with the allocation of tasks and responsibilities. i personally find it very important that the rapporteur points out that there is a need to focus more on the fight against fraud than on the fight against formal irregularities. in conclusion, i would like to underline the crucial importance of the timely adoption of the multiannual financial framework and of clear and definitive rules and guidelines for the member states. the experience presented in the report is also relevant for the czech republic.
i supported mrs gardini's report because it strengthens coordination in the areas of preparation, capacity planning and implementation of both civil protection and humanitarian aid. this means, in particular, closer cooperation with ngos, which are often present at the scene of disasters, in the case of humanitarian aid. the logistics sector is a particularly pressing problem in the rapid and joint response to the crisis, and i would welcome concrete suggestions for improvement. i also see a path forward in linking existing monitoring centres and mapping member states' capacities for different types of crises. at the same time, however, i am convinced that greater eu coordination must not 'compete' with the un's global coordinating role, but should, on the contrary, support it. the responsibility of member states, in particular in external humanitarian aid, should also be emphasised. i therefore consider it important that european emergency response capacities are for the time being under the sole command of the sending member state or the affected member state, respectively, and not under the command of the european emergency response centre. as regards the visibility of eu symbols in aid, i only wish that it should be kept within reasonable limits and not consume a significant part of the allocated funds.
i supported the report by rapporteur koch on the european road safety strategy 2011-2020 because the safety situation on european roads remains critical and requires a rapid and, above all, a consistent response. every year, more than 35 000 people die on european roads and 1.5 million are seriously injured. i therefore support this own-initiative report and call on the commission to come forward with concrete legislative proposals in this area as soon as possible. we should focus on improving safety on second and third class roads in europe, where more than 70% of road accidents occur on average every year. i also support the rapporteur's objective, the so-called vision zero, which is to end fatal accidents on european roads. if we cannot prevent accidents from happening, let us at least prevent them from being fatal. 'vision zero' applies to air and rail transport, and i fully support the introduction of this ambition in road transport.
in writing. recommendation for a council decision on the conclusion of the agreement between the eu and the palestinian authority of the west bank and the gaza strip on the liberalization of trade in agricultural products, processed agricultural products, fish and fishery products, establishing duty-free and quota-free access to the european market for these products imported from the territory of the palestinian authority. in contrast, some eu products will have duty-free access to the palestinian market, but will be subject to ceiling quotas for their import. i understand that the main objective of this agreement is to promote the development of the palestinian economy, to facilitate the possibility of exporting palestinian products to the eu and thus to help improve the economic development of the region. that's why i accept inequality of conditions. in this context, it is necessary to ensure that the rules on the country of origin of products are not abused in the application of the agreement, given that european citizens require to know the origin of the products they consume. in its judgments, the european court of justice considers the gaza strip and the west bank to be palestinian territory, i.e. that israel cannot apply the products of israeli settlements in these areas as israeli goods. we certainly cannot try to resolve the palestinian-israeli issue in this report, which is primarily aimed at helping farmers and fishermen in the area to export and sell products legally. the report as a whole is a contribution, which is why i shall vote in favour of its adoption.
in writing. − i believe that the common strategic framework should not be limited to research and technology-based innovation, but should encourage different sources of innovation. many companies, especially smes, tap into other sources of innovation such as customers, markets, users and, not least, employees, and this form of innovation is often more practical and focused on addressing specific issues related to processes, services or products. the solutions proposed are often found by the employees closest to the production process, markets and customers. the eu should therefore foster innovation that is practical and based on the experience and input of employees. collaborative research funding should offer greater thematic flexibility and user-friendly funding mechanisms to attract top scientists for projects and to respond to the needs of large and small groups. this should cover the whole innovation chain, from research activities to large-scale pilot projects and demonstration activities. i also express my doubts about the effectiveness of the use of funds for the creation of research networks of excellence and the organisation of conferences and events, and i support, on the contrary, the strengthening of measures for the electronic interconnection of research and innovation and the dissemination of research results via the internet.
in writing. the report seeks to highlight current problems with european schools, which are set up to provide education for the children of european union officials. this specific education system allows students to study all subjects in a multicultural and multilingual environment and with qualified teachers, while maintaining their mother tongue. european schools are a great example of education based on a proven approach to teaching and are an example of one of the best possible forms of teaching in europe based on the dissemination of european culture, values and languages. one of the current challenges is the need for greater compatibility of european schools' curricula with national education systems in order to facilitate the rapid integration of students returning to their countries of origin. the report also seeks to address the unfortunate situation in which the european schools currently find themselves, in a legal vacuum, which is manifested by the unclear legal and jurisdictional status of acts adopted by the bodies of these schools, the lack of possibilities to challenge these acts before national courts and the impossibility of appealing to the european ombudsman. i supported the report for the reasons i have given.
in writing. in the context of the future form of cohesion policy, attention should be focused, inter alia, on public-private partnerships at regional and local level, which, due to their flexibility, could solve co-financing difficulties. the commission should examine the existence and functionality of the legal framework for the implementation of public and private sector projects and, where appropriate, recommend to member states which have not yet adopted such a legal framework, to develop and adopt as soon as possible procedures for their effective implementation at regional and local level, taking into account the possibility of drawing on the structural funds and the cohesion fund in the next programming period for these projects. i find it worrying that most smes cannot draw on the structural funds themselves, because of the current administrative and financial obstacles and because they need support and advice from their representative organisations. i consider simplification of rules and procedures to be a necessary condition for smes to have access to the structural funds. the small business act and its 'think small first' and 'once only' principles and the principle of proportionality should be applied at all levels of decision-making to define investment priorities and the design of management, audit and control procedures to ensure better use of funds.
in writing. − i believe that we should first of all build on existing monitoring and prevention tools, such as on-site monitoring facilities and the tools of the european global monitoring for environment and security (gmes) or the inspire and galileo programmes, which can monitor potentially vulnerable areas in order to prepare more effectively for providing assistance to disaster victims. the commission should assess the possibility of equipping the eu with its own secure telecommunications capacity and integrated crisis management solutions, from prevention to recovery. i see considerable potential in using existing telecommunications satellite capacity to facilitate the development of public safety and emergency services for citizens, in cooperation with the european space agency, member states and stakeholders such as private operators or industry. it is the commission's task, in particular by means of telecommunications, including satellite communications, to create a communication and information system that would provide rescue teams with rapid access to accurate information enabling them to effectively recover from the consequences of disaster events. given that disaster prevention is often more cost-effective than disaster response, a comprehensive and innovative eu strategy for disaster risk reduction is needed and sufficient resources should be allocated to the early identification of potential disasters.
in writing. − setting criteria and involving mechanisms to make the supervision of dual-use goods more effective are one part of this issue. i would therefore like to remind you that you should also not forget about improving the quality of information and training of traders in this type of goods. to ensure that sufficient up-to-date and understandable information based on good legislation is available and that procedures are clear to all stakeholders: control authorities, traders and others. in conclusion, i would like to reiterate that transparency, conceptuality and efficiency should be the basis.
in writing. - personally, i'm a big advocate of improving road safety. unfortunately, i am not convinced of the correctness of the proposed measures to improve road safety. i am very concerned about the proposals to implement intelligent speed assistance system technology, which is only a short step away from being deployed to build a big brother. i would hate to see some central european brain dictating which communications the driver can take, or dictating the speed at which he drives. i'm concerned about the idea of technology taking responsibility for the decisions and actions of individuals. although the idea of having drivers take a breathalyzer test before they start driving is useful, i see the mandatory installation of alcohol locks in cars as a bad joke from a sci-fi movie. the questions that the mandatory deployment of these technologies would raise, in particular the resolution of liability for the malfunctioning of these systems, are not answered at all. we must also not forget the increased costs that these technologies entail. for example, for low-income groups, buying a new car will become even less affordable than it is now. we must also not forget the impact that the mandatory deployment of these technologies will have on smes. i believe that more than these technologies, education, raising safety standards and improving transport infrastructure can contribute to greater road safety.
in writing. the world and the balance of power in international trade are changing dramatically. while the eu accounted for 25% of world gdp in 2000, it is forecast to account for only 18% by 2020. in contrast, china and india, the two countries with the largest populations, continue to grow, both in terms of gdp and population. to remain a global player and thus a co-determining force in the future, it is essential that the eu responds to these changes and develops a long-term strategy for european trade policy. the report on trade policy in the context of the europe 2020 strategy critically assesses the commission's communication of november 2010. i agree with the rapporteur that this communication is more a set of guidelines than a comprehensive eu strategy for trade policy that could meet the challenges of a changing world economy. the ep adopted the 'global europe' strategy, which is based on the principle of 'europe for europe'. the objectives of this strategy have not yet been achieved. i welcome and endorse the rapporteur's call to the commission and the member states. an analysis is needed to identify why many of the targets have not been met and to draw conclusions from this. we endorse a number of opinions, statements and guidelines without any retroactive consideration of their functioning and effectiveness. but in the field of international trade, it is a necessity. i fully support the report presented by mr caspary.
for the ecr group. mr. chairman, my question will be very simple. we have eurozone members in the european union, we have non-eurozone members. there should be rules for eurozone members, because the euro is simply not for everyone. the euro requires certain criteria to be met. my country is not a member of the eurozone, but i certainly do not want the european single currency to collapse. so i ask you, do you accept the possibility that in order to save the single european currency, one or more countries will eventually have to leave the european monetary union in order to devalue their own currency and emerge from the economic crisis, because that is not possible in the current situation? so the question is whether you allow the possibility of either voluntary or involuntary exit of one or more countries from the euro area.
for the ecr group. i thank you, i was expecting something like this, mr. president. so let me ask you a second question. the second question is directed at non-euro area members. do you accept the possibility that those countries that are not yet members of the euro area and do not have a negotiated permanent exemption like the uk does, should be given the opportunity to reconsider their desire to join the euro area in the current situation? because countries like my country signed the accession agreement, where they committed themselves to adopt the euro, in a very different situation than the one we have today.
for the group gue/ngl. ladies and gentlemen, i think that everything that contributes to the development of peaceful relations and cooperation in europe should be supported. while i am not sure of the transparency of the motives of the architects of the eastern partnership concept, i would like to believe that the upcoming summit will strengthen european unity and not lead to the creation of some new sanitary cordon around russia. i would like the european union negotiators to pack their suitcases before they go to warsaw with a commitment to unite europe from ireland to the urals and to help solve problems such as the low growth of some of the partner countries. and also a commitment to help resolve disputes like the one over nagorno-karabakh. i would greatly appreciate it if the negotiators for the european union would learn to listen to their partners and not be compelled to advise them all the time.
madam president, allow me to speak on the subject of the growing tension between turkey and the republic of cyprus as the coordinator of the parliamentary group at the highest level for relations with the turkish cypriot community in the northern part of the island of cyprus, which is unfortunately still divided. as commissioner füle has mentioned, negotiations between the highest representatives of greek cypriots and turkish cypriots on reunification have been ongoing for more than two years, so far without success. these negotiations, which are being actively assisted by un secretary-general ban ki-moon and his special envoy, alexander downer, are entering their final phase in the coming days and weeks. and there's a lot at stake. either a mutually satisfactory and reasonable solution to this longstanding dispute is found, or the changes in the middle east will lead to a final and permanent partition of cyprus with fatal consequences for both greek and turkish cypriots. i would therefore like to appeal again from this place to the representatives of both communities, president christofias and dr eroğlu, to concentrate on the successful conclusion of the negotiations and not to take any steps that would lead to further escalation of tensions. it is in the interest of both communities to reach a lasting and reasonable compromise so that the cypriots can jointly decide the fate of cyprus, including its mineral wealth in the coastal waters.
on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, how are you, president barroso, how do you feel? i must admit i feel a lot of sympathy for you because i genuinely believe that you are aware that the situation is serious. at the same time, i have to admit that i do not share many of your views. i agree with you that fiscal consolidation, curbing of public expenditure and financial rigour are necessary because markets are very sensitive and markets at the moment do not forgive any mistake. however, good intentions are not enough. let us make one thing clear: the euro currency was imposed on states of different development, different purchasing power, different gdp per capita, different living standards, different budgeting and different levels of competitiveness. it was done purely for political and ideological reasons because the euro should have served as a tool for further integration, not primarily as a single currency per se. (interjection from mr cohn-bendit) then came public overspending, lack of discipline, and this directed some countries to the verge of collapse. in normal situations at such a time countries would devalue their currencies to cope with those consequences but, of course, as members of the eurozone, they cannot do that because they are not allowed to do so. so the only solution remaining are firstly bail-outs, more bail-outs and even more bail-outs, or whatever you want to call them, and secondly, transition of monetary union into a fiscal union and, thus, most importantly the debt union. the logic of the first step, the bail-out step, is that the debts of some will be paid by the others, and the second step will mean a further shift of national power to the european level and further diminishing of the role of member states. mr verhofstadt might like it, i very much doubt that most members of his own group like it as much as he does but, again, the most important thing is whether the people of europe like it and whether it will help to promote more pro-european thinking amongst european nations. if you think that it does, you are wrong, ladies and gentlemen. i can tell you in my own country a recent poll has shown that 75% of people are against joining the euro, and do you really think that this is a result of some anti-european propaganda? no, it is not a result of anti-european propaganda, it is just a result of people who see and think. they simply do not buy into this idea any more of a federal europe – that is very simple. i ask you frankly, president barroso, do you concede that it may come to a situation where, as the only solution to the current problems, it might be that one or more of the eurozone members would have to leave the monetary union? secondly, would you admit that some other still non-eurozone members could re-evaluate their thoughts about joining the eurozone or not? please do not get me wrong. if it comes to that, my feeling is that it will not mean the fall of the euro, because none of us wishes that, but the mentality of the euro at all costs is precisely the way to destroy the euro. i understand that, if it comes to a reduction of the eurozone, that might be a political disaster for those who invested a great deal of their political capital into that idea, into the idea of a united states of europe, but that is their problem. president barroso, i can agree with you when you stressed the necessity to reinforce competitiveness, to complete the single market, to improve the legal background. believe me, you achieve none of these things with an old and outdated eu framework. mr schuman, mr monnet, they were undoubtedly nice guys but their ideas are 50 years old. so please let us stop thinking in these terms because now, president barroso, unfortunately for you, you have almost no choice between bad decisions and even worse decisions. for instance, another ‘worse decision’ you came with, which you have just mentioned, is the financial transaction tax. it sounds fine at first glance, just a small fee for transactions, but in the real world such a tax will lead to only one conclusion: that countries and institutions will seriously reconsider their location and they will leave the european market and this will undermine further the european economy. it is very simple. we need a new eu paradigm for the 21st century. unfortunately, there is very little chance, almost no chance, of this new paradigm being born in this very building, in this very assembly. the longer i am here, the more i feel that this assembly is more a part of the problem rather than a part of the solution. i am sorry to say that, i am sorry for you president barroso, but anyway i wish you good luck.
after the berlin events of 1953, when the politburo of the sed issued a statement that the people had failed the politburo, bertolt brecht stated that he recommended the politburo to create another people that would not fail his trust. i have the impression from mr barros's and other speakers' speeches that the european states, the european peoples, have betrayed the commission's trust. and i therefore recommend to president barros that he create a different european people who will not betray his trust.
mr president, i had the honour of hearing your report and i would like to thank the commission as a whole for the work they have done under your leadership. you're doing a hard job in a tough time. i must also say that your report has given me a great deal of concern, alongside some positive feelings. as regards the future of the union, you mentioned the need for further integration steps. however, your next point, that not all the countries of the union will probably be able to proceed at the same speed in such a strengthening of cooperation, is already striking and even unacceptable to me. i understand that the commission is committed to the two-speed europe model, that it wants to continue to divide europeans into first and second-class citizens. i think that institutions like the european parliament or the european commission are supposed to unite europe, not divide it. i don't want to be a second-class citizen, and i don't want to be second-class. i therefore urge the commission to prepare, first and foremost, a model for a future europe that is united, not fragmented. only such a europe can succeed in global competition, can have competitive jobs, production and currency.
i voted in favour of this motion for a resolution, perhaps for the same reasons as those which my colleague has just mentioned. sexual orientation and gender identity continue to be the cause of serious human rights violations in a number of countries. persons belonging to this minority are victims of increased discrimination and ill-treatment. in many countries, sexual orientation and gender identity are not only grounds for criminal prosecution, but also grounds for inhuman treatment, torture and even the imposition of the death penalty, which is totally unacceptable to us. such violations of human rights are incompatible with the fundamental principles of the european union and the universal declaration of human rights, according to which every person should have equal rights without any discrimination. i therefore also welcome the united nations resolution, the first of its kind to specifically address human rights violations based on sexual orientation and gender identity, thus demonstrating the universality of human rights. i support open dialogue in this area and believe that the european union should take a unified approach to the application of human rights in the conduct of its external and internal activities.
in writing. − member states whose currency is the euro should pursue economic policies that promote the proper functioning of economic and monetary union and avoid policies that threaten it. in order to ensure the proper functioning of economic and monetary union, the treaty allows for specific measures to be adopted in the euro area beyond the provisions applicable to all member states. in my view, additional sanctions are needed to make the enforcement of budgetary surveillance in the euro area more effective. those sanctions should enhance the credibility of the framework for fiscal supervision in the union. sanctions for member states whose currency is the euro under the preventive arm of the stability and growth pact should provide incentives for prudent fiscal policies. this policy should ensure that the growth rate of public expenditure does not exceed, under normal circumstances, the carefully planned medium-term growth rate of gdp. i appreciate that under the corrective arm of the stability and growth pact, the penalty for a member state whose currency is the euro is an obligation to make an interest-free deposit in the event of a council decision on the existence of an excessive deficit and an obligation to pay a fine in the event of non-compliance with the council recommendation to address the excessive deficit. i consider it useful that, if the commission decides to impose a sanction, that decision can only be reversed by a majority of member states.
in june this year, the ep adopted a legislative package containing six proposals to create a more complete framework for the management of eu economic affairs, which should lead to greater macroeconomic stability in the member states in the long term. this should also be achieved by involving the ep and national parliaments more closely in economic policy coordination procedures. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the application of the principle of equal treatment for men and women in matters of employment, vocational training and promotion, and working conditions, is based on the principle of equal treatment for men and women. the system should be designed in such a way as to prevent asymmetric shocks and also to contribute to sustainable growth and job creation, and thus be in line with the europe 2020 strategy. although the final determination of the amount of the fine can sometimes be problematic, its amount and the way it is enforced have been established on the basis of a sensitive general political consensus. however, i have reservations about the proposed method of the possibility of imposing an interest-bearing advance before the actual sanction is imposed. overall, the report presented by mr carl haglund, which relates to the implementation of mrs ferreira's report, is a step in the right direction, but the interest-bearing advance appears to be problematic and would merit further discussion. i shall therefore abstain.
in writing. − i am of the opinion that the coordination of the economic policies of the member states within the union, as provided for in the treaty, should include respect for the main principles, including stable prices, sound public finances and monetary conditions and a sustainable balance of payments. it is certainly appropriate to complement the multilateral surveillance referred to in article 121 (3) and (4) of the treaty with specific rules for the detection, prevention and correction of macroeconomic imbalances. i agree that this approach will rely on an early warning mechanism that will detect emerging macroeconomic imbalances in a timely manner and will be based on the use of an indicative and transparent comparative overview combined with economic judgment. the monitoring and correction of macroeconomic imbalances with preventive and corrective elements requires enhanced control tools based on those used in the multilateral surveillance procedure. this could include, for example, enhanced monitoring missions by the commission in member states and further reporting by member states in the event of serious imbalances or imbalances threatening the proper functioning of economic and monetary union.
in writing. − the stability and growth pact is based on the objective of sound public finances, which is a means of strengthening the conditions for price stability and for strong sustainable growth supported by financial stability, which will contribute to job creation. i believe that the rules on budgetary discipline should be strengthened, in particular by placing greater emphasis on the level and evolution of debt and overall sustainability. the determination of the existence of an excessive deficit on the basis of the debt criterion and the procedure for that determination should, in my view, not be oriented exclusively towards non-compliance with the numerical reference value, but should always take into account all the decisive factors addressed in the commission's report under article 126 (3) of the treaty. the commission report under article 126 (3) of the treaty should take due account of the quality of the national fiscal framework, which plays a crucial role in promoting fiscal consolidation and sustainable public finances. in order to monitor compliance with the council recommendations and calls for the correction of the excessive deficit, i believe that they should set annual budgetary targets consistent with the required fiscal improvement after the adjustment from cyclical effects and without one-off and temporary measures.
in writing. the ep resolution is very general, accusing member states of having human rights violations related to sexual orientation and gender identity on a daily basis, but fails to give a single example. both the un report and this ep resolution are not binding documents, but it is a pity that these declaratory texts show so many errors and superficial judgments, misquote documents, deliberately remove certain rights from the human rights record and fail to give examples of violations. for these reasons, i did not support the resolution. moreover, there is no reason for such a resolution at this time.
the debate is closed. written statements (rule 149)
in writing. the 'food for free' programme has been helping people at risk of poverty for 20 years. the czech republic is one of the countries which do not support the draft regulation. in its opinion, it states that these issues require national rather than european social policy responses, including future regulations on the mobilisation of the european social fund. allowing food to be bought on the market, and thus moving away from the former main objective of the efficient use of union intervention stocks, has already put this issue off the agenda of the common agricultural policy. unfortunately, this opinion does not offer a timely solution for a programme that helps 18 million of the poorest every year. i believe that this is a fact that all actors should bear in mind when looking for a way out of the current impasse.

commissioner potočnik, i wish you good luck and success in new delhi.
in writing. despite article 194 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union, which gives member states the right to set the conditions for the use of their energy resources, the right to choose between different energy sources and to determine the basic composition of their energy supply, the green group succeeded in getting point 46 of the draft resolution on the drafting of the eu common position for the negotiations of the united nations conference on sustainable development rio+20 to be included in the second part, which calls for the phasing out of existing nuclear power plants and the halting of the construction of new nuclear power plants. such a proposal is unacceptable not only for the czech republic, which is and will be heavily dependent on nuclear energy. other member states are also counting on nuclear power and any restriction would increase dependence on fossil fuels and greenhouse gas emissions. if europe is to meet its binding climate change targets, it is necessary to use all available energy sources, including nuclear. there are risks associated with a number of other industries, and yet we do not ban oil or chemical production. instead, there is an effort to ensure the highest standards of safety and environmental protection by all available means. the rational discussion in the field of nuclear energy should focus on ensuring maximum safety and on the management of radioactive waste and spent fuel.
mr president, the ecr group will not support the proposal on the globalisation adjustment fund. the globalisation adjustment fund is a redistribution tool, which is taking money from the successful, using some of it, and allocating the rest to the unsuccessful according to our rules. success and failure are a natural part of the market. it's tempting for us politicians to give money to someone. often we forget that we are taking money from others. so instead of the successful person growing their business, they give some of their money to the unsuccessful. it is not a solidarity between the strong and the weak or the disadvantaged. practice has shown that the beneficiaries are usually very strong market players. from the very beginning of the fund, there have been doubts about where these funds are going. instead of globalisation, they often address migration between member countries and instead of the consequences of the crisis, they support planned restructuring.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i do not support the european commission's proposal. it appears that the member states most affected by the crisis have not benefited from the european globalisation adjustment fund. while countries with unemployment rates that were high above the eu average during the crisis did not in principle submit applications, countries that did not have more severe labour market problems benefited significantly from the fund. moreover, it should be noted that the interest in drawing on this fund has declined significantly. while there were 23 applications last year, there were only 7 this year before the holidays. this indicates that the financial crisis has now passed and the current economic difficulties are no longer the consequence of the original crisis of 2008 and 2009, but are the result of a debt crisis, which the european globalisation adjustment fund is not aiming to address. let's also admit that the globalisation adjustment fund is tied up in a lot of red tape. if we really want to continue to tackle problems of this kind with european funds, which i personally do not support, let us use the european social fund to do so.
mr president, the ongoing, alarming and daunting economic situation both in the eurozone and outside makes a serious case for decisive repudiation of the existence of the european globalisation adjustment fund. it has actually been functioning at the expense of those taxpayers who, despite the economic and financial crisis, survived within fierce global competition. having said that, i am obliged to say that pernicious consequences of the existence of this fund increase the level of redistribution, create market distortions and punish the successful. that is why i disagree with its existence.
colleagues, before we start the vote i have an announcement concerning the situation in syria. the european parliament remains gravely concerned about the situation in syria. brutal violence against the people, arbitrary arrests and detentions are unacceptable. over recent days we have also been following the case of rafah nashed, a well known syrian doctor who works with victims of psychological trauma and who sought a constructive dialogue between all syrians. she was arrested over two weeks ago for no reason at all. by no means is she a political activist. her arrest is totally inadmissible. the place of her detention remains unknown and there are fears that her health has seriously deteriorated over the past weeks. the syrian authorities must release rafah nashed immediately, as well as all the other people who have been arbitrarily detained and arrested. mr president, we voted against the report on the future of the european globalisation adjustment fund. i think this fund is completely useless and should be abolished. aid to people affected by globalisation is and should continue to be provided at national rather than european level. the functioning of this fund in the european union creates inequality between people, because only large companies can get money from it. but small businesses and entrepreneurs are also affected by globalisation and cannot be helped by the fund. that's why i don't support the operation of this fund.
in writing. i voted against paragraph 46 of the motion for a resolution tabled by the members of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, even with the support of the czech member of parliament, mr miroslav ouzky. the commission has also proposed that the eu should be given the right to adopt or maintain a decision on the renewal of nuclear power stations. i've long advocated the highest safety standards in nuclear power. but i reject the anti-nuclear hysteria, which is being stirred up by green politicians in particular in connection with the fukushima disaster. this hysteria completely ignores the implications of the anti-nuclear bill. i think it is populist and hypocritical that countries which block nuclear power generation on their territory are dependent on the supply of this very type of energy from neighbouring countries.
in writing. − i am satisfied that the egf was able to help around 10% of all workers made redundant in the eu in 2009-2010 and that 40% of workers who were eligible for egf support in 2009 were successfully reintegrated into the labour market despite the negative effects of the financial and economic crisis on the labour market. however, i do not share the commission's intention to continue the egf beyond the current mff. the egf should be closely linked to the european framework for restructuring in order to enhance its contribution to effectively supporting the training and retraining of workers to re-integrate into the labour market in difficult labour market situations resulting from unforeseeable restructuring of firms or sectors that causes or exacerbates mismatches in qualifications with job requirements. such an instrument would be significantly complementary to esf-funded measures, which are aimed primarily at adapting to global challenges with a view to a sustainably growing economy. this instrument would ensure, on the one hand, eu solidarity with workers adversely affected by restructuring and, on the other, that all member states could benefit from its timely and tailor-made intervention aimed at preventing long-term unemployment.
for the ecr group. mr. chairman, i was thinking about what kind of tie to wear today, whether to wear a black tie for a funeral, i ended up wearing a gray one. maybe the black one will come up next time. but i have to say that the situation is serious, and that you are basically offering nothing different from what the nation states that you criticize so much here offer. you are offering to buy out the european union together with those who have over-indebted themselves, who have behaved irresponsibly, whether it be banks or even whole countries, and i am telling you that the european economy simply does not have the means to do that. no eurovalu is tall enough to handle this. you start this, you'll never finish. and secondly, you offer a european economic government. so you are proposing the de facto abolition of the nation state, and i think that can only be proposed by a politician who has no idea what the mood is in the member states. look at finland, look at slovakia, look at the netherlands. i think people are sick of it, mr. chairman. this is all just a warm-up for the boiler, and the boiler's gonna blow. the only right solution, the only fair solution is an economic solution, i.e. to proceed on a case-by-case basis, to allow for managed bankruptcies, to allow for devaluation, to reduce the eurozone, and for that, courage is needed. it takes courage to do that, mr. chairman, because you don't need that much courage to do the other thing. if we do not summon the courage, if the european institutions do not recover, then, ladies and gentlemen, this european parliament will be good for one thing only, to have a beethoven song sung here, a piece that is claimed to be the anthem of europe. we're not good for anything else.
madam high commissioner, the massacre of christians in cairo on sunday, which was carried out by the egyptian army, which is the de facto ruling force in egypt today, is completely unacceptable. i was surprised that in your statement on monday you did not explicitly condemn it as a criminal course that egypt must not take under any circumstances. i agree with you that there is a need to investigate the case and bring the specific culprits to justice. but since this is not the only excess against the copts, it is not enough. it's a political act. i would ask you to warn the egyptian leadership that our european support will be uncompromisingly conditional on ensuring genuine freedom of religion and that the islamization of the copts is a crime. a crime that the union's elites must take a strong stand against and not allow to be relativized. otherwise, we lose respect.
