for the ecr group. madam president, allow me first to address your colleague, mr olle schmidt, and ask him to convey to mrs bowles a thank you for her cooperation in this matter. this is not, i would say, a politically correct thank you, it's a human thank you for the fact that mrs bowles really did her best to take into account all the views of the individual shadow rapporteurs, and i appreciate that very much. on behalf of the european conservatives group, i agree with the increase in the additional shares and i am pleased that our comments calling for greater control of the bank by the european parliament have become part of the final compromise even after the trilogue negotiations. i am also satisfied that the final compromise on the definition of tax havens was derived from the oecd definition. we believe that the additional capital will be used wisely and support structural development in countries that need it.
madam president, i would like to raise three points. the first is to offer my congratulations and thanks to mr casa. this is not political correctness but a reflection of my personal feeling. mr casa, i know that, as an mep, you have devoted yourself for almost seven years to the problems of vat and, as far as i know, you know what you are talking about. however – and this brings me to my second point and one of my concerns – i fear that the strategy of so-called green vat, which would reduce rates for energy-efficient and environmentally friendly products and services, is likely to result in tax fraud. we all agree that fraud is unacceptable, so we should not ignore that possibility. my time is up. i will speak to mr casa personally about my third point.
madam president, i appreciate that the report pays considerable attention to the question of eliminating bureaucracy in european legislation governing vat, in particular reducing the frequency of regular vat returns. europe's population is ageing, with implications for labour markets, savings, changes in consumption patterns and the stability of public budgets. the need to focus on restructuring tax systems is clear and obvious. in this restructuring, we will have to repress the importance of direct taxation in favour of indirect taxation. but i must warn against attempts to shift responsibility for reforming tax systems from the member states to the european union. tax systems and their parameters must remain the responsibility of the member states. attempts at vat harmonisation must be firmly rejected in their embryonic form. vat harmonisation would be only the first step towards the creation of a single tax administration. for europe, it is, in the words of the classic, "a road to hell".
the functioning of the internal market, of which an efficient tax system is an important part, is also a key tool for the eu's competitiveness. for this reason, the discussion on a common vat system in the eu is a step in the right direction, see the commission's green paper on the future of vat of 1 december 2010. i therefore welcome the commission's intention to build a comprehensive vat system that would remove the vulnerability of the current system to fraud, would be sufficiently stable and resistant to fraud estimated in the eu at up to eur 100 billion per year and would also contribute to increasing the transparency and competitiveness of european businesses. the vat system in new zealand could provide some inspiration for the eu. for social reasons, i consider it important in the reform to preserve the discretion of the member states in deciding on the amount of the reduced rate of vat on selected goods. the report also contains promising conclusions and proposals (broad-based vat, a place-based vat system, vat collection methods such as a limited data warehouse model combined with a certified taxable person model). overall, the report presented by mr david casy provides a detailed analysis of the issue, refers to the relevant legislation and also contains relevant recommendations on the future of vat in the eu.
in writing. in addition to its core mission of financing investment in the eu, the european investment bank (eib) is also involved in lending outside the eu, which at the end of 2010 represented eur 9 billion, about 12% of total lending. the eib was discussed in the ep on 17 february 2011 in the context of the adopted resolution p7_ta-prov(2011) 0062, which i also supported, including 5 amendments. the most important changes relate to the increase of the ceilings by eur 1 857 million and the reinvestments of the eib, the eu objectives and their conditionality in accordance with article 208 tfeu, the 2005 paris declaration and also the 2008 accra action programme, transparency and reporting e.g. emphasis on development impact analysis, environmental and social sustainability, eib performance indicators on development, environment and human rights, etc. and longer-term prospects, e.g. for the provision of micro-credit under the new financial framework. the proposed extended eib mandate should allow for better coordination in achieving eu objectives, allow for greater accountability and transparency and better use of eu budgetary guarantees. overall, the report presented by mr kalfin follows up on the previous ep decision and reflects the proposed amendments, and i therefore recommend that it be approved as presented.
in writing. − effective and clearly defined control measures on the import, export and transit of these goods are part of the security issue. in this context, i would like to remind you that the improvement of the working conditions of the relevant inspection bodies should not be forgotten. to have a well-developed and comprehensible legislation at their disposal. i see the legislative measures being prepared and some of those already in place as an attempt to improve the supervision of trade in this type of goods, to improve the effectiveness of control systems and, in particular, to combat the illegal handling of this material.
in writing. - the free movement of persons is one of the fundamental values of the european union. i voted in favour of the resolution because i do not think it is acceptable in general to have a double standard for different countries within the framework of commonly agreed rules. whether it's the rules of the schengen area or other issues.
madam president, i welcome this report and i do not agree with the view that it is a matter for the individual member states, because whenever we try to take action at european level in the social field, some people start shouting 'subsidiarity, tradition' and we know what the consequences are. in the czech republic, for example, all schemes that enable the employment of people with disabilities are to be scrapped. the creation of protected workspaces will be prevented, and the disabled will be much worse off. this is not just a situation in my country, but also in other member states, and it is therefore necessary, if we do not want to lose the confidence of the people in the european union, to act together.
ladies and gentlemen, promoting the inclusion of people with reduced mobility is one of the priorities of the eu 2020 strategy. the european union also ratified the un convention on the rights of persons with disabilities in january this year. this move is undoubtedly a sign of the eu's determination to build a barrier-free europe for some 80 million people. however, the achievement of the broadly defined objectives must be based on concrete practical steps at both the european union and the member states' level, and above all at the level of local communities. in the european parliament's committee on transport and tourism, we have long sought to ensure that people with disabilities have more accessible and tailored information about their rights to facilitate mobility. as the shadow rapporteur for the white paper on transport, i support the european commission's presentation as soon as possible of a document summarising the rights of passengers in all modes of transport, with a separate chapter on disabled people in the document. there are thousands of daily obstacles that people with disabilities have to overcome. i believe that access to information in an appropriate form is one of the prerequisites for better integration of people with disabilities into society and for removing barriers to better mobility.
madam president, commissioner, when we talk about public procurement, we also have to talk about the bottlenecks of public procurement. i think the bottleneck is the excessive bureaucracy, the legal certainty, the lack of transparency and also the lack of standardization and standardization in the awarding. i think that in this respect we should thank rapporteur rühle for the fact that the report is moving in the right direction and is a promise for future legislation. but what bothers me, as it does my colleague engel, is that only 5% of public procurement contracts are awarded electronically at european level. in doing so, the use of well-designed applications and other electronic tools can bring about greater transparency, less red tape, better application of good practice in procurement, but also greater awareness of public procurement and more competition through the strengthening of cross-border procurement.
mr president, i am sure we can all agree on the importance of smes for the economic development of the member states and thus for the stability of the european union as a whole. i really appreciate the approach of reducing and simplifying the bureaucratic burden on small business owners. i know from small business owners that this is a very positive direction. on the other hand, we must not forget the other regulatory standards adopted on the european parliament's premises. these have a very severe impact on smes. regulations for restaurants, excessive mandatory labelling and the like not only increase costs, but also reduce the competitiveness of these small businesses. the mere monitoring of changes in european legislation is a huge burden for them. the responsibilities of employing people are also a big problem. the desire to maximise the protection of workers often increases the reluctance of employers to comply with all the rules laid down by the legislation. therefore, we should always give great consideration to the impact on small businesses when adopting any regulatory standards.
madam president, the committee on women's rights and gender equality has contributed to this report with an opinion which i drafted. the free movement of workers is undoubtedly one of the fundamental rights and freedoms of the european union and contributes to strengthening economic and social cohesion. considerable progress has been made in this area, but legal and administrative obstacles to mobility within the european union still exist. the commission's report on the application of the 2004 directive on the rights of citizens of the union and their family members showed that the transposition of the directive itself is less satisfactory for family members. it is therefore essential that individuals are properly informed about their rights and options, including those of their family. i would like to stress here in particular that in the context of free movement of persons, family members or partners, the important word being 'partners', should be granted equal rights and adequate services such as childcare facilities, nurseries, schools, health services and others. i am therefore pleased that the employment committee has incorporated our committee's opinion on family members in its report.
madam chairman, i have long supported alternative dispute resolution, but with one major caveat, and that is to include family matters within the scope of this bill. this area falls within the competence of the member states, in accordance with their national traditions. moreover, complex family disputes, including those concerning the upbringing of children, should be decided by judges trained and specialised in these matters, and the eu should not intervene in this area. however, i welcome the development of alternative dispute resolution in civil and commercial matters, in particular consumer dispute resolution, as it offers cheaper, faster and more practical solutions. these systems are to be built as web-based and, in the first instance, fully automated, so as to be attractive to entrepreneurs, and to comply with the principles of impartiality, transparency, efficiency and fairness. i have already pointed out that experience should be used, for example, in the eu internet domain name system. in conclusion, i would stress that the commission should make a strong appeal to countries such as the czech republic, where alternative dispute resolution systems are not yet fully operational.
madam president, i would like to support the report and the work of my colleague matera on the situation of single mothers. i believe that member states should promote appropriate support models for mothers that would increase their chances on the labour market while also leading to the possibility of combining working life with childcare. it is appropriate to support parents in child rearing within tax systems, but beware not only of single mothers, but also of marriage with children as an institution that must not be economically disadvantaged, so that the number of single mothers does not increase in an intentional way. any investment in childrearing is the best investment possible given the demographic situation in europe. the european commission should ensure that member states exchange best practices on this issue and that they also support non-profit organisations that focus on working with single mothers and assisting them in re-entering the labour market.
madam president, i was the shadow rapporteur for the european conservatives and reformists group on this report. i mean, in the czech republic, one in four children live in a single-parent family, and 90 percent of single parents are mothers, which is very alarming. i therefore support policies that allow such women to work and thus combine work with childcare. i therefore consider flexible working opportunities such as part-time work or working from home to be key. in addition, adequate childcare infrastructure is also needed to enable single mothers to work. from my own experience in municipal politics, i know that it is necessary to ensure a good existence for single mothers and their children through various support policies. this report - for which i thank the rapporteur, and i think she has done an excellent job - highlights the main problems faced by single mothers and focuses on the right objectives. but the question remains for me as a member of the european conservatives and reformists group whether the political instruments mentioned in the areas of health, education, social security systems, etc., should be within the competence of the european union. i believe that many of them can be better safeguarded by the member states.
in writing. the draft report addresses three aspects of the situation of single mothers. i consider the most serious aspect to be the threat of poverty. the report cites a five-year-old figure that shows that 32 per cent of single parent households face this serious problem. however, it can be assumed that due to the economic situation, this figure will be higher. i am convinced that many times single mothers do not get into difficult situations by their own inaction. in my experience in the czech republic, the main causes of economic difficulties are the non-payment of child support by the other parent and the lack of support for single mothers in the labour market, i.e. lower chances of getting a job, the lack of flexible working hours or the low number of places in kindergartens and their financial burden. the urgency of support for single women is also supported by the fact that the poor living situation is passed on to children and that it is difficult for them to break out of the vicious circle. the chances of getting a good education and finding a job are reduced. i am glad that the european parliament is taking this matter seriously.
for the ecr group. madam president, i, like my colleagues, have listened carefully to the council's speech and i am probably the only one here who can say that i am satisfied with the council's speech and its communication. simply because the council is operating in a real political and economic space. the council representative finally told us. it is aware that there is not only the european parliament, that there are also national parliaments and that debate in national parliaments is not simple and not one-sided. that's the first thing. secondly, the council has conveyed the obvious truth here between the lines that the development of national economies is heterogeneous and that harmonizing the process is not an overnight task. thirdly, and this was stated by the council, the process is very complicated. if the council here says that there are 600 documents which are not even in the official languages, and national parliaments have to fight it somehow, then of course it takes some time. and finally, what i consider the most serious. static targeting six months ago is fundamentally inconsistent with the current turbulent economic environment. i'm satisfied with the council's response.
in writing. - i voted to accept the report. the advantages of adr are clear: in particular, simplicity, lower costs and time efficiency compared to litigation. however, in order for the whole system to be really useful, we must not only guarantee european citizens access to this option, but above all, and this is absolutely crucial from my point of view, we must inform them sufficiently about the very nature and possibilities of out-of-court dispute resolution. low awareness among citizens, consumers and businesses is one of the main reasons for the often insufficient use and effectiveness of this tool in different eu member states.
in writing. − the legislation we are responding to in the off-road engine sector as a result of the economic crisis in 2008 and 2009 is being submitted for approval now, in the autumn of 2011. for about a year now, the representatives of the companies concerned have been asking me when they will finally know what changes their production process will have to adapt to. i think the legislation, if it's going to help effectively, has to come in time. otherwise, it brings insecurity and chaos instead of help. i also supported the report as rapporteur for the committee on transport and tourism, but i regret that it has taken so long to be adopted by the ep plenary.
in writing. the principle of free movement is a fundamental right in the european union. sufficient access to public and private transport, including harmonisation of parking permits in the member states and the development of a single catalogue of passenger rights, is needed to make appropriate transport by different means available to all. it is also necessary to create websites that can be used by visually impaired persons and electronically accessible online services in sign language. smes play a particularly important role in improving social inclusion, both by providing information on the employment of people with disabilities and by participating in public procurement, manufacturing and providing services with the participation of people with disabilities. there are also significant reservoirs in education and training systems that are not sufficiently equipped to address the needs of people with disabilities, and the number of students with disabilities who leave early remains high. for example, in denmark, one of the most advanced member states, 44% of young people with disabilities started upper secondary education in 2007, compared with 75% of those without disabilities. about 5% of disabled people have a university degree, which is only a fraction of the total.
in writing. i appreciate that the commission has decided, through the review, to make the eu's public procurement legislation simpler and more flexible. however, there are also contradictions in the green paper. on the one hand, the scope and detail of the questions give the impression that a 'micro-decree' is being introduced for public procurement, and on the other hand, many of the questions or proposals put forward are contradictory. for example, quotas or binding procurement targets could be in contradiction with the declared desire for simplification and greater legal certainty, thus contributing to further bureaucratization and the creation of new legislation. i believe that the review of the public procurement directives should be based on the need to streamline public procurement legislation and bring it back to its essence, i.e. to guarantee transparency, non-discrimination and ensure competition. in my view, the main attention should be paid to the tendency of public authorities to rely on bureaucratic procedures in cases of legal uncertainty, which leads to an attempt to avoid risks and, in the case at issue, to award the contract for the cheapest product or service instead of the most innovative or the best offer. this is particularly problematic in a time of economic crisis and limited public budgets.
in writing. it should be noted that labour mobility is not only reflected in employment policy provisions but is linked to measures taken in the fields of completion of the internal market, coordination of social security systems, pension rights, protection of workers, education and training, language requirements and tax measures such as those designed to avoid double taxation. i also consider it important to align the impacts of labour migration with existing strategies at european level, in particular the eu 2020 strategy, and also with the strengthening of the role of smes. the involvement of smes will create more jobs and their dynamic operation can allow for a cyclical migration of workers, which will mainly consist in upskilling through training, in which new or extended knowledge is acquired, as well as in maintaining a competitive environment that leads to better end products. the current situation and the lessons learned from the financial crisis have brought a new range of opportunities to boost economic performance and employment rates once again. to this end, the contribution of labour mobility is very important as it will contribute to greater social and economic convergence and bring lasting shared experiences beneficial to both employers and employees. moreover, the job creation objectives will be better achieved if labour mobility is fully implemented.
in writing. − due to the ambiguity of article 39 of the services directive as regards the precise definition of the objectives of peer review, peer review is confused with work on the implementation and application of the services directive. it should therefore be specified that peer review takes place after the implementation phase. i consider this order to be justified, as during the peer review member states discussed changes in their regulatory framework and rules that were maintained, repealed or amended as a result of the implementation of the services directive. this has enabled these discussions to provide a better understanding of the situation in each member state and also of the remaining obstacles that have not disappeared even after the transposition of the services directive. i consider this to be a major contribution to a better assessment of the current situation in the single market for services. however, peer review raises questions about the transparency of the procedure and whether and to what extent member states should be involved in a procedure that directly concerns them. the full and correct implementation of the services directive should, in my view, remain the top priority. bilateral meetings between the commission and the member states on the implementation of the services directive are already under way and the commission should not hesitate to use coercive measures if necessary.
in writing. on 24 october 2011, the parliament's plenary discussed a report on the situation of single mothers. the report contains a number of interesting findings and suggestions for improving the position of single mothers. the report finds that 5% of european women are single mothers. they are particularly affected by high unemployment and the cyclical nature of poverty, which affects the access to education for their children. low access to education leads to fewer opportunities to enter the labour market at a competitive level. statistics also show that single mothers are less likely to be healthy than mothers in two-parent families. often they cannot afford proper medical care or proper rest. statistically, single mothers across europe are less educated than married mothers. personally, i find very useful suggestions for measures that draw on the experience of other member states: introducing tax-free food for single mothers or providing tax breaks and incentives for companies that operate childcare facilities at work and in crèches, allowing single mothers to work while their children are looked after and educated. for the reasons stated above, i supported the report.
in writing. − the report on the situation of single mothers is a very useful report, which highlights the difficulties facing single mothers across the eu, where single mothers represent nearly 90% of single parents. this report encourages member states to review their policies towards single mothers in the areas of healthcare, childcare, education, pensions, social security and welfare and housing. whilst the ecr group does not object to the eu raising the awareness of such issues and encouraging cooperation and the sharing of best practice, we do oppose interference at eu level on social and employment issues. we defend the rights of national governments to make their own policies in the areas of healthcare, pension system, education and social security. it is for this reason that we have chosen to abstain on this report.
- organised crime is essentially transnational. it is therefore appropriate to recall the need for effective international cooperation, and in particular the cooperation of the competent authorities whose competence it is to combat organised crime. our contribution is first and foremost to the development of good legislation and thus to the support of the institutions in their work. i would also like to remind you that you should not forget to set up control mechanisms that will not allow any abuse of anything under the pretext of fighting organised crime and that you should set up rules for maximum transparency.
for the ecr group. mr president, let's sum up where we are, then, except that we are in the middle of a crisis. i must say that in the last few days and weeks i have felt that we have arrived in europe of the 19th century rather than the 21st. i feel like we're in europe sometime after 1815, after the congress of vienna, when it was called the "concert of the great powers", because despite all the talk about european unity, about european solutions, about the communitarian method, about the need for a transnational solution to rise above the national ones, i've only seen the last two national leaders in the last fortnight who were running the show and who were making the decisions here. and there was one chancellor and one president who even sandbagged another prime minister. he advised him to keep quiet. i am not a federalist, and mr schuman and mr monnet are not among my favourites, but i would say that this does not sound very european and perhaps both gentlemen are turning in their graves. otherwise, to the crisis itself. it's the same thing, and it's just a matter of passing on irresponsible behavior to the taxpayer. the irresponsible behaviour of banks and financial institutions that have played here like in a casino, the irresponsible behaviour of states that have bribed voters by spending public money that they really did not have, and the resulting debts we propose to europeanise, transfer to everyone, buy them in the name of saving the euro and it does not matter what we call it. tomorrow the european union will only buy time for one year, two years, maybe three years, but after a while we will be in the same situation again. let's face it, let's admit it - many of you here admit it in the roundtables, don't have the courage to say it but publicly - that the euro is not a project for everyone, that there will have to be a situation where not all the current members of the eurozone will be able to continue to participate in this project, because they will have to go through a managed bankruptcy, they will have to go through a devaluation. and you know that, in the colony, they talk about it, but you're afraid to say it out loud. so tomorrow's just about buying time. it's the most traditional way, it's probably the most comfortable way, it's the most expensive way, it's the least credible way for markets, and it's not the way of the state. because the difference between a statesman and a politician is that he has the courage to take on unconventional solutions, to take on new solutions, to convince people of them, to make them happen. so tomorrow we will find out, among other things, whether there is really a european statesman in europe or whether we have only politicians here.
for the ecr group. mr president, european steel demand is declining. the world's largest steel producer arcelormittal is therefore temporarily closing one of its three blast furnaces in poland. he's already shut down furnaces in belgium, germany and spain. and we're starting to talk right here about the need for european intervention, about the subsidies from the european globalisation adjustment fund. we're trying to figure out how to fix the market, which logically, with falling demand, is driving down steel prices. it is demonstrated that arcelormittal is able to restructure its production itself and at the same time to provide for its employees at a higher standard. in 2004, arcelormittal laid off 1 700 employees in the czech republic. in 2006, it was over 1000 and in subsequent years it was again over 1000. arcelor applied a voluntary redundancy policy. people who left voluntarily received severance pay equal to one to two and a half years of average monthly salary. the move has helped the czech company recover from a loss of nearly a billion crowns and to turn a profit of 700 million crowns last year. i would therefore consider it a huge mistake and an injustice now for eu money, our money, to finance the restructuring of the furnaces in belgium or other western european countries. the czech experience clearly shows that this is not necessary.
madam president, it is undeniable that the so-called flexicurity policies, which symbolise the modernisation of labour markets, have played a positive role in limiting the impact of the crisis on the labour market. however, the crisis has shown the need to continue labour market reforms and to focus on promoting labour mobility and reducing segmentation of labour sectors. i welcome the european commission's flagship initiative under discussion, which aims to contribute to the competitiveness of member states' economies, to reducing the transaction costs of doing business in the union and, more generally, to creating a better business environment, strengthening and improving the tertiary education system and modernising and making labour markets more flexible. it should be stressed, however, that newly launched employment policy activities should not be too prescriptive, as flexicurity policies must be based on the conditions, priorities and needs of individual member states. a specific mix of labour market policies cannot be defined for the whole of the european union; individual approaches are needed. this is the case, for example, of a single contract, where, while i agree with the general intention of reducing the differences in protection between fixed-term and indefinite-term contracts, i am nevertheless convinced that the specific form of implementation of that intention must be adapted to the conditions, needs and traditions of the member states. i therefore call for a sober and realistic approach in the implementation of this flagship initiative. in particular, we should support job creation by reducing administrative and legislative obstacles. reducing unpaid labour costs and facilitating the transition from informal or undeclared work to regular employment is also welcome. fiscal intervention distorting the market, activist table-setting, or over-regulation certainly don't deserve our support.
draftsman of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection. - mr. chairman, everyone is talking about the crisis today. yes, there is a crisis. and member states are being forced to make substantial cuts in both their national budgets and the union budget. we are in an acute financial crisis. i think it is totally inappropriate to increase the eu budget by more than 2%, as proposed by the member states. the proposal we have on the table today, a 4.9% increase, gives me the feeling that its authors are living outside of economic reality. yes, i am in favour of the budget supporting employment. i'm all for a budget that supports growth. but this can be achieved by changing his philosophy, that is, by no longer increasing all budget chapters in a flat rate, but by auditing the usefulness of the projects financed today, and simply cutting back on expenditure on those that are not viable. we will then concentrate the savings on areas such as the internal market, whose functioning is key to economic growth and the competitiveness of the union as a whole.
in writing. in the past months, the rapporteur, mrs gräßle, has done a tremendous job with a number of concrete and genuinely positive results in the form of visible changes in the revision of the union's financial bible, the financial regulation. the main purpose of the new proposal is to simplify and reorganise the financial regulation by creating a simple legal instrument linking the principles and basic rules of budgetary and financial management. the new eu financial regulation should make european union spending more precise, transparent and more convenient for users of european funds. the new proposal stresses that improvements in eu financial management must be supported by close monitoring of developments in the commission and in the member states, and therefore insists that member states should take responsibility for the proper use and management of funds and issue regular annual national reports on the use of eu funds at the appropriate political level, the so-called national declarations.
mr president, the economic, financial and social crisis is mainly causing job losses. in total, 5.6 million have died in europe since 2008. the fact that a substantial proportion of the unemployed are young people under 30 is very worrying. if a young person does not start working after completing his education or university, his knowledge and skills disappear and he himself gradually becomes socially excluded from society. this situation does not allow them to properly start a family and to lead their children to work, thus assuming financial responsibility for their parents or grandparents later on. i stress the need to rethink the system of secondary education. in many european union countries, education produces hundreds of thousands of universally educated secondary school students who are out of work, while demand for traditional crafts persists and the european market is facing a long-term shortage of craftsmen. the report presented contains mostly appropriate measures to increase employment in europe and i therefore voted in favour of its adoption.
in writing. i am disappointed that neither the commission nor the council are proposing to increase support beyond the initial plans for investments urgently needed to implement the seven flagship initiatives in the fields of competitiveness and entrepreneurship, research and innovation, education and lifelong learning. i reject any form of transfer of funds from the 7th framework programme, as proposed by the commission in its set of proposals on how to finance iter, as this would jeopardise the successful implementation of the 7th framework programme. i also support the increase in commitment appropriations for selected fp7 items (capacity building research for smes, cooperation energy, ideas, people, energy research) as i believe these items are a tool to ensure growth and investment in key areas that are central to the europe 2020 strategy. i agree with the increase in the overall commitment appropriations for the competitiveness and innovation framework programme (cip intelligent energy and cip enterprise and innovation) compared to the initial allocation and believe that this increase will contribute to improving access to this programme for smes and to the development of specific programmes and innovative financial mechanisms. smes play an important role in improving the performance of the eu economy and the cip is an indispensable tool for post-crisis recovery in this regard.
in writing. − the eu 2020 growth strategy should not become a victim of the current austerity climate. i also reject the council's cuts in cohesion policy spending. that is why i agree with rapporteur balzani that investing in growth is really necessary to help europe out of the crisis. the budget for the next year is drawn up by the european parliament in a parsimonious way, and the increases relate mainly to the additional activities promoted by the european council, composed of the relevant ministers from the 27 member states. the european prime ministers' surprise of their decision to raise their salaries is a regular spectacle for their voters.
in writing. the case of switzerland, which is covered by a european directive under a bilateral agreement between switzerland and the eu, shows how hollow the current tax system in the eu is. dividends received by eu parent companies from swiss subsidiaries are not subject to taxation and can be deducted anywhere in the eu. the state thus 'loses' with multinational companies which evade tax obligations and thus contribute to public budgets in an inventive way. i therefore agree to the introduction of a requirement that, where capital leaving the country is not allowed to be taxed, a minimum tax of 25% be applied in the country where the capital is coming in. i therefore support the inclusion of stricter provisions in the directive to prevent abuse. dividend inflows from legal entities established in third countries must, within the scope of the directive, be subject to minimum requirements in order to limit distortions of the tax base by eu legal entities. on the other hand, i do not agree with the introduction of a common consolidated corporate tax base as a measure to combat double non-taxation. this measure will not lead to a reduction in the tax evasion opportunities of legal entities in the eu and a reduction in the administrative burden on businesses, but to a reduction in tax competition in the internal market, which will undermine its competitiveness.
in writing. − i welcome the effort to simplify the rules of budgetary policy in terms of administration and financing during the programme and project phases (application, evaluation and management), which should benefit stakeholders as a matter of priority. science and research are uncertain processes involving some inherent risk, and results-based funding can narrow the focus of research projects to less risky projects and market-oriented research, which would prevent the eu from promoting high-quality and frontier research. unfortunately, the overly strict interpretation of the financial regulation in previous years has led to a general risk-avoidance and zero trust in the participants of research programmes, thus reducing the effectiveness of its implementation. european research funding should be more based on mutual trust and responsible partnership, tolerate risk in terms of participants at all stages, and have flexible eu rules applied in accordance with national laws and procedures. the evaluation of research funding should be based on the nature of the research and focus on excellence, relevance and impact. simplification is not a separate objective but rather a means to ensure the attractiveness and availability of eu research funding, and simplification measures should also make it possible to avoid errors more effectively.
in writing. − i consider the new skills and jobs programme to be an important part of the eu 2020 strategy, because only a skilled workforce is a necessary prerequisite for the development of a competitive, sustainable and innovative economy. i support the promotion of integrated approaches involving key actors at national, regional and local level and stress the importance of the regional dimension of the implementation of the new skills and jobs agenda. there is also a need to improve the level and diversity of employment services provided to both jobseekers and employers, and in particular to pursue the development of personalised public services for the unemployed, young people and vulnerable groups in this area. i believe it is essential to ensure that funds for developing new skills are used more efficiently and that member state governments are more involved in supporting job creation. it is also possible to use the social economy as a means of creating employment opportunities for disadvantaged social groups, which means in particular the use of the relevant funds and programmes: the european social fund, the european regional development fund, the rural development fund, the lifelong learning programme, the progress programme and the interreg programme. in this context, i stress the importance of synergies between the different european funds and the importance of a decentralised approach to the current use of these funds in order to respond to labour market requirements.
mr president, mr ombudsman, when i read your speech on this subject last year, i thought my words were still relevant. your institution's purpose is to deal with cases of injustice by the european bureaucracy in a cheaper and more accessible way to the public. cases that could also be resolved by ordinary remedies or judicial review. your solution to the complainant's problem should therefore be fundamentally cheaper, faster and more flexible than a judicial solution. i read in your 2010 report that you had a budget of eur 9.3 million last year, with 2 667 cases registered. this amounts to an average of eur 3 500 per case. by comparison, the european court of human rights registered 61 300 cases last year, with a budget of €58.4 million. this translates to eur 953 per case. i know that any comparison is oversimplifying. however, i would ask you to consider and explain why you are so much more valuable than the judicial institution.
in writing. the report assesses the ombudsman's activities in 2010, highlighting as an important part of the report the new strategy of the office published on the occasion of its 15th anniversary. in 2010, the ombudsman received 2 667 complaints from citizens, businesses, associations, ngos and regional offices (3 098 in 2009) and handled a total of 2 727 complaints. 65% of the inquiries opened by the ombudsman in 2010 concerned the european commission (219 inquiries), an increase compared to 56% in 2009 (191 inquiries), which i do not consider to be a good trend. i agree with the ombudsman that the fundamental principle of the provision of information and documents in the eu, where openness is the rule and secrecy the exception, should be upheld. personally, i agree with the ombudsman's view that there is still a lot of work to be done to convince officials that defending the ombudsman is a missed opportunity for their institutions and threatens to damage the image of the union as a whole. 33% of all closed investigations and included refusal to provide information and refusal of access to eu documents, which i personally consider to be a high figure and a greater degree of transparency and openness should be required in the future.
on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, there are a few good things in the package that has been adopted, but unfortunately there are many more bad things in it. one good thing is the call for a balanced budget – the so-called balanced budget rule. everybody wants to have a balanced budget. that is true. i just wonder how it will be received by those political forces and those politicians who have been used, for a very long time, to bribing their voters with public overspending. will they stick with that rule or not? that is another question. the main bad thing in the whole package is that it is actually only a buying of time, a temporary solution. what has happened? the vast amount of both private and public funds will be poured into the debt bailouts. other debts will simply be written off and a bond union will be created. this means that we are at the very beginning of a massive fiscal transfer, and one never before seen in european history. this is an unprecedented case and i am afraid that it will create an enormous burden for the eu economy and that it will result in very high long-term costs. i am afraid that loss of economic growth and loss of international competitiveness is unfortunately what is to be expected. even worse, this might have a side effect on other, very viable, strategies, such as completion of a single market. i was very pleased to see sunday’s council giving a renewed commitment to completion of a single market. we must not just drop the single market while we attempt to save the single currency. i represent a group with the most non-eurozone members and as a eurozone outsider, as an observer, my feeling is that the eurozone at the moment does not serve all its 17 members, but just a few. i have the feeling that the only answer to the current problems will be the reduction of the eurozone, and sooner or later this will have to be done, like it or not. that is the only reasonable way out of this mess. on non-eurozone countries, i have one more remark to make – last but not least. we should allow non-eurozone members, such as my country, to decide again if they even wish to enter. we signed up in our accession treaty to a monetary union, and not to a transfer union or a bond union. that is the main reason why, for instance, the czech prime minister wishes to call a referendum on this matter, which has my full support. to sum up, where are we now? we have found a very costly and only temporary solution. another treaty change is even being considered. let me say that this is completely out of touch and completely unrealistic. this will never be passed. have you already forgotten the lessons of the lisbon treaty? i do not think so. i am sorry to say this, but there is no real reason for optimism. merely rearranging the deckchairs on the titanic is not enough.
madam president, i abstained from voting on the ombudsman's 2010 activity report because, among other things, i cannot support the point where the european parliament congratulates the ombudsman on reducing the average time needed to complete the investigation of individual cases. i personally consider this period, which is still nine months, to be extremely long. incidentally, the cost of investigating a case is still four times that of, say, the european court of human rights.
in writing. − when voting on the report by iliana malinova iotova on the 2010 annual report of the european ombudsman, i abstained because, among other things, i cannot agree with the assertion that the ombudsman's role in strengthening the openness and accountability of the decision-making processes and the administrative structure of the european union contributes significantly to the creation of a union in which decisions are taken as openly as possible and as closely as possible to the citizens. the vast majority of the population of the member states is unaware of the european ombudsman's work and has never heard of it.
in writing. − i am in favour of a greater convergence of the two categories of protection refugee status and subsidiary protection , and thus a general designation person enjoying international protection. in this way, the implementing authorities will be informed that the two categories of protection are complementary. the additional protection is also very important for persons who are at risk of serious harm upon return to their country of origin. there is also a greater convergence of the rights which form part of protection, the most important of which is probably access to the labour market. already most member states do not make a large distinction between the two groups. however, discrimination can still occur in three areas: social care, integration measures and residence permits. however, some progress has been made in the latter area, as the residence permit of beneficiaries of subsidiary protection must be extended for at least two years after one year. only a few member states currently apply a different procedure. the ep considered that this procedure was important from the point of view of integration and also gave the person concerned a greater sense of security.
in writing. - for many years we have been talking about the dangers of corruption in the countries in transition, especially those east of the eu's borders, and corruption at the highest levels. of course, the corruption stories are true, but they're also full of legends. today, ukrainian society and media are divided over the trial of former ukrainian prime minister yulia tymoshenko. was she corrupt or not? the resolution's drafters are convinced that it is not. i don't take away their right to their opinion. but in civilized countries, these issues are decided by the courts. i have no doubt that the courts can be wrong. the rule of law provides for the possibility of appealing to a higher court, perhaps as far as strasbourg. i've never heard of a former prime minister being denied that right. i recognize that this process is time-consuming, unpleasant and imperfect. but it's the best one yet. the resolution suggests that the court has been politicized. i don't know, man. but the commission is certainly intervening politically, and now the european parliament too. is it really enough to stop a politician or an oligarch suspected of corruption just because we don't like his opponent? or are you afraid that corruption in the highest places could be prosecuted in the union?
in writing. − one of the fundamental tasks of the community is to protect the health of its citizens. in this context, i consider the amendments which specify and tighten the conditions for the use of antibiotics, particularly in agriculture, to be a significant step forward. i welcome the emphasis on prevention, including in the field of human health. the amendments also aim to guarantee the responsible development of new preparations and, on the other hand, to allow the use of antibiotics in justified cases. it is right that the joint resolution stresses the gradual reduction of antibiotic use in agriculture and its gradual replacement by compliance with hygiene standards and biosecurity measures.
in writing. i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution on the threat to public health posed by antimicrobial resistance. the reason is that i believe that there is a need to end the regular prophylactic use of antibiotics in the breeding and treatment of livestock. instead, precautions such as adequate hygiene, appropriate accommodation and animal handling are desirable. this is also the view of the world health organization (who), which this year showed that the widespread use of antibiotics in agriculture is contributing to the rise of antibiotic resistance.

rapporteur of the committee on employment and social affairs. - madam president, on the question of mutual recognition of qualifications, i would like to stress a few basic points. the first is that the mutual recognition of professional qualifications promotes the free movement of persons, workers and their access to the labour market in the member states of the european union, and undoubtedly contributes to employment and labour mobility. the legislation applicable in this area undoubtedly requires change, in the direction of simplifying administration, speeding up the recognition of qualifications, reducing the number of regulated professions and perhaps also better coordinating training. as far as professional certificates are concerned, which we think of more as a kind of database or an electronic database, we see two views of this. the first is a database of people who have a certain qualification and wish to be in such a database in order to facilitate the recognition of qualifications in other countries, and this database must be voluntary and could be organised at national level. unlike the database of people who were forbidden to practice their profession for whatever reason. such databases must be compulsory and uniform throughout the community. in conclusion, i would like to emphasise that the european union has no competence to decide on professional qualifications, but only to make recommendations to the member states and to create an infrastructure for the mutual recognition of qualifications between the member states.
madam president, first of all, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur and all the shadow rapporteurs on a very well-crafted report. europe today is on the verge of if not recession, then at least stagnation. it's going to be very difficult to get through this period. the union and its institutions are already suffering from a lack of legitimacy and citizens' confidence in its future is rapidly declining. the remedy for the current situation is certainly not to strengthen the union even more and to transfer more powers to brussels, but to return to the original ideas of european integration, the 'relaunch' of the internal market and the single market act. increasing the mobility of skilled workers and reducing the number of regulated professions is an effective remedy for the current economic problems of some member states. i would therefore also like to call on the member states not to be tempted by populist appeals to protect national labour markets. on the contrary, they should remove existing barriers more quickly and instead of creating national preferential measures, focus on strengthening their own competitive strengths in a functioning internal market.
that concludes the item.
