the next item is the report by jürgen creutzmann, on behalf of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, on online gambling in the internal market [2011/2084(ini)] (a7-0342/2011).
mr president, this report and the commission's green paper are the right step after four years of debate on how to regulate the unregulated. that is, online gambling crossing not only state borders but also the union. however, there is a need to find common ways to protect consumers from fraud, to combat money laundering more effectively and to protect young people from gambling, all at european level. i also support respect for different national approaches at this stage, but subsidiarity must not prevent close cooperation in the detection of crime, nor be a pretext for protectionism. therefore, it is important to delete point 20. states that restrict licences should objectively justify this by non-compliance with national legislation.
mr. chairman, online gambling is very convenient, you just click your mouse at home and you're in the game. the risks and social costs of compulsive gambling are therefore enormous. in the european union, this can be as high as 5.5% of the population, so we are talking about millions of european citizens. that is why i consider consumer protection and the prevention of pathological gambling to be essential requirements. i believe it is important to adopt common european rules that will lead to effective prevention and protection of vulnerable groups, especially young people, who are most at risk from this type of gambling. the member states need to cooperate much more in this area, particularly in combating the negative effects of gambling. i am therefore pleased that the report includes a call to the commission and the member states to focus on research into the occurrence, development and treatment of gambling addiction. the combination of prevention of negative effects and effective assistance to victims of gambling is the only possible way to actively confront the phenomenon of online gambling.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow (tuesday, 15 november 2011). written statements (rule 149)
the next item is the report by csaba sándor tabajdi, on behalf of the committee on agriculture and rural development, on honeybee health and the challenges of the beekeeping sector [2011/2108(ini)] (a7-0359/2011).
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow (tuesday, 15 november 2011). written statements (rule 149)
in writing. beekeeping provides key services to agriculture in the form of pollination and helps to preserve species biodiversity and maintain ecological balance. it also provides the livelihood for more than 600 000 eu citizens. the economic value of beekeeping is strategic precisely because the very success of food production depends on bee pollination. european beekeeping is faced with occasional but large declines in whole bee colonies, the causes of which cannot be sufficiently and timely demonstrated. the deaths are undoubtedly the result of heavy environmental pollution and the widespread use of pesticides and antibiotics in agriculture. genetically modified crops may also contribute to the death of bee colonies. my view is reinforced by the fact that gmo producers are not involved in research on the impact of gmos on bees. beekeeping can benefit greatly if the eu soon revises and establishes the types of pesticides and fertilisers that are allowed and prohibited in agriculture. i welcome the commission's intention to make its website available in all language versions for dialogue and exchange of information between beekeepers in all member states, regions and thus between professional and amateur beekeepers. i support the creation of specific financial assistance programmes for new and young beekeepers across the eu and the promotion of the breeding of original bee species that have been genetically more resistant to viruses and mites. the diversity of bee species can greatly benefit the situation in both beekeeping and agriculture.
the next item is the report by kerstin westphal, on behalf of the committee on regional development, on demographic change and its consequences for the future cohesion policy of the eu (2010/2157(ini)) (a7-0350/2011).
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow (tuesday, 15 november 2011). written statements (rule 149)
the next item is the report by peter simon, on behalf of the committee on economic and monetary affairs, on reform of the eu state aid rules on services of general economic interest [2011/2146(ini)] (a7-0371/2011).
there are considerable differences between member states as regards the definition and scope of public service obligations and their exemptions from the internal market rules. the commission should be supported in its efforts to map legislative rules in the member states. we should insist on simplifying the rules for the mobilisation of state aid and support the commission in its efforts to introduce, within the possibilities provided by subsidiarity, greater transparency and control of the proportionality of the level of subsidies. in particular, large investment contracts for public service enterprises, whether state or municipal, are a source of enormous corruption. but the commission should also adjust its procedures and investigate suspicions and complaints of excessive subsidies and corruption much more rigorously. the commission's investigations are often formal and rely mainly on the opinions of ministerial officials, who cover up the purposeful assessments by the excessive or illegal level of state aid. i found this to be true when i read the answers to my questions.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow (tuesday, 15 november 2011). written statements (rule 149)
in writing. the report presented by the commission deals with the commission's proposal to amend the package of services of general economic interest (sgeis) which play an important role in the eu's common values, in particular by promoting fundamental rights and social, economic and territorial cohesion, thereby supporting the fight against social inequalities. the sgeis also make a significant contribution to the economic performance and competitiveness of the member states, which seems very important in the light of the current problems in the euro area. lastly, they contribute to the successful implementation of the europe 2020 strategy and should contribute to the achievement of the growth objectives, in particular in the areas of employment, education and social inclusion. i welcome the commission's intention to make things simpler, clearer and more proportionate. in the area of social services, i consider it essential that, for social services of general economic interest which are, by their nature, locally limited, specific higher thresholds than the compensation payment, the failure of which is not expected to have an impact on trade between member states, be introduced. however, i also support general exemptions from the obligation to report for other areas of social services of general economic interest, such as facilities for the care of elderly and disabled people or health care. overall, i think that the report presented by mr peter simon is a good one.
madam president, poverty and social exclusion cannot be underestimated in our time. i'm not sure the platform as it's designed will help. however, i think that from an economic point of view, we should look at how it is possible that the poverty rate is increasing and the social exclusion rate is increasing. this is certainly the result of the reduction in competitiveness of rigid labour laws, high labour taxes and an overly rich social system that discourages employers from offering work and discourages some employees from seeking work. politics must create space for people to become rich based on their abilities and their decisions. this must be done through the liberalisation of labour law, for example to enable women to benefit from flexible working hours, reduced working hours with their employers or to make it easier for young people to enter into employment relationships, as their employers will not risk too much if the young person or the employer does not like or agree with their future employment relationship. the state must put in place a robust retraining system for people who have not received a good education, while ensuring that parents make sure their children go to school. and this is especially true of the poorest and those who really need to get an education. in this context, there is a very serious debate about the so-called minimum wage. you may be in favor of raising the minimum wage threshold, but be assured that any codification of a high minimum wage means that the opportunity to work for the most socially vulnerable people with the lowest education is taken away.
in writing. on 15 november, the european parliament discussed the report on the european platform against poverty and social exclusion. it is sad that the european union, as one of the richest regions in the world, has more than 80 million citizens at risk of poverty, of whom 20 million are children and more than 6 million are the active population. this situation is incompatible with the european values of justice and solidarity enshrined in the treaties. the financial crisis since 2008 has exacerbated this phenomenon, increasing the pressure on the most vulnerable sections of the population. although the fight against poverty and social exclusion is the exclusive competence of member states' governments, the european union can play an important role in raising awareness of the problem, in strengthening coordination and in financing activities in the member states to tackle poverty and social exclusion. this multifaceted problem requires an integrated response involving the joint efforts of all levels of public administration, from the european level to the local level. personally, i believe that the most effective instruments can be implemented by local authorities, as they are closest to these groups, and that these instruments must be complemented by systemic measures in the social system at the level of each member state with the aim of reducing poverty and social exclusion.
madam president, europe has the oldest population and the lowest population growth rate in the world, while the waves of migration from third countries are still increasing. if we take these population changes as an opportunity and co-create them appropriately, they will not become a ticking time bomb for the european union. unlike the strategies, the member states, regions and cities can use the structural funds. the commission notes that the aid is granted to the following undertakings: regional policy is the main instrument to address demographic change. i call on the regions to make greater use of the opportunities offered by the european union and to work on adequate projects to support the elderly, children and families, and to employ young citizens. on immigration, i stress the need for the european union to distinguish in its policies between migrants who move europe forward and those who migrate to the european union only with the vision of a simpler life. the report presented offers a number of suitable solutions, which is why i voted in favour of its adoption.
in writing. i supported the eu-georgia air transport agreement, which will create a single framework for all airlines in the eu and in georgia. the application of the existing rules on the prevention of unlawful acts and aviation safety and air traffic management in georgia will allow for the mutual opening of the market and non-discriminatory participation of all economic operators in the common aviation space. in future, all air carriers in the eu and georgia will be able to offer their services from anywhere in the eu and anywhere in georgia. i am convinced that mutual market opening will lead to the entry of new entrants, the operation of services at hitherto less used airports and will also facilitate consolidation among eu air carriers. i agree with the commission's estimate that the gradual introduction of the eu-georgia common aviation area will bring an increase of 25 000 passengers in the first year and a total benefit to consumers of approximately eur 17 million.
in writing. − as i have pointed out on other occasions, there is a need to have quality control mechanisms in place to ensure that we are operating in a transparent and efficient manner. the benefits of temporary suspension of the general duty on imports of certain goods are intended to stimulate the growth of the local economy, to strengthen competitiveness. the application of control regimes should be a safeguard element that leaves the possibility to enjoy these benefits for the entire agreed period. there should be no situation where such preferred types of goods become the subject of illegal trade or other speculation outside their original purpose. i believe that the experience gained in the control of the end-use of this type of goods in the previous period will help to improve this process in the years to come.
in writing. the government of the kingdom of spain, on behalf of the authorities of the autonomous community of the canary islands, has requested the extension of the existing arrangements providing for the suspension of customs duties on imports of certain industrial products. the literal aim of these measures is to strengthen the competitiveness of local businesses and thus to ensure more stable employment in the islands. i did not support this report because i am against the introduction of customs exemptions in the european union. european funds in the form of the erdf and the esf are used to boost competitiveness and employment. i would also point out that the suspension of customs duties will lead to a loss of revenue from the union's own resources, which, on the contrary, the european parliament is trying to increase with proposals for various taxes on financial transactions, etc.
in writing. - i believe that when the insolvency regulation is revised, the commission should focus on some important aspects, such as extending the scope of the regulation to include situations where the management of the company remains in the hands of the debtor, as this would open up a number of possibilities for the debtor with powers of disposition to reorganise the company. i consider the concept of the place where the interests are concentrated to be the most important one, since the determination of that place is the most important rule of the insolvency regulation for determining jurisdiction which court will have jurisdiction to open the main proceedings and which law will be applicable. however, this term is not defined and this creates uncertainty. in my view, the definition of an establishment should include not only human and material resources but also services. finally, the obligation to communicate and cooperate should not only apply to the administrator of the estate but also to the courts.
in writing. − i am convinced that the structural funds must be better adapted to the challenges of demographic change. the commission should regard this change as a key priority for the development of europe. at the same time, member states and regions should take this issue more into account than in the past and consider it a horizontal priority in their operational programmes. demographic indicators should be taken into account when allocating structural funds resources at regional level. we face significant infrastructure challenges not only in rural areas but also in cities. the outflow of population and social exclusion of the elderly must be prevented, and urban planning must be changed. the structural funds can provide support here. cities and towns must be attractive to their inhabitants, which also includes child- and family-friendly infrastructure and good public transport links over short distances. given that demographic changes are mainly affecting older people, children and families, it is necessary that the european regional development fund should also be used to support low-interest loans that can help build housing suitable for older people or promote multi-generational housing. universal healthcare, sufficient nursing staff and free round-the-clock childcare are essential and can be supported by cohesion policy.
in writing. even after the march accident at the fukushima daiichi nuclear power plant in japan, for understandable reasons, nuclear power remains a key part of the energy mix for many european union countries, or the only way for them to meet their needs. however, fukushima has put before us a clear demand for further enhancement of nuclear safety, especially on two levels: tougher international standards and strengthening of specific safety measures. in addition, the general challenge of dealing with nuclear waste management remains. the proposals presented are precisely targeted at these areas. in addition, they align the euratom programme with the current budgetary framework until 2013. and these are also the main reasons why i recommend the whole package for approval.
in writing. − i welcome the debate on the directive and its amendments. it is right that emphasis is placed on the gradual harmonisation of professional qualifications. our attention cannot be focused only on highly qualified professionals, but also on the level of technical workers with a secondary education. i support a substantial increase in the exchange of information on specific expertise between member states. i also support the argument that language skills should not be seen as a fundamental barrier to the exercise of expertise. i disagree with the positive assessment of the impact of the so-called bologna process. on the contrary, i consider that it needs to be reviewed in order to be an effective means of upgrading qualifications comparable in all the member states. so far, they're just a barrier to getting a full college education.
in writing. the new consumer policy strategy should reflect the ongoing changes in consumer protection legislation and emphasise greater cross-border enforceability of consumer rights, protection of intellectual property rights as well as protection of personal data. i also find it very important that the report draws attention to the importance of standardisation and highlights its potential to contribute to simplifying complex procedures and providing comprehensive information to consumers, for example in the area of services, including the possibility of involving consumer organisations in the standard-setting process. i believe that consumer law should continue to retain its private law nature and that the overall objective of the new strategy should be to strengthen the enforceability of consumer rights.
in writing. − i am convinced that an independent and strong national regulatory authority is a prerequisite for an open and organised online gambling market. this authority must establish the framework conditions for gambling and, in particular, must be able to enforce those framework conditions. national regulatory authorities must therefore be equipped with the necessary powers to prosecute infringements of the conditions laid down and to act against illegal providers. however, given the cross-border nature of the internet, member states are not in a position to regulate all areas of online gambling on their own. therefore, very close cooperation between national regulatory authorities is necessary. so far, cooperation has been limited to small-scale activities, for example in the form of bilateral procedures. however, institutionalised cooperation would be needed, for example on the basis of an internal market information system, so that information could be shared efficiently and quickly. only a common european outlet can prevent unlicensed providers from exploiting loopholes and national regulatory authorities from competing with each other. the commission and the member states are therefore invited to act swiftly to protect european consumers from unscrupulous gambling providers.
in writing. i supported the report in particular because it respects the principle of subsidiarity and one of its conclusions is the rejection of a european-wide regulation to implement the regulation in this area. the fact that the report emphasised respect for the right of nation states to apply national rules to the organisation of gambling in their territory only reinforced my conviction of the report's accuracy. i also very much appreciate the space that the report has given to sport, especially the preservation of the ethical integrity of sport. i strongly support cross-border cooperation in fraud detection, transparent and legal gambling, as well as cooperation in prevention programmes addressing the negative impacts of gambling, with a focus on at-risk groups such as young people.
in writing. − although the text of the report correctly underlines the common view of the european parliament and the commission not to seek in any way to deregulate and liberalise online gambling, i could not vote in favour of the proposal. i welcome the fact that the report notes the results that clearly show the negative social and health impacts of gambling addiction. it rightly points out that pathological behaviour resulting from such dependence affects a high percentage of the population in some member states. despite these findings, however, the proposed measures are not, in my view, strong and therefore ineffective. this includes, for example, the reluctance to introduce regulated access to these games through identification cards or the reluctance to ban advertising for these games. the justification that advertising is part of the promotion of sport is rather vague. the health and social consequences of gambling addiction are comparable to those of smoking or excessive alcohol consumption.
in writing. − i believe it is essential to look at the pan-european level for solutions to the excessive mortality of bees. the problem is the lack of reliable and comparable data on the number of beehives, beekeepers and hive losses in the eu. there is a strong need to apply an effective harmonised surveillance system in order to estimate the extent of bee mortality and to obtain more accurate knowledge on the extent and causes of bee colony loss and other bee health problems. in this context, i welcome the commission's pilot programme on bee health surveillance and, in line with the council conclusions, i call on the commission to make its results available to all interested parties, while stressing that further efforts are needed if an adequate surveillance system is to be established in the eu. the commission should also support the establishment of a european reference network for beekeeping to monitor the health of bees in relation to environmental conditions and beekeeping or farming practices. i also welcome the establishment of an eu reference laboratory. this laboratory should complement the activities of existing networks of experts or national laboratories. i also stress the need to support diagnostic laboratories and field tests at national level. in addition, the commission should provide facilities to laboratories which analyse the quality and safety of apiculture products and ensure the dissemination of this information at european level.
in writing. − as shadow rapporteur in the envi committee, i decided to support an alternative resolution to the bee health report for the following reasons: firstly, the alternative resolution contains an objective criticism of the toxicity of pesticides, specifically systemic neurotoxins, criticises the shortcomings of the impact studies carried out and respects the precautionary principle. secondly, the report submitted by the agri committee does not indicate the dangers posed by the use of neurotoxic pesticides, in particular the group of neonicotinoids, which act as hormonal and digestive disruptors. however, scientific studies confirm the dangers of not only nicotinoids but also of entire plants containing these substances. fatal consequences for bees may result from mere contact with the gutter dew of neonicotinoid-treated plants. third, significant concentrations of the active substances were measured: 47 mg/ l for imidacloprid, 23.3 mg/ l for clothianidin and 11.9 ml/ l for thiametoxam. however, other experiments have measured much higher values. bees exposed to such toxic dew stop flying within a few minutes and become pedestrians or soon die. the symptoms are irreversible. clothianidin and thiamethoxam were found to be more toxic than imidacloprid, even at very low concentrations in the gout drops. the commission has also proposed that the eu should be able to provide a more comprehensive definition of the term 'neonicotinoids' in the eu.
in writing. i welcome the commission's plans to review the rules on state aid for services of general economic interest in order to harness the potential of these services for social cohesion, environmental sustainability and economic prosperity. i emphasise the potential of the industries defined in the member states as services of general economic interest and their importance not only for europe's competitiveness and sustainable economic growth in line with the conditions and objectives of the europe 2020 strategy, but also as sectors that can undergo rapid development and become globally competitive, as demonstrated for example in the telecommunications sector. i welcome the commission's proposals to further clarify the procedures for state aid to local and regional authorities. i am convinced that undistorted competition and the transparent application of state aid rules to services of general economic interest are crucial to prevent protectionism, to revitalise the single market and to deliver better public services. the way in which compensation for the provision of services of general economic interest is granted has an impact on public finances and the efficient use of public resources. therefore, a high level of transparency should be ensured through strict reporting obligations and the availability of information on all state aid allocations to services of general economic interest.
mr president, i listened very carefully to commission president barroso and we made a few remarks. he said his programme called for sustainable and massive growth, renewed confidence in the european union as a growth tool, a common commitment, consolidation of stability, a return to growth and an employment strategy. it would be quite easy to find these statements made by mr barroso. however, they may think otherwise, they may be aware of the deepening loss of competitiveness of the european union, of european union firms and of european economies. perhaps he means that the european commission will actively repeal a number of senseless regulations that are hurting business. it may announce the abandonment of the policy of large-scale harmonization and it may call for the liberalization of labour law so that as many people as possible get jobs, so that as many people as possible can work. because debt is not the cause of today's unhappy state, debt is the consequence of this state, and therefore something needs to be done about it.

for the ecr group. mr. chairman, i think we're all lying to each other a little bit. as a participant in several climate conferences in recent years, i've already fallen into considerable skepticism. we have consistently stressed the need for a global agreement and without a global agreement, action on european soil is pointless. if you look at the emissions numbers today, they're for elementary school students. what we have done now before durban and after cancun is to move back to unilateral commitments. we're saying cancun was a success. cancun was saved from ruin and tragedy at the last minute. as a colleague said, it almost caused the collapse of the un. let's not pass this off as a success. if we again in our declaration offer the unilateral decarbonisation of the european union, if we offer unilateral commitments that have no global impact, if we offer the inclusion of a financial transaction tax that still does not exist, then please forgive me, but i cannot sign this document and i declare that i withdraw my signature on behalf of the ecr group. i will, of course, wish our delegation the utmost success, but i am skeptical and must emphasize that no agreement will be reached.
mr president, there is no doubt that the figures we have heard are alarming. but i would like to draw our attention to one aspect of this debate, namely that at the budgetary control committee meeting last week, commissioner šemeta named the three countries with the alleged greatest deficiencies in cohesion policy: italy, spain and the czech republic. since i am from the czech republic and the czech republic is mentioned for the first time in this context, i have tried to find out the details of these statements. and the answer is only that in the czech republic 16 projects or payments were audited and 4 of them were classified as seriously quantifiable errors. i do not want to question the work of the court of auditors, which i very much respect, but i would like to call on the commission to be more restrained in its statements, because they must first be factually based. of course, i welcome further, perhaps even better, mutual information on these matters.
madam president, i have also been a member of the acp-eu assembly for many years and, after the treaty of lisbon, i looked forward to the european external action service helping us to promote common objectives, including respect for human rights. as vice-president for human rights, i would welcome the really active work of this service on the ground in the individual countries where the problems are occurring, especially, of course, with regard to open violence against women. it would also be necessary for our envoys to help in the formulation of strategic plans and the implementation of what we are dreaming of in our mixed assembly. in conclusion, i would also like to ask what action the commission has taken or will take against the violent collectivization of land. it's been mentioned here. after all, it is impossible to ignore the expulsion of white farmers, whether it be in zimbabwe or kenya and other countries. i would also like to know how the responsibilities between the commission and the external action service will be organised. in conclusion, i would like to join others who regret that our reports on the acp countries are not being discussed here in plenary.
on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, i am a very disciplined person so i will try to keep this within the time, since there is nothing much to talk about. all of you have said that more centralisation, more fiscal and political union, is needed in the euro zone. your direction is clear. instead of reducing the number of euro zone members that might create a leaner, healthier and stronger single currency, you have gone the other way round. i am sorry to say this but this is once again ideology prevailing over economy. people from my part of europe lived in a system that promoted ideology over economy for a long time and it did not go well at the end. one good thing you are trying to do is to restore fiscal discipline in the euro zone. but you are not doing it through political will and democratic means but by implementing some kind of fiscal dictatorship which would be run from brussels, frankfurt, berlin and paris. i can agree to a large extent with mr schulz that this is much more like the vienna congress than a european union, but where i disagree with mr schulz is that the community’s so-called community method can save us. the community method and all that federalist ideology is only an illusion in my understanding. it is only a coat of varnish behind which power games are played out and if this continues it will undermine the very little that remains of the credibility the eu has in the eyes of its citizens. secondly, any treaty changes would require the unanimous approval of all 27 eu members, not just the 17 euro zone members. there are 27 european union members, including my own country and including the united kingdom. you might or might not like it but that is the situation and if you open the treaties you should expect that those non-euro-zone members will negotiate. they will not grant automatic approval to any treaty change because what happens in the euro zone does affect all of us. we will negotiate: you may want more centralisation, we do not. we will negotiate for more flexibility. i am not scared of a two-tier or a multi-speed or a variable geometric europe. so, if you want to open the treaties you must be prepared for negotiations and there will negotiations just not in a one-way road but in a two-way road. it is that simple.
mr. chairman, i voted against the resolution for the climate change conference in durban. the world's climate has changed, is changing, and will change regardless of human activity. humans have always adapted to a changing climate, and will continue to do so in the future, without the need for expensive central climate controls in brussels. global warming, or more recently climate change, cannot be a pretext for global social engineering, which involves imposing new taxes, banning certain technologies and subsidising others, supposedly green. the cost of these measures is already enormous and places a heavy burden on the economies of the countries that have introduced them. we see this, after all, in the steadily rising price of electricity in the european union, driven by the extremely high cost of renewable energy. to continue in this way is absurd, for the world simply does not have the means. moreover, this approach does not lead to emission reductions on a global scale. that is why i voted against this report.
in writing. the second reading recommendation for a decision of the ep and the council establishing a european union action for the designation of 'european heritage' aims primarily at strengthening the sense of belonging to europe and therefore the eu among european citizens. cultural, historical or other monuments could be awarded the designation 'european heritage' through a selection procedure every two years, the main criterion being proof of their symbolic value for europe and european integration. while there are already a number of designations for cultural and other sites, the 'european heritage' designation system can bring added value in terms of tourism growth at a small financial cost. it can thus become another symbol for european and world tourists and contribute, at least in a small way, to economic growth in europe. i welcome any such initiative and that is why i voted to accept the recommendation put forward.
in writing. − while i do not deny the good intentions of the initiators and authors of the 'european heritage' designation, i believe that the designation of monuments, after a rigorous monitoring, with the 'european heritage' designation is far from the main thing that would, according to the report's proposal, 'strengthen the sense of belonging to the european union among european citizens and contribute to strengthening intercultural dialogue'. i consider it to be a pointless measure, but one that can be accepted, because it does not harm anyone, so to speak. but the way in which citizens feel connected to the european union is shaped by fundamentally different things, such as how the eu affects their standard of living, how much democracy is not lacking, and how well the union is coping with the economic crisis.
in writing. currently, 17 eu member states, including the czech republic, are active participants in an intergovernmental project on the 'european heritage' label. this designation has been awarded to 64 sites by mutual agreement between the member states. the first nominations were made by the individual national ministries. it was agreed that each country participating in the initiative could propose a maximum of 4 nominations. in the case of the czech republic, these are the kynžvart castle, the vítkovice machinery group (a national cultural monument), the town of tomáš bati zlín and the antonín dvořák monument in vysoká u příbram. the original markings in the form of metal plaques were produced at the expense of france. each plaque bears the european heritage label in 3 language versions, one of which is the national language of the country concerned. this project is now moving to the eu level. i supported the draft decision because this label plays an important role in promoting knowledge of europe's cultural traditions and will promote tourism in these places.
in writing. − i agree with the proposal for some form of separation of infrastructure management from the operation of transport services, with the aim of creating a structure where infrastructure is operated independently of services and ensuring that the natural monopoly that this network is is managed for the benefit of all service operators. this would allow for more competition, investment and better services. if the eu is to increase the share of rail transport in the transport sector and create a single european railway area, it must deliberately separate the railway systems. i support the commission's approach, which proposes to increase the independence and powers of national regulatory bodies, and i believe that it is necessary to strengthen regulatory control of the railway sector. effective regulatory control at national and european level is a necessary precondition for any further integration of national rail transport markets, as it ensures fair and non-discriminatory rail market rules. on the contrary, i do not think that more emphasis should be placed on the european dimension of regulatory control. i therefore do not support amendments which would lead to the creation of a european network of regulatory bodies, which would create the conditions for the creation of a european regulatory body.
in writing. the report by mrs serracchiani is based on a number of erroneous assumptions. the first is the argument that in order to ensure effective and non-discriminatory access to railway infrastructure, it is sufficient to separate infrastructure from operation. the second error is to claim that the creation of a single european railway area is a prerequisite for increasing the share of rail transport in the railway sector. the author completely ignores the need for harmonisation of safety and operational regulations between states. on the contrary, it vaguely emphasises the need to observe the strictest safety rules possible and similarly makes a light reference to the employment and working conditions of employees. the introduction of a uniform collection of data on incidents and the effort to reflect external costs in infrastructure charges are positive. at the same time, the report in annex viii points to the importance of simplifying and harmonising infrastructure charges. the possibility of changing the rules contained in the annexes outside parliamentary deliberations is very dangerous for the stability of railway operations. i agree with the rapporteur that annexes ii to viii can only be amended by the ordinary legislative procedure. the whole report has its usual weaknesses and i have to oppose the constantly repeated thesis that further liberalisation is the only correct way to increase the weight of rail transport. however, i support the report in part, and therefore i will not reject it.
in writing. − the directive under discussion on the creation of a single european railway area brings together existing legislation in a single legal act, which introduces a large number of substantive changes compared to the existing legislation. these concern in particular the financing and charging of infrastructure, non-discriminatory and transparent conditions for competition on the railway market and railway market surveillance. although i supported the european parliament's position because i consider it a fragile compromise, i am convinced that the proposed application of the principles of internalisation of external costs will lead to an unnecessary increase in the regulatory burden. i am also wary of proposals to delimit the commission's powers and to transfer competence in the regulation and supervision of competition in the railway sector to a single regulatory authority. in this respect, the proposal would merit considerable clarification.
in writing. − i support different ways of financing cinema digitisation at political level. i consider the structural funds to be one of the most important funding instruments. the relevant aid intensity must therefore be included in the new financial perspective for the period 2014-2020. the european regional development fund has already helped some cinemas to better cope with the challenges of digitalisation. the commission notes that the aid is granted to the cinema operator in the form of a grant of eur [...] per cinema operator. i appreciate the importance of the media programme, which since its inception 20 years ago has contributed to favourable financial conditions for european distributors and has made a significant contribution to the development and promotion of european films. new initiatives need to be developed under the media programme to improve and promote translation, dubbing, the use of common or large subtitles, and also training programmes to enable audiovisual and film industry representatives to adapt their knowledge to digital technologies.
in writing. i very much appreciate almost all the contributions to the debate on the issue of the coexistence of roma with the majority population in the member states. unlike previous debates on this subject, i welcomed the openness with which some colleagues have spoken about things that were previously taboo. finding a solution for the 10 million roma minority in the eu will not be easy. given the different attitudes towards roma in different countries, and also on the basis of historical experience, it will be very difficult to find a uniform strategy applicable in all member states.
the rapporteur. - madam president, the aim of the legislation we voted on in part on tuesday, when we approved three of the four proposed legislative standards, is to ensure the continuation of the nuclear research programme for 2012 and 2013. the 7th framework programme for science and research covers a seven-year period, the euratom framework programme a five-year period, so it was necessary to secure funding for two years. the euratom framework programme covers research activities in the fields of nuclear energy, nuclear fusion and fission, radiation protection and the development of expertise in these fields. it is part of a legislative package containing draft decisions on the framework programme itself, on the two specific programmes for direct and indirect actions and on the rules for participation in the programme. as a reporter, i've had the opportunity to work with other shadow reporters, and i thank them very much for that cooperation. i would also like to thank the other institutions with which i have worked, including the joint research centre, which has provided me with expertise and valuable information throughout. all the time i've been working on the news, i've been emphasizing that this legislation is about research, not the use of nuclear energy. there are different views on the use of nuclear energy for electricity generation and in the energy mix, which we all have to respect. the member states have ultimate competence in this area. the field of nuclear research is different. there is a need to collect scientific knowledge and fund further research simply because these phenomena are part of the reality of the world. if we do not investigate them, our competitors from other continents will, and this will have a negative impact on our competitiveness and lead to our falling behind. in my report, i tried to reflect on the world after the fukushima tragedy. i tried to highlight the issues in favor of countries that have decided to phase out nuclear power. i stressed the importance of research into nuclear dismantling, safety improvements and the safe management of nuclear waste. in this context, i would like to emphasize one thing. since the beginning of the negotiations on these four reports, our green colleagues have tabled a large number of amendments aimed at eliminating the budgetary items of this programme and limiting it as much as possible. the vast majority of these proposals were rejected in the committee vote, and unfortunately yesterday's vote on the direct actions of the joint research centre approved amendment no 9, which partially limits the commission's original text on actinide research. this research is fundamental and could lead to a nuclear waste treatment that would significantly reduce the amount of longer-lived isotopes, thus making a significant contribution to environmental protection. this research is also important for the development of new medical applications for the benefit of all europeans. ladies and gentlemen, european nuclear research and training is essential to enhance competitiveness, economic growth and the achievement of european scientific excellence. i therefore urge you to support this programme.
for the ecr group. madam president, first of all i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr březina, for the material he has presented and for his work. in an age when access to nuclear technology is often accompanied by an exaggerated, even hysterical, atmosphere caused by the events at fukushima, it is difficult to have a rational discussion. fortunately, that's not the case here in the courtroom. the argument put forward in the report that it is important to pay more attention to nuclear research and development of new technologies, particularly after the events in japan, is correct. it is new approaches and technological practices that are not only improving the operational safety of nuclear installations, but also advancing knowledge for other fields. nuclear facilities are not just for power plants, but they are used in many other fields, they are indispensable in health care or in materials research, genetics and other fields. i therefore fully support the view that nuclear research is needed after fukushima, rather than simply condemning these technologies. as a shadow rapporteur, i am pleased that the text also mentions support for the technology of fourth generation fast neutron reactors. these reactors have higher safety, while having higher efficiency of utilization of uranium fuel while producing lower levels of radioactive waste. one of the largest research programmes in which the european union is involved is the iter project. nuclear fusion is perhaps the best hope for solving the world's growing energy needs. this project, with its enormous financial demands, requires very good coordination and financial control. supervision of this project is essential to ensure that it does not become mere research without a resulting effect. as the ecr shadow rapporteur, i fully support and recommend the report by mr březina.

madam president, allow me to begin by applauding the report by my colleague mr březina. the extension of the 7th framework programme for nuclear energy is a logical step. this is to ensure compatibility with the existing financial perspective until 2013. we all know that the compromise proposal, which i personally support and which contains many improvements in the field of nuclear safety, was drawn up in the council with great difficulty and delay. i appreciate the many improvements in simplifying the programme, including the reduction of administrative burdens. key comments, mainly on security aspects, were made by austria and luxembourg, which had long blocked the agreement and had left negotiations at a standstill. even the compromise proposal of the hungarian presidency was considered by these states at the time to be insufficiently responsive to their requirements. however, it is now important that the legislative proposal is adopted as soon as possible, but no later than the end of 2011, so that the programme can continue from 2012. the european parliament should therefore not be too complicated to step on the spot, to bring politics into expert issues, and should approve the text without emotion.
the reporter. - madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, let me thank you once again for your contribution to the debate. whatever you mentioned about any of the elements of this very broad field of research, it was in a constructive way. whether it was the integration of nuclear research carried out by the esnii industrial initiative or the european strategic energy technology plan set into strategy 2020. whether you have stressed the urgency of time to address budgetary issues in relation to both the jet project and the iter programme. whether you emphasized education and the need to train engineers or the advantage of fourth-generation nuclear reactors, it was all in a positive direction. thank you very much for these comments, but let me make one general comment. this debate, which should not have taken place because this report, or these four reports, were originally to have been discussed without debate, was initiated by the green group. i have been dealing with the amendments of this group all this time and i was not bothered because i respect the different political views of my colleagues. but to have someone call a debate and then not one member of this group participates in the debate, sounds to me like a certain disregard for democratic principles. i felt an inner urge to say this.
the eu-georgia association agreement is a highly contradictory document. the european union has agreed that the main thing is to change the legislation and to give verbal approval to any demands of the eu negotiators. the rest will come. the important thing is to criticize russia, not to recognize the independence of south ossetia and abkhazia and to declare that georgia is a european state. i consider that to be one of the many unnecessary claims in this report. the document does not mention the issue of the abolished third autonomous entity (along with south ossetia and abkhazia). her liquidation apparently escaped the negotiators. it would be interesting to see how the exemplary application of democratic principles required by the text for the so-called occupied territories is progressing in this case. the individual chapters illustrate the range of problems that need to be addressed in stages. it is practically all areas of law, justice and security. similarly, in the chapter on economic and sectoral cooperation, it is clear that the country is only at the beginning of a difficult journey and is struggling to meet the basic requirements of international standards. the road to closer relations with the european union is clearly still very long and winding. the citizenship manipulation we're hearing about is definitely not a good sign on this journey. the gue/ngl group and i personally are not opposed to the agreement, however.
in writing. overfishing poses a serious threat to biodiversity and the sustainable marine environment. this situation is exacerbated by illegal fishing, which further exacerbates the social and economic impacts of the loss of fish stocks. last but not least, potential food security risks are increasing, given that fish and other seafood are the primary source of protein for more than a billion people on earth. the problem with illegal fishing is not that there are no means of monitoring or securing evidence in case of infringement. the problem is that everyone is always making excuses, and so far half a million tonnes of illegally caught fish worth over €1 billion are being imported into the eu every year. the lack of political will is hurting both european fishermen and fish stocks. so, the origin tests using the world's dna database today cost about eight euros per sample, and you don't have to do a lot of them, you just have to do them randomly. and if we can pay someone to use satellites to photograph or listen to anyone on the planet, we can sometimes use this technology for something sensible. here, too, the cost is not so staggering compared with the losses caused by illegal fishing. it is therefore necessary to review the adequacy of control mechanisms and their application in practice.
in writing. i abstained on the report on the further development of an integrated maritime policy. as a member of the european parliament from the inland state, i was led to do so for the following reason. the integrated maritime policy has so far been paid for by funds enabling the financing of pilot and preparatory projects and supported by the cohesion fund, the european regional development fund, the european fisheries fund, the 7th research framework programme or the european neighbourhood and partnership instrument and other related programmes. the new activities of the maritime states should be financed from a specific envelope, with the commission proposing to allocate eur 50 million for the years 2011 to 2013. while i support the commission's efforts to coordinate maritime-related activities of the maritime states, in view of the need for efficient spending, i do not consider it appropriate to create additional budgetary commitments for this area. but as a resident of the inland state, i have no direct interest in this area, so i decided to stay.
in writing. i call on the commission to ensure the consistent application and implementation of the existing eu regulatory framework in the field of telecommunications and to assess, within six months of the publication of the findings of the berec investigation, whether further regulatory action is needed to ensure freedom of expression, free access to information, free choice for consumers and media pluralism, as well as to achieve effective competition and innovation and to deliver broad benefits for citizens, businesses and public authorities using the internet. the commission should ensure that internet service providers do not block, discriminate against, restrict or reduce the ability of any person to use the services to view, use, send, publish, receive or offer any content, application or service of his or her choice, regardless of the source or destination. the commission must provide the ep with information on current practices in the area of internet traffic management, the connectivity market and network congestion, as well as on any possible link to underinvestment and the issue of 'device neutrality' to undertake further analysis.
in writing. − i support the opening of negotiations on a deep and comprehensive free trade area as soon as possible once georgia has implemented the key recommendations put forward by the commission and endorsed by the eu member states in order to allow georgia to be more closely integrated with its largest trading partner, which is necessary to sustain georgia's economic growth and overcome the economic crisis and the damage caused by the 2008 war with russia. it should be noted that the georgian government has made significant efforts in recent years to open up the country's economy to the outside world by introducing very low industrial tariffs, adopting a legal and administrative framework facilitating trade and investment, and promoting the rule of law. i would highlight the progress georgia has made in improving legislation, increasing the efficiency of institutions and ensuring strict quality control standards for georgian products in order to meet the requirements set by the european commission. the eu should provide financial and technical support to georgia to ensure the continuation of the legislative and institutional reforms necessary to adapt to a deep and comprehensive free trade area and to accelerate the process of putting into practice the key recommendations set out in the eu-georgia action plan. it is important that georgia ensures proper disposal of toxic and radioactive waste on its territory as a necessary precondition for trade facilitation, in particular with regard to agriculture, in order to ensure food safety.
in writing. on 17 november, the european parliament's plenary adopted a report on mainstreaming gender equality in the work of the european parliament. the main objective of gender mainstreaming is to change the nature and institutions of traditional politics to take more account of the needs, expectations and experiences of the whole of society, both women and men. parliament hereby undertakes to adopt and implement on a regular basis a gender mainstreaming policy plan in the european parliament, the overall objective of which is to promote equality between women and men through a genuine and effective gender perspective in all policies and activities, in order to assess the different impacts of measures on women and men, coordinate existing initiatives and identify objectives and priorities as well as the means to achieve them. i personally consider it a very important commitment to integrate gender analysis into all stages of the budgetary process to ensure that the needs and priorities of women and men are taken into account equally and to assess the impact of the allocation of eu resources to women and men. i consider this approach to budgeting to be crucial in the context of the fight for gender equality, which is why i supported the report.

for the group gue/ngl mr. chairman, the case of blogger alaa abd al-fattah is just a small symbolic example of the helplessness of our politics. the celebrations of the arab spring, when, with the help of the european union states, unknown people with unknown aims and interests come to power, end in the arrest of people who are guilty of one thing, that is, of their own opinion. we must learn once and for all that we cannot support people for whom the slogans of human rights and democracy are just a spanish wall behind which they hide the desire for power and the defence of their own privileges. most of the problems of north africa and the middle east are long-term and have social roots, often intrinsically linked to european colonialism. successful, and mostly warlike, solutions to such problems only lead to new problems, often worse. but, as we saw before the invasion of iraq, instead of seeking compromises, ways of understanding and change, we are seeing a policy of confrontation which makes any manoeuvre impossible. i must emphasize again that it is easier to make war than to make peace. easier and cheaper. do we really need to have millions of iraqi refugees or a bombed-out sirte behind our policies? the task of europe is to be a cultured and unambiguous promotion of human rights, which does not apply a double-track policy. i agree with the turkish calls for an expedited solution to the situation in syria. but i don't understand why it's possible to use violence to suppress kurdish rebels and not to use violence to suppress syrian rebels. the fate of alaa abd al-fattaah should remind us that human rights are indivisible.

on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, this item on the agenda is called ‘preparations for the european council meeting’, but sometimes i do not know to which european council we are referring. at the moment, i have the feeling that we have at least two councils: one council is the formal one which is represented by 27 member states and takes place occasionally according to an agreed schedule. the other one has only two members who are mr sarkozy and mrs merkel, and it seems to be in session permanently. this is the one which really decides. that, by the way, is a good example and good evidence that when hard times arrive the whole so-called community method turns into a big illusion and it is the powerful ones who decide. please do not get me wrong, this is not sarcasm. i have a certain understanding for mrs merkel because – there are many quotations in many languages and i will use the english one – ‘he who pays the piper, calls the tune’. and germany pays. mr schulz is right, germany pays. but it also wants some pay-back. that pay-back is the ultimate control over fiscal policy of all the eurozone members. if all of you in the eurozone want to keep the same currency you must be prepared for that; you must be prepared to give up your fiscal sovereignty. perhaps you can even save some public money by dissolving your national finance ministries, because you will not need them anymore. your national budgets will be drawn up somewhere else. anyway, this is your responsibility in the eurozone. i can only wish you good luck. however, i cannot agree with one thing. i cannot agree with this fiscal protectorate being spread over all 27 member states, as mr schäuble proposed only very recently. that is something which is unacceptable because one thing is clear from now on: today, there are two european unions within one. we definitely have a two-tier europe: we have the eurozone and the non-eurozone. this is not disintegration. that does not mean an end to the european union. this is just a reality that should be not only admitted but should be also reflected in our actions. therefore both parts of the european union must remain equal. eurozone members should not be superior to non-eurozone members and the eurozone cannot just automatically drag the others with them. any further arrangement must recognise this reality. it does not matter whether this is a treaty change or some other ways and means, such as an enhanced stability pact. if the eurozone states want to move forward in one direction, they must allow the others to move in their own direction as well. this is no longer a one-way street. this is reality. no longer do we go at the same speed and in the same direction. to use this crisis only as a platform in order to pursue further the project of political union and to further centralise power is short-sighted. it is wrong and in the end it will tear the eu apart. it is also wrong to accuse others of wanting to destroy the euro. no one wants to destroy the euro here, definitely not my group, because our countries are very much dependent on the stability of the eurozone. we are not opposing the very existence of the euro; what we are opposing are wrong measures which will not save the euro. the eu must either be flexible or multispeed, whatever you wish to call it, or unfortunately very soon there will be no eu – with or without the euro.

in writing. i welcome the recommendation of the committee on foreign affairs that the european parliament give its consent to the accession of the republic of croatia to the european union and to the council decision accepting the application for accession. if the european parliament approves the recommendations, the accession treaty will be signed at the european council on 9 december this year and we will welcome croatia as the 28th member of the eu on 1 july 2013. i trust that the eu member states will complete the ratification process in time, even in these turbulent times. the accession of the republic of croatia to the eu is a very positive signal at a time of growing anti-european tendencies. it is also a signal to all of us that it is possible to work together for the benefit of citizens and that life goes on regardless of the blackmail or intimidation by all those anonymous circles who are so annoyed by a united europe and the european union. i see the signing of the accession treaty as a proof of the credibility and quality of the european enlargement policy. in view of the traditionally good relations between the czech republic and croatia, i am particularly pleased that the accession comes at a time when the czech commissioner, mr štefan fülle, is responsible for enlargement and neighbourhood policy, and i warmly congratulate him on this.
in writing. the report addresses, among other issues, budgetary discipline not only within the euro area but also within all eu member states. as one aspect of the introduction of a common economic and budgetary policy for the euro area countries, the report states that the experience of spillovers of budgetary problems from one country to another, affecting all euro area members, clearly demonstrates the need for greater coordination and cooperation in the field of economic and budgetary policies. personally, i consider it a good call for member states to involve and regularly consult national and regional parliaments, social partners and civil society in the preparation of national reform and development and cohesion programmes. it shows that, if integrated surveillance of economic policies is to be effective, it must not be limited to the evaluation of the budgetary and structural policies of the eu member states, but must be consistent with objectives and actions at eu level, as well as with the size and nature of the union's financial resources. eu policies and actions under the eu 2020 strategy are therefore crucial in this respect, in particular as regards cohesion, research and innovation policies.
