in writing. the single market forum, hosted by krakow, poland, this year was a landmark event. it has enabled, for the first time, a truly comprehensive debate on the single market. i am pleased that, in addition to the regular participants in such meetings, the forum has also included the general public and that european citizens have had a unique opportunity to express their expectations or existing obstacles to the functioning of the single market. i believe that the forum was the first in a long series of similarly thematically focused meetings, and that others will follow in the coming years. i consider it crucial to strengthen the debate with the general public in view of the development of the single market. i therefore welcome the fact that the resolution adopted calls on both the commission and the member states to involve citizens more closely in the development of the single market. the call for public consultations to be available in all the eu's official languages and to be more understandable is certainly appropriate. statistics clearly show how much potential is being underutilized. i hope that this call will finally be heard and taken into account by the commission. let us enable citizens to participate more actively at european level. this can only help the successful functioning of the single market, which we all want.
mr president, the debate today, and not only today, is about whether the single currency will be saved by an amendment to the treaty of lisbon, closer fiscal coordination, the dependence or independence of the european central bank, the european minister for finance or the european monetary fund. i think we need to ask a more general question, and i think a much more serious one. is this the euro crisis or is this the debt crisis? in this context, it is necessary to ask whether these are debts that are the cause of the current situation or are a consequence of the current situation, and whether they are debts that have arisen over the last few years or are the consequence of developments in europe over the last long decade. in this case, i do not think that the current difficulties of the euro need to be seen as a euro crisis, because these difficulties have only revealed deep structural problems in part of the european area. the call for fiscal responsibility in the member states is therefore right. however, in such a case, we are faced with the question of whether human behaviour, accustomed to the very high demands of the european welfare state or the european welfare state, will change. if we don't have the courage to make significant reforms, even transform the welfare state, and get rid of the heavy budgetary costs, then we're going to have a very hard time getting out of the current difficulties.
mr president, it was not easy to reach agreement on the draft budget this year, and we know that the compromise reached was adopted by a fragile majority in the council. the total expenditure of just over €129 billion, an increase of 1.86%, reflects in my view the consolidation efforts of the member states at national level and the reality of the economic situation in which the european continent finds itself today. simply put, european spending has managed to keep itself in check and below the expected inflation threshold in times of crisis. for me personally, as coordinator of our group in the committee on regional development, the main priority when deciding on the 2012 budget was, is and will continue to be to ensure that there is enough money for cohesion, i.e. for heading 1b, to cover all the commitments of previous years. it is therefore essential for us that the budget agreement includes a strong common commitment to payments under point 3.1 to ensure sufficient funding for the cohesion policy area if necessary.
in writing. − the eu2020 strategy to which the member states have committed themselves must not remain on paper. it therefore needs to be adequately funded, in particular its key pillars, i.e. lifelong learning, research and innovation. in the light of the recent political changes in the mediterranean, in the context of the so-called arab spring, it is clear that a strong neighbourhood policy is also essential for european stability and prosperity. the report by mr böge proposes that the funding gaps in these key policies, which the council is forced to fill despite previous commitments, be filled by means of the flexibility instrument. the commission and the ep consider this to be the best way forward, and i would like to support it.
in writing. − the use of repayable instruments, as provided for in the 2007-13 general regulation governing the structural funds, has been subject to some degree of legal uncertainty regarding their use. in order to regularise this anomalous situation, and in light of the intervention of the court of auditors, the commission believed it necessary to introduce a general definition of repayable assistance. i supported proposed changes which promote the usage of innovative financial instruments, which could support revenue generating projects, alongside more traditional sources of funding, such as non-reimbursable grants. i am satisfied that the proposal provides clarity about the legality of existing legal practices in our member states. the parliament’s report also reduces the administrative burden on the part of the member states by limiting some of the proposed additional reporting obligations by the commission to those required by the court of auditors.
in writing. − this proposal aims to harness cohesion policy in order to provide additional financial resources to member states such as greece and portugal, which face huge difficulties in managing their debts. this proposal allows for additional financial assistance through cohesion policy by allowing for a temporary "top-up" increase of present co-financing rates by up to 10%. while cohesion policy can be used as a tool to assist those member states who are struggling to finance quality public investments, it is questionable whether this proposal is an adequate reaction to the economic and fiscal problems in the member states concerned. in many ways, it is just as important to pay attention to the quality of the supported projects and their potential contribution to economic growth, rather than merely seeking to maximise absorption rates of structural and cohesion funding in the member states concerned. moreover, the proposed arrangement can appear unfair, in that operational programmes in member states which managed their public finances more carefully will be subject to less favourable co-financing conditions than those member states such as greece, thereby creating a perverse reward for financial irresponsibility. given these factors i was unable to support this proposed measure and opted to abstain.
in writing. − article 349 of the treaty authorises the member states of france, spain and portugal to provide for a difference between the taxation of local products and the taxation of products from other member states. this was introduced on the basis of the perceived permanent handicaps that affect the economic and social conditions of these "outermost regions", as they are defined in the treaty. this report accepts the commission's proposal to extend the application of exemptions from and reductions in a tax known as aiem for certain products produced locally in the canary islands. in line with consistent ecr policy on such article 349 measures, i have refused to support this report in committee, on the basis that the treaty provision grants an unfair advantage to the three member states concerned, even though all member states have their own peripheral regions and yet cannot benefit from such exemptions from harmonising community provisions on vat.
in writing. − article 349 of the treaty authorises the member states of france, spain and portugal to provide for a difference between the taxation of local products and the taxation of products from other member states. this was introduced on the basis of the perceived permanent handicaps that affect the economic and social conditions of these ‘outermost regions’, as they are defined in the treaty. this latest article 349 measure concerns rum produced in french overseas departments and sold on the french mainland with a reduced rate of excise duty. the reduction is limited to an annual quota of 108 000 hectolitres of pure alcohol. the commission’s proposal is extend this special tax exemption for two additional years until december 2013, and to increase the annual quota concerned to 125 000 hl. i have voted against such article 349 measures, on the basis that the treaty provision grants an unfair advantage to the three member states concerned, even though all member states have their own peripheral regions and yet cannot benefit from such exemptions from harmonising community provisions on vat.
in writing. the proportion of pupils who leave school early is already very low in the czech republic. the czech republic, alongside slovakia, poland and slovenia, has long been one of the four member states with the lowest rate of early school leaving and is also relatively well below the eu target for 2020 of 5.6% (the eu average was 14.9% in 2008). i was against this report when it was being discussed because i do not think that we will solve the situation by developing free public nurseries and day care facilities or by providing extracurricular activities and clubs by the member states as proposed in the report. at the same time, i am wary of the effect of the proposed social mapping of early school leavers by centralising and collecting data from all member states here in brussels. rather than the activist measures that the report calls for, i think that we should focus on analysing the specific causes of early school leaving and responding immediately to the deterioration of the situation, i.e. increasing the percentage of early school leaving.
- the regulation on statistical returns in respect of the carriage of goods by road provides for the obligation of the member states to send statistical data on road freight transport to eurostat. i have no objections to this regulation, although i express reservations about the delegation of power to the european commission to adopt implementing measures by means of delegated acts. i also consider it important to consult proposals at expert level when preparing delegated acts, with the commission ensuring that the administrative burden on member states and reporting agents is not significantly increased.
in writing. i recommend a high degree of caution before calling for deeper integration in the customs area. i am therefore very pleased that the amendment to create a new european customs agency has been rejected. in the area of customs modernisation, it is important that all member states communicate with each other and apply a unified approach, rather than deeper integration, transfer of powers and the creation of other transnational institutions, as is often suggested by some. in order to increase the competitiveness of businesses, it is important to focus on simplifying customs procedures and standardising customs thresholds, as well as on applying the 'one stop shop' principle in customs procedures. we should pay particular attention to the protection of intellectual property rights and its link to customs procedures.
- simplification of the customs system in the form indicated can help all the parties involved to some extent in a number of areas. it should be economically beneficial, facilitative, efficient. the promotion and facilitation of trading conditions is one, but a very important part. another, equally important part is to increase the efficiency of customs procedures themselves and to improve the effectiveness of the fight against illicit trade. i believe that this will enable the competent customs authorities to fulfil their obligations under the law better and in many cases will facilitate their work.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, in some cases we are taking decisions which may have consequences for the citizens of the union for decades to come. often our attitude will affect the lives of our children, while we may not even be able to verify whether we voted for a meaningful project. and iter is one of them. the prospect of nuclear fusion succeeding, however, is very appealing, and frankly, we don't have many options for how to deal with a situation where we run out of energy resources. that is why i am convinced of the need to support the iter project. yes, it's expensive, yes, the outcome is not 100% clear, but there is a chance. without bold decisions, humanity would not advance and move forward. to fund such ambitious programmes as the nuclear fusion reactor, we should. i am glad that all the problems are finally being solved, and i hope that they will be despite the alleged recent obstruction by some member states. and so europe will not be an untrustworthy partner for partners such as the united states, russia, japan, india and others. i don't know if it's entirely accurate to say that the current cost of iter represents only 3% of the us military budget last year. but it just goes to show that the money that's been spent so senselessly, as some people try to say, is not for us.
for the ecr group. madam president, first of all i would like to thank the rapporteurs and the other colleagues who have contributed to the drafting of this extremely important directive. the protection of victims must not be limited by national borders, as victims often move to escape violence. in our common area without internal borders, it is therefore essential that protection measures can also be applied in another member state without having to initiate new legal proceedings after relocation in order to re-establish the protection measure. i fully support the aim of this directive and welcome the fact that the co-decision process has managed to make some fundamental changes and that the group of european conservatives and reformists can now support this final version. the amendments i have in mind concern mainly the scope of the european protection order and also the legal basis for issuing the european protection order. i welcome the fact that the current wording provides for a sufficient degree of flexibility in cooperation between member states in providing protection for victims of all crimes. i am very pleased that this directive allows us to provide protection for victims of all crimes without imposing an obligation to change the national systems of the member states and that it takes account of the different legal traditions of the member states. it would be a great pity if this important issue, and i am referring to the protection of all victims of crime throughout europe, were to be called into question by further unnecessary harmonisation within the european union.

blue-card answer. – mr president, i am very much afraid that it seems that mr leichtfried, as a member of the socialist group, seems to misunderstand a little bit the role of the financial sector in the european economy. this is not only a question of the city; this is a question of the entire prosperity of the entire european union because i can assure you, mr leichtfried, that our economies are mutually intertwined. if one economy suffers, the others will suffer as well.
madam president, i voted in favour of the recommendation on the future fishing arrangements under the agreement of the general fisheries commission for the mediterranean. the mediterranean is the most endangered aquatic ecosystem in europe. many species of fish, coral, and other animals have been fished so massively that some species have been driven to extinction. a whole host of others are highly endangered. the problem is that within a species, individuals with similar dna make-up survive, making the entire local ecosystem more vulnerable. the slightest change in the food chain, pollution, or climate can lead to the extinction of other species. the work of this committee is therefore essential to protect biodiversity, reduce the amount of catches that can be taken and increase the number of reserves where fishing is prohibited. with its approach, humanity has placed fishing among the most endangered of all activities, and now humanity must help it regenerate. fishermen may have less work today, but they must have work in the future. a total withdrawal would result in the permanent loss of the fishery as an industry. it is our duty to preserve the biodiversity of species and fisheries for future generations.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the adoption of a directive on eu citizens and their rights when they become parties to criminal proceedings. in such cases, whether in the person's home state or in another member state, a fair trial must be ensured in accordance with the case law of the european court of human rights. this means that fundamental procedural rights such as the right to consult a lawyer or, if necessary, to have documents interpreted or translated, must be respected. i consider it useful that this report amends the commission proposal to ensure that the directive will apply to all forms of restriction of personal liberty, regardless of how the member states classify the procedure under which the suspect has been deprived of his liberty. no one can know in advance how long a suspect will be held in police custody or how long he will be deprived of his liberty. although the various rights listed in the bill of rights may not always be relevant, a suspect or accused person is nevertheless entitled to receive written information on his or her procedural rights without delay.
in writing. - some member states are facing budgetary constraints as a result of the financial and budgetary crisis, which may prevent them from meeting their commitments under the community's co-financed programmes, in particular the structural funds. i therefore understand that, on the basis of these facts, the commission is proposing to temporarily increase the maximum share of union co-financing for programmes under way in member states benefiting from the european financial stability mechanism currently greece, ireland and portugal and from the balance of payments facility for non-euro area member states currently latvia and romania. this temporary increase in community co-financing, which would be granted at the request of the member state concerned, would reduce the corresponding amount of funds needed in the financial years 2011 and 2012. this should contribute to the overall economic recovery in those member states. it is important to me that the commission proposal does not provide for any additional commitment appropriations. the payment of the additional appropriations needed to finance the increased eu support will be carried over from the financial year 2013 to the financial years 2011 and 2012. there will be no transfer of financial allocations between member states, and that is why i was able to support this proposal.
draftsman of the committee on industry, research and energy. - mr president, i am here as rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on industry, research and energy on this subject. there is a strong likelihood that the financial crisis and the associated budget cuts will lead to programme cuts with a negative impact on the defence industry. the absence of a common policy in this area may further exacerbate the negative impact on the defence industry. while a common arms policy makes economic sense, it can also threaten the sovereignty of individual member states. for these reasons, european defence policy should lead to greater multilateral cooperation, but also take into account the national specificities of the defence industry. more specifically, to mitigate the impact of the financial crisis on the defence industry, it is necessary to consider greater demand consolidation, for example in the areas of joint scientific research projects or pooling of demand for shared capacities. exports help some firms to mitigate the effects of the financial crisis and some are not internationally competitive. these firms should continue to diversify their portfolios, for example into civil security.
in writing. the european parliament's contribution to the european neighbourhood policy (enp) evaluation process is certainly needed, among other things, to illustrate our continued interest in this topic. and perhaps our recommendations will also help us to avoid being caught off guard by events as we were earlier this year by the arab spring. the evaluation could also, in its conclusions, prevent attempts to 'buy democracy' for belarus for usd 9 billion or prevent a repetition of the undignified level of representation of member countries at enp events, such as this year's eastern partnership summit in warsaw. this is not what the qualified polish presidency deserved. my reservation also applies to the current government of the czech republic. as regards the southern dimension of the enp, it is essential that the diversity of the civilisation, culture, religion and historical traditions of the peoples of this region be strongly respected in the implementation of the ep recommendations. this is practically the conditio sine qua non for the success of the enp towards the mediterranean. we also need to explain to our constituents why political islam doesn't matter anymore. for example, the suppression of the muslim brotherhood movement in egypt was seen as a war on terrorism for the last ten years. at the same time, it's up to us to convince our voters that their money for the southern dimension will not end the arab winter. otherwise, they'll be told about us by geert wilders, marine le pen or pia kjersgaard.
in writing. neighborhood policy is one of the most important policies. it should be stressed that the resolution on the issue already contains a somewhat vague formulation in recital b: 'eu overall policy in the epc should be based on values'. this vague wording does not indicate how individual eu countries will pay off debts that have not been paid by the gaddafi regime, nor does it indicate whether this is the way to go about silent support for the various islamist groups that have gained significant strength in the recent elections in the arab maghreb states. the question is what values the union as a whole and the individual countries promote. the creation of the euro-mediterranean erasmus and leonardo da vinci programmes is a clear positive step. but the resolution does not indicate whether the eu's policy towards its neighbours in the near future will be based on openness to these partners or on the search for ways to strengthen the external border, the 'schengen solidity'. it is very strange that the resolution does not criticize the dictatorial elements of the regime in georgia at all and seems to ignore the continuing delay in the declaration of a palestinian state. the parts concerning belarus go beyond the bounds of good taste by their interference in the internal affairs of a neighboring state, while a number of negative phenomena in turkey's foreign policy, such as the occupation of part of cyprus, are overlooked. for these reasons, the gue/ngl group cannot support this motion for a resolution.
mr president, it is certainly in the interests of consumers to be able to distinguish when buying drinks between genuine fruit juices and drinks made from concentrated nectar. in this respect, it is certainly a clarification of the directive and it is going in the right direction. however, i have followed with concern the proposals to introduce a new obligation to label nectars with the words 'sweetened' or 'added sugar' followed by a maximum amount of added sugar calculated as dry matter and expressed in grams per litre. such a change would be highly confusing for consumers and would clearly have a negative economic impact on producers. nectars would become the only category of soft drinks for which the amount of added sugar would have to be compulsorily indicated, which would put those products at a significant disadvantage compared with other drinks. so i'm glad that common sense prevailed and this bill didn't pass. the citizens of the czech republic will also be pleased to learn that despite the euro crisis, the european institutions have managed to resolve a long-standing dispute over whether tomatoes are a vegetable or a fruit. this european directive establishes once and for all that tomatoes are, like the quinoa, a fruit. i have my doubts, however, about amendment no 22, which also considers carrots to be fruit. i'm afraid i can't convince the czech gardener of that.
in writing. i am convinced that the polish presidency has been a success and its results contrast sharply with those of the czech and hungarian presidencies. the poles have coped very well with the very difficult situation that has arisen in the eu, and what i like most is that after years of the then leadership's collusion with the euro-monger klaus, they have turned into a strong pro-european power.
in writing. − fruit juices produced in the european union are high-quality products that contribute to a healthy and balanced diet and are produced according to sustainability standards that are supported by our legislation. it is therefore important to guarantee the high quality of all products sold on the european market. without calling into question any import policy, the report approved rightly gives priority to local products which do not have to be imported from great distances, which are not produced under social and working conditions that are contrary to community legislation and which comply with union food safety rules. but this is not about european protectionism, it is about the sustainability of all sectors of our economy. the general quality of products is assessed, inter alia, in terms of their ethical, environmental and social quality. while i agree with most of the requirements of the commission's draft revision, i have some doubts about the future function of this body, particularly with regard to delegated acts. i therefore agree to amend article 7a (1a) so that the period for delegating powers to the commission is changed from 'indefinite' to 'five years'. i would also add that, if after five years there are no substantial changes justifying a revision of the legislation, the ex post renewal mechanism should be used.
in writing. the report focuses on the legal environment for the labelling of fruit juices. this is mainly about defining terms such as fruit juice, fruit nectar, fruit drink and lemonade. the main difference between the two terms is the fruit juice content, which is 100% fruit, whereas fruit nectar has a fruit content of up to 25% and fruit drinks have a fruit content of less than 25%. the amendment also introduces a new obligation to use at least half the letters of the name of the product, the description if it is a fruit juice, nectar, drink or soda, on the packaging. this measure will help to better inform the consumer about the content of these products and to make it easier for him to recognise the quality of the product. likewise, in the future, it will not be possible to determine whether the product is 100% fruit or whether it contains any substitutes. i consider all the proposed measures to be beneficial to consumers. the older generation in particular will be able to better understand what the actual content of the product is, which i think is important in the coming year of active ageing.

in writing. − i welcome the joint communication from the commission and the high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy on a new approach to a changing neighbourhood. at the same time, i recognise the european aspirations and european choice of some partners and their commitment to building a strong and sustainable democracy, and in this context i stress the need to establish new and different relations between the eu and the eastern partnership countries and to support their efforts to consolidate sustainable democracy and market economies. i believe that countries should be given tangible and compelling incentives under the neighbourhood policy to engage in the common goal of building a strong democracy, and that differentiation based on political, economic and social realities, performance and results of individual countries should be based on clearly defined criteria and measurable benchmarks for individual partner countries, which should be monitored regularly. in this respect, i call on the commission and the european external action service to consider the benchmarks set out in the joint communication as objectives to be achieved.
in writing. − i welcome the commission communication entitled 'eu counter-terrorism policy: achievements and future challenges' and stress that this communication must be linked to the eu's internal security strategy in the making. however, i consider it wrong that its scope is rather narrow, limited to the application of agreed policy measures and does not focus on national counter-terrorism policies or national measures implementing policies agreed at european or international level into national law. i also regret that no more thorough investigation has been carried out into possible legal loopholes or overlaps or duplications of counter-terrorism measures and instruments adopted at eu level. unfortunately, the communication does not sufficiently cover and develop in more detail the actions of dgs other than dg jls (e.g. dg tran, dg enter or dg markt), nor does it provide a clear picture of how the actions interact, whether they overlap or whether there are gaps between them. this requires consideration of all the above levels, as european, national and international measures are complementary and an assessment of individual measures would not provide an overall picture of the impact of counter-terrorism policies in europe. the european commission also owes it to eu citizens to explain how certain eu counter-terrorism instruments, such as data retention, passenger name records and the swift agreement, are integrated into the counter-terrorism strategy.
in writing. the long-running process of increasing the openness of public administration has its roots in the signing of the aarhus convention in 1998. the report on the regulation seeks to minimise the number of documents to which the public is not entitled and to ensure maximum information and openness in decision-making processes. the principle underlying this regulation is the principle of active openness, i.e. the active publication of all documents drawn up by the eu institutions. the new regulation introduces an obligation for each general administrative department to appoint one official responsible for providing information upon request from citizens. the obligation to actively publish documents is also introduced, and a new common interface for linking the document registers of the various institutions of the european union is also introduced. this measure should allow for the interconnection of individual registers, thereby increasing citizens' access to documents. the new obligation to make all information about decision-making processes available at every stage of the decision-making process. in conclusion, i would like to stress that, in the spirit of the aarhus convention, all information must be provided free of charge, so that no further barriers to greater openness and awareness are created.
for the s&d group. mr president, the european commission's vision for 2050 is an important document to set the future direction of european transport policy. we therefore see it not only as a material for discussion but, above all, as a framework within which concrete objectives and actions will be set in the short and medium term. the report therefore calls on the commission to set these targets in concrete terms by 2020. it's important to start with the present. the european transport policy must clearly build on previous documents and also reflect the baseline state of infrastructure in all member states. in the final proposal, we therefore call on the commission to present an analysis of the current situation as soon as possible, and no later than 2013. european cohesion policy instruments must continue to support a faster convergence of infrastructure levels in the member states, with an emphasis on transport sustainability and safety. the resulting compromise also addresses a very important area, namely the conditions of transport workers. this sector employs about 10 million people, so it has to be an important part of our transportation thinking. whether it's the quality of jobs, job security, training or education. the traffic is not without people. their working conditions must therefore be appropriate to this demanding and responsible profession and have a direct impact on road safety. effective cooperation between different modes of transport is an important part of future transport policy and the eu's long-term emission reduction targets. i therefore consider it important that we agreed in the final compromise to promote sustainable transport modes, across all distances. i also agree with the commission that we should have a systematic approach to long-distance transport at a pan-european level. in conclusion, i would like to thank the rapporteur for his great efforts in negotiating acceptable compromises and i believe that the final proposal will be approved by a large majority tomorrow.
mr. president, removing barriers to the free movement of people, goods, services and capital is our long-term priority. i therefore support the european commission's efforts to create a single european transport area. however, a comprehensive and efficient interconnection of transport networks across the european union and the creation of a cohesive multimodal network are also important conditions. in this respect, i think it is right that the white paper makes several references to the differences in transport infrastructure between the eastern and western parts of the european union. on the other hand, there is no specific action or initiative, not only in this white paper but in the commission's activities in general, to eliminate these differences. in this respect, the commission's transport activities seem to me to be somewhat inconsistent. i expected the commission to come up with a solution to close the gaps in transport infrastructure between the east and west in the form of the proposed infrastructure fund. but i was very disappointed by the commissioner because he again concentrates most of the aid in the old member states, even though he takes 10 billion from cohesion policy, which is primarily for the weaker regions. this is not only a great disappointment, but a fatal flaw in european integration.
in writing. the current crisis has clearly shown that rational and good solutions for citizens cannot be achieved by market forces alone. i welcome the results of the intelligent energy for europe (iee) programme, which is not just about concrete successful projects and sharing their results. it is about building a wider awareness of the need for rational behaviour in european civilization. the promotion of research and innovation in investment in sustainable energy projects has, despite some minor flaws or problems, put the issue of sustainable energy itself on the agenda. without energy-conscious decision-making by public administrations and without public pressure to make such decisions, the objectives we are now discussing in the context of the energy efficiency directive cannot be considered at all. the mff does not contain any reference to the continuation of the iee programme. horizon 2020, the framework programme for research and innovation, as part of the 2014-2020 multiannual financial framework, must therefore take over the role of the iee and, in particular, ensure sufficient funding for this programme. without further supporting and facilitating energy-efficient decision-making, it will not be possible to ensure the internalisation of energy externalities in transport, nor to create a new impetus in the field of smart grids. without economic and innovative thinking, integrated energy projects cannot be launched on a large scale and european energy cannot become sustainable.
in writing. i voted against the proposal for a regulation (ec) no 1406/2002 establishing the european maritime safety agency (emsa). there are several reasons. first, the regulation has already been amended three times, the last amendment being in 2006. but i think we should change the legislation as little as possible. secondly, the proposed amendment to the regulation aims to extend the tasks of emsa. however, i consider the proposed scope of the agency's tasks to be too broad and i do not see a clear definition of the powers in the draft regulation. third, i'm worried about budgeting. i believe that the additional tasks of emsa should be addressed by redeploying existing staff of the agency rather than by recruiting new staff.
in writing. − at a time when europe is on the verge of another economic crisis, i consider it a huge risk to set strict targets requiring a 20% reduction in co2 emissions from road transport, as well as a 30% reduction in emissions from aviation and shipping. such requirements can be a deathblow for the european economy and its global competitiveness in these uncertain times. the transport sector is a major contributor to europe's competitiveness and growth, and should therefore be supported, not hindered. i therefore consider it important to complete the european internal transport market by opening up other transport networks and markets. i therefore support the proposal to stabilise the financing of the construction of the trans-european transport network and i particularly welcome the proposal to include the eurovelo long-distance cycle network in the trans-european transport network. i generally regard the report as a good one, but unfortunately i am sorry for the trojan horse that has been smuggled into it in the form of unrealistic environmental targets to reduce co2 emissions from transport, the implementation of which will reduce europe's competitiveness. as i do not think it would be good if a number of the positive steps proposed in the report were not implemented because of the trojan horse mentioned earlier, i have only given my conditional support to the report. i therefore give notice in advance that i will not support any legislative measure arising from this report which implements an unrealistic plan to reduce co2 emissions.
in writing. i voted against this report because i consider the quantitative targets set, for example in the area of emission reductions, to be too ambitious, without any concrete steps being outlined to achieve them. moreover, i am convinced that, while reducing emissions in transport is important, it should not be the absolute priority of the new transport policy, but only one of the main objectives, alongside improving mobility and overall eu transport connectivity, which will contribute to increasing the eu's competitiveness. it should also be noted that most of the targets are highly dependent on the development and implementation of modern technologies, a process which is very difficult to predict. for these and other reasons, i voted against.
